## Session 176 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `setErrorFromCaught` migration in `src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx` (close session 159 layout-shared carryover)

**Random pick:** `$((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1 (List 1: Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs).

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **1 stale `setX(messageFromError(...))` site in the layout-shared Sidebar migrated to `setErrorFromCaught` + source-pattern test extended with a Sidebar describe block (5 new assertions). All 2096 jest tests pass + tsc + eslint + build all green.**

### What shipped

| # | File | Change |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx` | 1 import swap (`messageFromError` → `setErrorFromCaught`) + 1 catch-block migration at line 313 in the `runDeployAction` callback (shared by `handleUpdate`/`handleRestart`/`doRebuild`). The pre-migration site was `setMessage(messageFromError(err, fallbackForDeployMessage(startedMessage)))`; migrated to `setErrorFromCaught(setMessage, err, fallbackForDeployMessage(startedMessage))`. The other 5+ `setMessage("...")` literal sites (success labels, phase labels, timeouts) are unchanged. |
| 2 | `tests/unit/set-error-from-caught-source-pattern-list1.test.ts` | Extended with a second `describe()` block ("List 1.5 Sidebar (layout-shared)") that pins the post-migration shape on the Sidebar: (a) zero `setX(messageFromError(...))` sites via generalised regex, (b) positive regex on the import + canonical call shape `setErrorFromCaught(setMessage, err, fallbackForDeployMessage(`, (c) negative regex on the now-unused `messageFromError` import. The pre-existing 5 logs-page assertions are unchanged. JSDoc history block updated with the session 176 entry. |
| 3 | `pr-body.txt` | Prepended the new session 176 entry. |
| 4 | `pr-body-headline.md` | Updated headline to add session 176 + bumped the recent-sessions block. |
| 5 | `references/session-176-list1-sidebar-seterrorfromcaught.md` (new) | The session writeup under the `refactor-sweep-mission` skill. |

### Why this is byte-equivalent

`setErrorFromCaught(setX, err, fallback)` is literally `setX(messageFromError(err, fallback))` per `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:223` (the helper body). The 4 unit tests in `set-error-from-caught.test.ts` lock this byte-equivalence claim for 6 distinct input shapes (Error, empty Error, string, null, undefined, TypeError). The catch-block's externally observable behaviour is unchanged for every input shape. The 4 surrounding lines (`setBusy(false)`, `busyRef.current = false`, `pollDeployStatusRef.current(action)` happy path) are unchanged. The Sidebar's other 5+ `setMessage("...")` literal sites (success labels, phase labels, timeouts) are unchanged. The `fallbackForDeployMessage(startedMessage)` value is unchanged.

### Pitfall 1 — "Layout-shared" components are a new List surface category

The session 159 closure of the `setX(messageFromError(err, "..."))` → `setErrorFromCaught(setX, err, "...")` migration was scoped to the 4 page-local List 1 files. The Sidebar (the layout-shared component that wraps every page in Control Hub) was missed. The pre-migration site `setMessage(messageFromError(err, fallbackForDeployMessage(startedMessage)))` was the only stale carryover in the Sidebar, but it was on the most-shared hot path in the app.

**The general lesson:** the 4-list taxonomy (Dashboard/Sessions/Memory/Logs = List 1, Cron/Missions/Chat = List 2, Models/Agents/Skills/Tools/Personalities = List 3, Models/HERMES.md/Environment/Settings = List 4) covers page-local code. Layout-shared components (Sidebar, AppPageShell, PageHeader, MobileHeader) are the **"List 1.5"** surface — not page-local but page-facing. They share all of List 1's invariants (catch-block patterns, envelope-typed reads, `setErrorFromCaught` setters) but are easy to miss in a per-page scan. **Any future "scan for X pattern across the codebase" recipe should explicitly include or exclude layout components.**

### Pitfall 2 — Pre-flight scanner false positives on success/phase/timeout labels

The Sidebar has 5+ `setMessage("...")` call sites that use string literals (success labels like "Update started…", phase labels from the deploy-state machine, timeouts like "Timed out — check ch-restart.log in Logs"). The pre-flight scan that finds "setX(messageFromError" call sites is intentionally NARROW (`setX(messageFromError`, the 2-hop form), not WIDE (`setX(`, the literal form). Widening the scan would create false positives on every literal setter call. The session 176 test pins the narrow scan explicitly:

```ts
const FORBIDDEN_INLINE_SETX_MESSAGFROMERROR = /set\w+\(\s*messageFromError\s*\(/g;
```

**The general lesson:** when auditing for a "2-hop vs 1-hop" pattern, the scanner regex should match the discriminator (the helper call name), not the outer function (the setter). A wide scanner will hit every literal setter call; a narrow scanner hits only the bad pattern.

### Pitfall 3 — `import { messageFromError } from "@/lib/api-fetch"` is now unused in the Sidebar

The post-migration Sidebar has zero `messageFromError` call sites. The `messageFromError` import was removed and replaced with `setErrorFromCaught`. A future "is the import still used?" linter rule would catch a re-introduction of the import without a call site, but the new source-pattern test's "the Sidebar no longer imports messageFromError" assertion pins this invariant in CI.

**The general lesson:** when migrating a call site that was the sole consumer of a helper import, the import must be swapped (or the migration is incomplete). The new test asserts the post-migration import line is present AND the old import line is absent — both halves of the swap are pinned.

### Audit finding — `setX(messageFromError(...))` 2-hop form is now fully closed in src/ user-land

With the Sidebar migration, every `useState<string | null>` setter in user-land that wraps a caught error goes through `setErrorFromCaught`. The only remaining `messageFromError` call sites in src/ are:
- `src/lib/api-fetch.ts` — the helper itself + its JSDoc example + 1 internal call (`safeApiCall` line 96)
- `src/lib/cron/hermes-sync.ts` — 1 call inside a `PushResult` builder (not a setter wrapper)
- `src/lib/memory-providers/holographic.ts` — 3 calls inside error-message builders (string concatenation, not setter wrappers)
- `src/hooks/useApiData.ts` — JSDoc reference only
- `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts` — 1 setter wrapper (`setCategoryError(messageFromError(...))` line 389) + 2 result-object wrappers
- `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts` — 1 JSDoc reference + 2 result-object wrappers
- `src/app/api/memory/hindsight/route.ts` — 3 calls inside `{ error: messageFromError(...) }` return-value builders (not setter wrappers)
- `src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx` — now ZERO calls (this session's migration)

The next carryover is `useMissionsPage.ts:389` (List 2 territory).

### Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`: clean
- `npx jest tests/unit/set-error-from-caught-source-pattern-list1.test.ts`: **10/10 pass** (5 pre-existing logs-page assertions + 5 new Sidebar assertions)
- `npx jest`: **280 suites / 2096 tests pass** (up from 280/2091 = +5 tests in the extended source-pattern file; no regressions)
- `npm run build`: clean

## Session 175 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — close session 174 carryover (4 dashboard helpers + safeApiCallData migration in logs)

**Random pick (session 174):** `date +%Y%m%d | awk '{print $1 % 4 + 1}'` = 1 (List 1: Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs).

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **Session 174 carryover resolved + 2 pre-existing source-pattern tests updated to pin the new shape. All 2091 jest tests pass + tsc + eslint + build all green.**

### What shipped

| # | File | Change |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `tests/unit/dashboard-helpers-session-174.test.ts` | Fixed the 2 `topNTemplates` tests that were failing in session 174. The session 174 reference's diagnosis (Jest state-leak / `jest.doMock` scope issue) was wrong; the real cause was that both tests used 4-input arrays, and the helper's no-op fast path returns `[...templates]` for any input of length `≤ 12` (the default `n`), so the sort never ran. New fixtures use 13-input arrays to force the sort path; assertions use plain-node-verified expected outputs. |
| 2 | `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts` | Updated the "≥ 8 inline `safeApiCallData` calls in dashboard" assertion. Post-session-174 the 8 batched-load calls live in `loadInitialDashboardData` (testable in isolation); the dashboard itself only has 3 inline calls (one per polling/mutation site). New assertion: `helperExecCalls.length === 8, dashboardExecCalls.length === 3`. The 2 sister assertions (import line, zero `safeApiCall<{ data?: ... }>` envelope sites, zero `.data?.data` double-unwraps) remain green. |
| 3 | `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1-logs.test.ts` | Flipped from pinning the pre-migration shape to pinning the post-migration shape. The old test asserted "≥ 1 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` call in logs page" and "≥ 1 `?.data?.X` indirection read" — the session 174 migration to `safeApiCallData<{ cleared?: number }>` deliberately removed both patterns. The rewritten test asserts "≥ 1 `safeApiCallData<{ ... }>` call" and "zero `?.data?.X` indirection reads". The 2 sister assertions (source file readable, .tsx file on disk) remain green. |
| 4 | `pr-body.txt` | Prepended the new session 175 entry. |
| 5 | `references/session-175-list1-dashboard-helpers-closeout.md` (new) | The session writeup under the `refactor-sweep-mission` skill. |

### Why this is byte-equivalent

The 3 test-file changes are all PURE test changes — no production code was touched in this session. The 2 `topNTemplates` test fixes update fixtures (more inputs) and expected outputs (verified with plain `node`); the 2 source-pattern test flips update assertions to match the post-migration shape. None of the 5 source files touched in session 174 changed in this session.

### Pitfall 1 — The session-174 diagnosis was wrong, and the test fixtures masked the sort path

The session 174 reference proposed 3 fixes (split the test file, `jest.unmock`, or `jest.requireActual`) for a supposed Jest state-leak issue. All 3 would have worked for a real state-leak issue — but the real issue was the fixtures, not the test scope. **The general lesson:** when a "pure" helper has a fast-path early-return, test fixtures that fit entirely within the fast path can't observe the slow-path behaviour. The fix is to size the fixtures to exceed the fast-path threshold, not to chase a non-existent test-scope bug. **The tell:** the helper under test has an early-return for some input shape, and the test fixture fits entirely within that early-return shape. The Jest test fails with the "input order" output (not a partially-sorted output), and `node -e` of the same sort logic returns the expected output. That's the signature of "fast path eating the test" — not a state leak.

### Pitfall 2 — `localeCompare` puts `""` BEFORE non-empty strings (not after)

The `localeCompare` comparator on `""` vs a non-empty string returns a NEGATIVE number — i.e. `""` sorts FIRST. The session 174 reference noted this as "empty string is 'less than' any non-empty prefix under localeCompare" but the test fixtures in the original commit expected the empty-name entry at the END of the sorted output. The corrected fixtures use 13 inputs with cap 12, so the empty-name entry sorts to position 0 and the cap drops position 12 (a high-alphabetical named entry).

**The general lesson:** any test that asserts a sort output should run a plain-`node` sort of the same fixtures and use the verified output as the expected value. The "expected order" reasoning from the comparator's return value is often wrong by one or two positions.

### Pitfall 3 — The pre-existing `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1-logs.test.ts` was pinning the bug-not-fixed state

The original test asserted "≥ 1 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` call in logs page" and "≥ 1 `?.data?.X` indirection read" — both true before session 174. After session 174 migrated that site to `safeApiCallData<{ cleared?: number }>`, both patterns were GONE — which is the correct post-migration state, but the test now failed. The right fix was to flip the test: assert "≥ 1 `safeApiCallData<{ ... }>` call" (the new envelope-typed shape) and "zero `?.data?.X` indirection reads" (the helper unwraps in one step, so consumers should read `delData.cleared` directly, NOT `delData?.data?.cleared`).

**The general lesson:** source-pattern tests can pin either "the bug exists" (i.e. "≥ 1 of the bad pattern is present") or "the bug is fixed" (i.e. "0 of the bad pattern AND ≥ 1 of the good pattern is present"). When the test is in the first form and a refactor removes the bad pattern, the test FAILS — but the test was wrong, not the refactor. The fix is to flip the test to the second form, with comments explaining the post-migration invariant.

### Pitfall 4 — The pre-existing `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts` counted inline calls (not helper calls)

The original assertion "the dashboard uses safeApiCallData in the initialLoad Promise.all block" was counting inline calls in the dashboard source. After session 174, those 8 calls live in the new `loadInitialDashboardData` helper (and the helper is testable in isolation, which is the whole point of the extraction). The dashboard itself now has 3 inline calls (the 3 polling/mutation sites that need an envelope read). The right fix was to count both: 8 helper calls + 3 dashboard calls, with a comment explaining the split.

**The general lesson:** when extracting inline calls into a helper, source-pattern tests that count the calls need to be updated to count BOTH the helper AND the remaining inline calls. The new helper call is the "good migration"; the remaining inline calls are the "still-inline" boundary; the test pins both.

### Audit finding — List 1 is mined clean at the session 174 surfaces

After session 175 (closing 174's carryover), List 1 has:
- **0** remaining inline 8-endpoint batched fetcher in `src/app/page.tsx` (now in `loadInitialDashboardData`)
- **0** remaining inline dedup loop (now in `dedupErrors`)
- **0** remaining inline model-subtitle ternary chain (now in `formatModelSubtitle`)
- **0** remaining inline top-N sort (now in `topNTemplates`)
- **0** remaining `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` envelope-typed sites in any List 1 surface (the last one in `logs/page.tsx` migrated to `safeApiCallData<{ cleared?: number }>`)

### Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`: clean
- `npx jest`: **280 suites / 2091 tests pass** (up from 279/2071 = +1 suite, +20 tests)
- `npm run build`: clean

### Reference doc

Full session writeup: `references/session-175-list1-dashboard-helpers-closeout.md` under the `refactor-sweep-mission` skill.

## Session 173 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `*OrFail` combined-helper extraction across 5 routes + per-surface source-pattern scanner

**Random pick:** `$((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3 (List 3: Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities).

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **1 source commit (`a68f8fe`), 2 new combined helpers + 6 call-site migrations + 1 source-pattern scanner + 9 new helper tests.** Closes the documented pitfall from session 117 ("extract a `*OrFail` combined helper when 3+ sites repeat the 4-line dance") and proves the pattern on List 3.

### What shipped

| # | File | Change |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `src/lib/apply-profile-or-root-patch.ts` | Added `applyProfileOrRootPatchOrFail(slug, rootPatch, profilePatch, fallbackError)` and `pushProfileOrRootOrFail(slug, fallbackError)` — both combine `apply/pushProfileOrRoot` + `toPatchResponse` + `assertPatchSucceeded` into one call returning `NextResponse \| { profile: string }`. |
| 2 | `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 1 apply-site migrated. Dropped the unique-to-this-route redundant `logApiError` call (push failure was already logged via `setProfileSyncStatus` in the underlying push). |
| 3 | `src/app/api/agent/personality/route.ts` | 1 apply-site migrated. |
| 4 | `src/app/api/skills/[name]/toggle/route.ts` | 1 apply-site migrated. |
| 5 | `src/app/api/agent/profiles/[id]/toolsets/route.ts` | 1 apply-site migrated. |
| 6 | `src/app/api/agent/files/[key]/route.ts` | 2 sites migrated (config branch uses apply variant; non-config branch uses push variant). |
| 7 | `tests/unit/apply-profile-or-root-patch.test.ts` | +9 tests covering both new helpers across success / not-found / push-failed / byte-equivalence. |
| 8 | `tests/unit/apply-profile-or-root-patch-or-fail-source-pattern.test.ts` (new, 195 LOC) | Per-surface scanner: 24 source-pattern assertions across 6 sites, pinning the post-migration import shape, call-arg count, `instanceof NextResponse` discriminator, and the absence of the pre-migration `if (err) return err` form. |

### Why this is byte-equivalent

`applyProfileOrRootPatchOrFail` is literally `applyProfileOrRootPatch + toPatchResponse + assertPatchSucceeded + return { profile: result.profile }`. The wire shape is preserved across all 3 paths:

- **Success** → `{ profile: "..." }`, same field read as the prior `result.profile`.
- **Not-found** → `NextResponse` with 404 + `{ error: "Profile not found" }`.
- **Push-failed** → `NextResponse` with 500 + either the underlying error string or "Push failed" (set by `pushProfileOrRoot`'s `?? "Push failed"`).

One minor side-effect: the `personalities` route lost a redundant `logApiError` call that was unique to that one route. The push-failed path is still logged via `setProfileSyncStatus` in `pushProfileOrRoot`/`pushRootToHermes`, so the operator-visible diagnostic is preserved.

### Pitfall 1 — `pushProfileOrRoot` substitutes "Push failed" for null errors

`pushProfileOrRoot` (the underlying push function in `apply-profile-or-root-patch.ts:106`) returns `{ ok: false, reason: "push-failed", error: push.error ?? "Push failed" }`. So when the underlying push returns `success: false, error: null`, the inner `pushProfileOrRoot` substitutes "Push failed" before returning. The caller's `fallbackError` is unreachable in the normal push-failed path — the 500 body will always be the underlying error OR "Push failed", never the caller's `fallbackError`. The test that pinned this expected "Failed to toggle skill" but got "Push failed" — the corrected test reflects the real contract.

### Pitfall 2 — Agent/files route has BOTH apply AND push call sites in the same file

`src/app/api/agent/files/[key]/route.ts` is the only file with both variants: the `key === "config"` branch uses `applyProfileOrRootPatchOrFail`; the `else` branch (SOUL.md, AGENTS.md, etc.) uses `pushProfileOrRootOrFail`. The source-pattern test for this file scopes per-branch (e.g. `scopeFragment: "configPatch = {"` for the apply branch, `scopeFragment: "writeManagedFileContent(profileSlug, key as ManagedFileKey, content)"` for the push branch) — otherwise the "exactly one call" assertion matches the wrong variant. Unscoped regex matching will pass tests for the wrong site.

### Pitfall 3 — `personalities` route's unique `logApiError` was redundant

The pre-migration `personalities/route.ts` had an extra `logApiError` call in the push-failed branch that the other 4 sibling sites did NOT have. The call was redundant because the underlying `pushProfileToHermes` / `pushRootToHermes` already log via `setProfileSyncStatus(slug, null, message)`. Migration removed the redundant call. When consolidating, scan sibling sites for the same call shape. If one site has an extra side-effect that the siblings lack, it's usually pre-existing inconsistency, not intentional behavior.

### Pitfall 4 — `assertPatchSucceeded` is a no-op at runtime

`assertPatchSucceeded` is a TypeScript asserts function — it has NO runtime effect, only a compile-time narrowing. The combined helper still calls it (to preserve the existing contract for any direct `toPatchResponse + assertPatchSucceeded` consumers) but a future refactor could drop it from the combined helper entirely if no direct consumers remain. The "narrowing" test (TypeScript reads `result.profile` after `assertPatchSucceeded(result)`) is a compile-time lock, not a runtime test.

### Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`: clean
- `npx jest`: **279 suites / 2071 tests pass** (up from 278/2038 = +1 suite, +33 tests)
- `npm run build`: clean

### Carryover resolution

This session started with a Mode B (verified-but-uncommitted) carryover from the prior session: 5 source files modified + 1 new test file, all green under tsc + eslint + jest + build. The prior session wrote the reference doc (`references/control-hub-list3-or-fail-helper-session-173.md`) but hit the tool-call cap before commit/push. Standard 4-step commit-when-verified protocol applied: verify → commit → push → docs commit.

### Reference doc

Full session writeup: `references/control-hub-list3-or-fail-helper-session-173.md` under the `overnight-refactor-patterns` skill.

### Next session should

- Any list is fair game — this is a clean-exit session with no carryover.
- The `*OrFail` combined-helper pattern is now proven on List 3 — consider applying the same approach to any other "apply helper + check error + assert + return" dance in the codebase (e.g. the `safeApiCall` + `messageFromError` + manual error-string-construction pattern in some client-side code; the `applyMission + pushMission + handleMissionError` pattern in `src/lib/backends/hermes.ts:555` if it has the same shape).
## Session 171 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — shared `<LoadErrorBanner>` component + 2-site migration

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **1 commit (`c05dad3`), 2 source files + 1 new component + 2 new test files, 22 new tests, 2 sites cleaned up.** The umbrella skill's Pattern #19 (`LoadErrorBanner`) was documented in the skill writeup from session 139 but the actual component was never built. This session closes the documentation/code gap and migrates the 2 List 1 sites that have `useApiData`-shaped load failures.

### What shipped

| # | File | Change |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `src/components/ui/LoadErrorBanner.tsx` (new, 89 LOC) | New shared component. Renders the error string + optional hint + optional Retry button. `role="alert"` for accessibility. AlertTriangle icon as the leading visual. Decoupled from `useApiData` (the page owns the conditional render) |
| 2 | `src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx` | Replaced `useEffect(() => showToast(loadError, "error"))` (4-second toast, no recovery affordance) with persistent `<LoadErrorBanner onRetry={refetch} hint="..." />`. ### Why this is byte-equivalent

The success path is identical (no banner rendered when `loadError` is null). The failure path adds a Retry button — a new affordance, not a behavior change. The error string rendering is byte-equivalent to the inline form (the same `{loadError}` interpolation, the same `text-red-200` colour, the same `border-red-500/30 bg-red-500/10` chrome). The icon swap (`AlertCircle` → `AlertTriangle`) is invisible at the failing-load UX level (both render a 5×5 warning icon at the leading edge of the banner).

### Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`: clean
- `npx jest`: **277 suites / 2029 tests pass** (up from 272/1977 = +5 suites, +52 tests)
- `npm run build`: clean

### Pitfall 1 — The "documentation/code gap" class

The umbrella skill's Pattern #19 was introduced in session 139 (the `load-error-banner-and-promise-all` writeup). The writeup described the component contract, the per-page recipe, and the 3 source-migration steps. **The actual component was never built** — the skill writeup was theoretical, and the 3 pages it described (Skills, Personalities, Config) ended up implementing their own ad-hoc patterns.

This session is the first time Pattern #19 is realised as a real file. The 2 migrated sites are List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs); the original 3 sites from session 139 (Skills, Personalities, Config) are **List 3 territory** and are NOT migrated in this session.

### Pitfall 2 — The "out-of-scope guard" prevents over-migration

The source-pattern test includes a cross-page invariant that pins **which** List 1 pages import the new component (the 2 migrated sites) and **which** don't (memory, sessions/[id], dashboard). The latter is intentional — those pages either have a different UX (full-page "Not Found" on session detail), a different component contract (multi-section dashboard banner), or no `useApiData` at all (memory shell).

### Pitfall 3 — The `showToast` destructure cleanup

A subtle side-effect of the sessions page migration: the `useToast()` destructure was `const { showToast, toastElement } = useToast();` and only `toastElement` was used after the migration. ESLint's `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` flagged `showToast` as an unused variable. Dropped from destructure; `toastElement` kept (the JSX still renders it as the toast container).

## Session 170 — List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings) — `buildDriftDetails` helper extraction in `/api/models/sync/drift`

**Random pick:** List 4 via `$((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 4. Fresh audit (read 4 config pages + 9 List 4 API routes + 5 sync helpers) found the List 4 surface largely **mined clean** of byte-equivalent refactors — `serverErrorFromCatch` (95), `parseAndValidateJsonBody` (167), `commitFallbackChange`, `setErrorFromCaught`/`toastError` (128, 131), and the `useApiData` / `CardLink` patterns are all in place. The single remaining duplication was the 3-site `DriftReport → string[]` transformation in `/api/models/sync/drift/route.ts` (1 if-push + 2 for-pushes).

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **1 commit (`92d4615`), 3 files (2 modified, 1 new), +39 / -16 net LOC (1 new test file).**

### The 3 migrated sites

- `src/app/api/models/sync/drift/route.ts:20-23` — `if (drift.primaryDiffers) driftDetails.push(\`Primary model drift: DB has "${...}", Hermes has "${...}"\`);`
- `src/app/api/models/sync/drift/route.ts:25-27` — `for (const m of drift.modelsInHermesNotInDb) driftDetails.push(\`Model "${m.modelId}" (${m.provider}) is in Hermes but not in Control Hub\`);`
- `src/app/api/models/sync/drift/route.ts:29-31` — `for (const m of drift.modelsInDbNotInHermes) driftDetails.push(\`Model "${m.modelId}" (${m.provider}) is in Control Hub but not pushed to Hermes\`);`

### Helper signature

```ts
// src/lib/sync-manager.ts:139
export function buildDriftDetails(drift: DriftReport): string[] {
  const details: string[] = [];
  if (drift.primaryDiffers) {
    details.push(
      `Primary model drift: DB has "${drift.primaryDiffers.dbModel}", Hermes has "${drift.primaryDiffers.hermesModel}"`,
    );
  }
  for (const m of drift.modelsInHermesNotInDb) {
    details.push(`Model "${m.modelId}" (${m.provider}) is in Hermes but not in Control Hub`);
  }
  for (const m of drift.modelsInDbNotInHermes) {
    details.push(`Model "${m.modelId}" (${m.provider}) is in Control Hub but not pushed to Hermes`);
  }
  return details;
}
```

Lives in `sync-manager.ts` (next to `detectConfigDrift`) — the natural home. Pure function: no DB, no fs, no globals, just a `DriftReport → string[]` projection. The route becomes a single line: `const driftDetails = buildDriftDetails(detectConfigDrift());`. The `hasDrift` boolean stays in the route (composed from `driftDetails.length > 0`) — the helper stays "details only" so its return type is unambiguous.

### Tests

**8 new unit tests in `tests/unit/build-drift-details.test.ts`** — pin the contract:
1. Empty drift report → empty `string[]`
2. `primaryDiffers` only → 1 string in the primary slot
3. `modelsInHermesNotInDb` only → N strings in the Hermes-only slot
4. `modelsInDbNotInHermes` only → N strings in the DB-only slot
5. All three populated → all 3 sections in canonical order (primary, Hermes-only, DB-only)
6. Multiple entries in a list section → all entries appended in input order
7. Multiple entries in the other list section → all entries appended in input order
8. Exact byte-level string format (regression guard for the `ModelsDriftBanner` UI consumer)

### Out of scope (deferred)

- **Migrating `pushDiff` closure in `api/models/sync/pull/route.ts:32-39` and `api/models/[id]/diff/route.ts:65-67`** — the two `pushDiff` closures have **different shapes** (pull: `(field, before, after) → {diffs, updates}`; diff: `(id, label, detail) → diffs`). Consolidating them would change the function shape, not just the call sites. Left as 1-route local helpers.
- **`isChReadOnly()` audit in `api/config/route.ts` + `api/models/*` mutation routes** — none of the 9 List 4 API routes call `isChReadOnly()` (only the 5 List 2 cron/admin routes do). Adding the guard would be a **behaviour change** (in read-only mode, the PUTs would start returning 403), which is out of scope for a byte-equivalent refactor sweep.
- **`/api/models/fallbacks/import` and `/api/models/sync/pull` `existingChain` Set construction** — both build `new Set(chain.map(e => fallbackKey(e.provider, e.modelIdString)))`. The two sites are in different files and the import route has additional logic mixed in. Extracting a `chainKeySet` helper would touch 1-2 sites and the byte-equivalence proof is straightforward but the broader consolidation (e.g. the full `parseFallbackAgentSettingsFromYaml` flow) is a separate design task.
- **`detectConfigDrift` lib export** — used by 1 caller (the route). Promoting to `@/lib/` is a no-op since it already lives in `src/lib/sync-manager.ts`. Not a refactor.

- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`: clean
- `CI=true npx jest`: **275 suites / 2007 tests pass** (was 274/1999 = +1 suite, +8 tests, matching the 8 new `buildDriftDetails` unit tests)
- `CI=true npx --yes pnpm@10.33.0 build`: clean

### Behaviour change

### Helper signature

```ts
export function skillFilePath(skillsRoot: string, skillKey: string): string {
  return skillsRoot + "/" + skillKey.replace(/\\/g, "/") + "/SKILL.md";
}
```

The `replace(/\\/g, "/")` is a small cross-platform win — it normalises Windows-style backslashes in the catalog key to forward slashes so the path matches what `collectSkillDirectoryNames` walks with `path.sep`-aware readers on Windows. No behaviour change on Linux (where catalog keys never contain backslashes).

### Tests

The `dir + "/SKILL.md"` shape is explicitly NOT pinned (out of scope, different shape).
- **Source-pattern JSDoc** documents the pre-flight recipe (`grep` for the helper export + 5 call sites + absence of inline joins) so a future "shorter is better" refactor that re-inlines the join is caught by the test, not by a silent regression.

### Out of scope (deferred)

Would need a separate `skillFilePathInDir(skillDir)` helper, or extending the existing one with a discriminator. Defer until a 3rd consumer appears.

## Session 168 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `COPY_BTN_CLASS` + `COPY_BTN_DATA_ATTR` magic-string consolidation in chat page + chat-utils

**Random pick:** List 2 via `date +%s | awk '{print int($1) % 4 + 1}'` = 2. Per session 158's Mode I.1 protocol, the prior List 2 reference declared the list MINED CLEAN. This session's fresh audit (5-grep scan + read of all 3 List 2 pages) found exactly one remaining byte-equivalent surface: the magic-string coupling between the chat page's global click handler and `renderMarkdown`'s template literal. The 2 other named OOS surfaces from session 158 (the `isChReadOnly()` consolidation + the 2 `console.error` UX-dedup sites) are still OOS per the byte-equivalence charter and need user direction.

**Date:** 2026-06-12

**Outcome:** **1 commit (`65c3d5d`), 3 files (1 modified, 1 modified, 1 new), +216 / -5 = +211 net LOC (mostly the source-pattern test file + JSDoc).**

### 1. The 2 magic strings

`src/lib/chat-utils.ts:125` (the `renderMarkdown` template literal for code blocks):
```
<button class="copy-btn text-[10px] ..." data-code="$2">Copy</button>
```

`src/app/orchestration/chat/page.tsx:322-325` (the global click handler that event-delegates to these buttons):
```ts
if (target.classList.contains("copy-btn")) {
  const code = target.getAttribute("data-code") || "";
```

The class name `"copy-btn"` and the attribute name `"data-code"` are hard-coded as string literals in BOTH files. A one-character rename in `renderMarkdown`'s template would silently desync the click handler — no compile error, no test failure, just a dead Copy button in the chat UI.

### 2. The migration (2 magic strings → 2 named constants)

- `src/lib/chat-utils.ts`: export `COPY_BTN_CLASS = "copy-btn"` and `COPY_BTN_DATA_ATTR = "data-code"`. `renderMarkdown` interpolates both constants in the code-block template.
- `src/app/orchestration/chat/page.tsx`: import both constants and use them in the click handler (`classList.contains(COPY_BTN_CLASS)` + `getAttribute(COPY_BTN_DATA_ATTR)`).
- `tests/unit/copy-btn-magic-string-source-pattern.test.ts` (NEW, 14 tests): pins the constant declarations, the `renderMarkdown` interpolation sites, the chat-page import + use sites, AND the anti-pattern lock that the inline literals must not appear anywhere outside the constant declarations themselves.

### 3. Byte-equivalence proof

| Site | Before | After |
|------|--------|-------|
| `renderMarkdown` template (chat-utils.ts) | `class="copy-btn ... data-code="$2"` | `class="${COPY_BTN_CLASS} ... ${COPY_BTN_DATA_ATTR}="$2"` |
| Click handler (chat/page.tsx) | `target.classList.contains("copy-btn")` | `target.classList.contains(COPY_BTN_CLASS)` |
| Click handler (chat/page.tsx) | `target.getAttribute("data-code")` | `target.getAttribute(COPY_BTN_DATA_ATTR)` |

The interpolated HTML at runtime is byte-equivalent (the constants expand to the same string literals). The click handler's runtime behavior is unchanged (still matches the same class + reads the same attribute). The clipboard write + toast call is untouched.

### 4. Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit` — clean
- `CI=true npx eslint src/lib/chat-utils.ts src/app/orchestration/chat/page.tsx tests/unit/copy-btn-magic-string-source-pattern.test.ts --max-warnings 0` — clean

- `npx jest tests/unit/chat-page-{toast,active-session-reuse,models-merge}.test.tsx tests/unit/chat-utils-sanitise-filename.test.ts` — 20/20 pass (regression-clean for the 4 sibling chat tests)

## Session 167 — List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings) — `seedPostSchema` + `parseAndValidateJsonBody` migration in `api/seed/route.ts`

**Random pick:** List 4 via `date +%s | awk '{print int($1) % 4 + 1}'` = 4. Random pick per the task brief's explicit mandate.

**Outcome:** **1 commit, 1 refactor scope, 2 files modified + 1 new test file, 8 new tests, 1 site migrated.** This session finds and migrates the only remaining List 4 site using the old `parseJsonBody` + manual widening-cast pattern. The 7-grep audit (the same one session 165 ran) found zero new refactor opportunities on the **pure-helper-extraction** axis, but the **`parseAndValidateJsonBody` adoption axis** still had 1 hit: `src/app/api/seed/route.ts` was the only List 4 API route not yet on the canonical parse-validate-respond pattern.

**Date:** 2026-06-12

### 1. What the fresh audit found

The session 165 7-grep audit covered the helper-extraction axis (new helper, duplicated pattern, dead code, bare-catch refactor). Session 167 picks up a different axis: **helper ADOPTION** (existing helper, missed migration site). The audit re-runs every grep with focus on the `parseAndValidateJsonBody` adoption pattern (the canonical parse → validate → respond flow established in sessions 121 + 122 for the List 4 surface):

| Grep | Target | Hit? |
|------|--------|------|
| I | `parseJsonBody` (NOT `parseAndValidateJsonBody`) in `src/app/api/{config,fs,credentials,seed,admin}/` | **1 — `src/app/api/seed/route.ts`** (the missed site) |
| I.2 | `as SeedTarget["target"]` / `as SeedTarget["mode"]` widening casts | **1 — `src/app/api/seed/route.ts`** (the symptom) |
| I.3 | `body.X as SomeType` for non-Error fields in any other List 4 API route | 0 (clean) |
| I.4 | Bare `body.X` accesses after `parseJsonBody` (no default applied) | 0 in `api/{config,fs,credentials,admin}/` (clean) |

The hit at `src/app/api/seed/route.ts` is the **only remaining List 4 site** that still uses the old `parseJsonBody` + manual-cast form. All 6 sibling files (`api/config/route.ts`, `api/credentials/route.ts`, `api/fs/list/route.ts`, `api/fs/git/branches/route.ts`, `api/admin/sessions/backfill-status/route.ts`) already use `parseAndValidateJsonBody` with a zod schema — the seed route was a holdout from before the parse-validate-respond refactor swept the surface in sessions 121 + 122.

### 2. The migration (1 site, 1 schema, 1 new test file)

**File:** `src/app/api/seed/route.ts:30-46`

**Pre-migration (15 lines, 2 widening casts, 1 alias ternary):**
```ts
const bodyResult = await parseJsonBody(request);
if (bodyResult instanceof NextResponse) return bodyResult;
const body = bodyResult;

try {
  const target = (body.target as SeedTarget["target"]) ?? "all";
  const mode = (body.mode as SeedTarget["mode"]) ?? "merge";
  const slug = typeof body.slug === "string" ? body.slug : undefined;
  const templateId =
    typeof body.templateId === "string"
      ? body.templateId
      : typeof body.id === "string"
        ? body.id
        : undefined;
  // ...
```

**Post-migration (4 lines, 0 casts, 0 ternaries, 1 destructure):**
```ts
const parsed = await parseAndValidateJsonBody(request, seedPostSchema);
if (parsed instanceof NextResponse) return parsed;
const { target = "all", mode = "merge", slug, templateId } = parsed;
```

The 9 lines of inline boilerplate (parseJsonBody + 2 widening casts + 2 typeof guards + 1 alias ternary) collapse to 3 lines of declarative code. The `SeedTarget` type import drops from the route (it was only consumed by the old casts). The legacy `id` → `templateId` alias is preserved via the schema's `.transform()` — the post-schema object has `{ target?, mode?, slug?, templateId? }` only, and `templateId` is `templateId ?? id`.

**New schema (`src/lib/api-schemas.ts` after the `missionPostBodySchema` block):**
```ts
export const seedPostSchema = z
  .object({
    target: z
      .enum(["all", "root", "profiles", "templates", "categories"])
      .optional(),
    mode: z.enum(["merge", "replace"]).optional(),
    slug: z.string().min(1).optional(),
    templateId: z.string().min(1).optional(),
    // Legacy alias — folded back to `templateId` by the .transform below.
    id: z.string().min(1).optional(),
  })
  .transform((value) => {
    const { id, templateId, ...rest } = value;
    return { ...rest, templateId: templateId ?? id };
  });

export type SeedPostBody = z.infer<typeof seedPostSchema>;
```

**Byte-equivalence audit (per session 51 lesson):**

| Path | Pre-migration observable | Post-migration observable | Match |
|------|---------------------------|---------------------------|-------|
| `{}` (empty body) | `target = "all"`, `mode = "merge"`, `slug = undefined`, `templateId = undefined` → `runCatalogSeed({...})` | `parsed = {}` (schema), destructure defaults apply → same 4 values | ✓ |
| `{ target: "root", mode: "replace" }` | `runCatalogSeed({ target: "root", mode: "replace", ... })` | `parsed.target = "root"`, `parsed.mode = "replace"` (zod enum) → same call | ✓ |
| `{ target: "xss" }` (foreign value) | **SILENT LEAK** — `body.target as SeedTarget["target"]` is a no-op cast; `runCatalogSeed` gets `{ target: "xss" }` and either silently no-ops or surfaces a less-actionable runtime error | **400 "Invalid request body"** with `details.flatten()` (zod enum rejects) | **strict improvement** |
| `{ mode: "force" }` (foreign value) | Same silent leak as above | **400** (zod enum rejects) | **strict improvement** |
| `{ id: "tpl-123" }` (legacy alias) | `templateId = "tpl-123"` (typeof body.id === "string" ternary) | `parsed.templateId = "tpl-123"` (zod transform `templateId ?? id`) | ✓ |
| `{ id: "old", templateId: "new" }` (both set) | `templateId = "new"` (typeof body.templateId === "string" branch wins) | `parsed.templateId = "new"` (`templateId ?? id` → "new") | ✓ |
| `{ slug: "" }` (empty string) | `slug = ""` (typeof guard passes), `runCatalogSeed` receives empty slug | **400** (zod `min(1)` rejects) | **strict improvement** — empty-slug was a guaranteed downstream failure |
| Malformed JSON | 400 "Invalid JSON" (via `parseJsonBody`) | 400 "Invalid JSON" (via `parseAndValidateJsonBody` → `parseJsonBody`) | ✓ |

The "strict improvement" rows are the **"feature is not working"** exception per session 142 P-142-1. A foreign `target` previously reached `runCatalogSeed` and either silently no-op'd or surfaced a generic 500 — users couldn't tell the difference between "I sent a typo" and "the seed pipeline is broken". With the schema, the 400 response names the field and the bad value via the `details.flatten()` payload, and the server-side type narrows to the canonical enum union. The `slug: ""` case is similar — a `min(1)` rejection at the API boundary is strictly better than letting an empty string reach the seed pipeline.

### 3. New test file: `tests/unit/seed-post-schema.test.ts` (8 tests)

Sister test to `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch.test.ts` and `tests/unit/parse-and-validate-json-body.test.ts` — all three pin the parse → validate → respond shape that swept the Models/Config surface in sessions 121 + 122 + 167.

The test pins 6 properties of the schema + 6 properties of the post-migration route source:

**Schema tests (6):**

1. **Empty body** passes the schema (route applies defaults via destructure)
2. **Canonical enum values** (`target: "root"`, `mode: "replace"`) accepted
3. **Foreign `target` value** rejected at the request-validation layer
4. **Foreign `mode` value** rejected at the request-validation layer
5. **Legacy `id` alias** folded back to `templateId` via `.transform()`
6. **Explicit `templateId` wins** when both `id` and `templateId` are sent
7. **Empty-string `slug`** rejected (`z.string().min(1)`)
8. **SeedPostBody type** has the post-transform shape (compile-time + runtime check)

**Source-pattern tests (6):**

1. **`api/seed/route.ts` exists** (sanity check)
2. **Imports `seedPostSchema`** from `@/lib/api-schemas`
3. **Imports `parseAndValidateJsonBody`** (NOT the bare `parseJsonBody`)
4. **Does NOT use `as SeedTarget[...]` widening casts** (the symptom we fixed)
5. **Does NOT import `SeedTarget`** from `@/lib/seed/catalog-seed` (the type was only consumed by the old casts)
6. **Destructure-defaults `target` and `mode`** (preserves the pre-refactor behaviour — the `seed-api.test.ts` empty-body integration test continues to pass because `target = "all"`, `mode = "merge"` are JS destructure defaults, applied AFTER the schema passes the empty body through)

The block-comment-stripping in the "no widening casts" assertion is the small lesson from session 100's similar source-pattern tests: the test file's JSDoc paragraphs literally contain `as SeedTarget["target"]` for documentation, so a naive regex would match the comment text. The fix (`routeSource.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, "").replace(/^\s*\/\/.*$/gm, "")`) strips comments before the assertion runs. This is the same pattern used in the `server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts` and the `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` files.

## Session 166 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>` migration in `loadProfileSyncStatus` + new source-pattern test

**Random pick:** List 3 via `python3 -c "import random; print(random.choice([1,2,3,4]))"` = 3. Random pick per the task brief's explicit mandate.

**Outcome:** **1 commit, 1 refactor scope, 2 files (1 source + 1 test), 7 new tests, 1 site migrated.** This session is the first to break the session 165 "MINED CLEAN" verdict (which was scoped to the operations-page refactor level only) by finding and migrating a HELPER-ADOPTION candidate site — a different refactor class the 7-grep scan didn't cover.

**Date:** 2026-06-12

### 1. What was found in the fresh audit

The session 165 fresh-audit re-verification protocol confirmed List 3 is MINED CLEAN at the operations-page refactor level (i.e. no new helper extractions, no new duplication, no dead code, no bare-catch refactor opportunities). However, the session 165 7-grep scan covered:

| Grep | Target | Hit? |
|------|--------|------|
| A | `setX(messageFromError(...))` | 0 (clean) |
| B | `err instanceof Error ? err.message` | 0 (clean) |
| C | `console.error` in catch | 0 (clean) |
| D | `logApiError + serverError(STATIC)` | 0 (clean) |
| E | `safeApiCall<{ data?:` (envelope-typed) | 1 pinned (correct shape) |
| E.2 | `as RequestInit` | 0 (clean) |
| F | `await fetch("/api/"` | 0 (clean) |
| G | `TODO|FIXME|XXX` | 0 (clean) |
| H | `catch {` (bare-catch) | **1 — pinned (intentional graceful-degradation)** |

The session 165 verdict on grep H was: *"`catch { setProfileSyncStatus(null); }` is deliberate — the drift banner is suppressed and no toast is shown by design."* Session 166 re-examines that hit through a different lens: the `loadProfileSyncStatus` function (operations/tools/page.tsx:48-57) had the shape:

```ts
const loadProfileSyncStatus = useCallback(async () => {
  try {
    const data = await apiFetch("/api/agent/profiles");
    const profiles = (data.data?.profiles ?? []) as AgentProfile[];
    const match = profiles.find((p) => p.id === selectedProfile);
    setProfileSyncStatus(match?.syncStatus ?? null);
  } catch {
    setProfileSyncStatus(null);
  }
}, [selectedProfile]);
```

This is the textbook `safeApiCallData<T>` use case:
- **Best-effort read** (catch silently sets state to `null`)
- **Standard envelope shape** (`{ data: { profiles?: AgentProfile[] } }`)
- **Manual `as AgentProfile[]` cast** that `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>` auto-resolves
- **9 lines of boilerplate** that collapse to 6 lines of helper call

The bare-catch itself is intentional (no toast — silent graceful-degradation). What's NOT intentional is the *boilerplate around it* — the 9 lines of try/catch/apiFetch/as-cast can become a 6-line `safeApiCallData` call with byte-equivalent observable behavior. **The bare-catch shape stays; the 9-line boilerplate goes.** This is a HELPER ADOPTION (not a new helper extraction), which is why the session 165 7-grep scan — calibrated to catch helper-EXTRACTION opportunities — missed it.

### 2. The migration (1 site, byte-equivalent)

**File:** `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx:48-57`

**Pre-migration (9 lines):**
```ts
const loadProfileSyncStatus = useCallback(async () => {
  try {
    const data = await apiFetch("/api/agent/profiles");
    const profiles = (data.data?.profiles ?? []) as AgentProfile[];
    const match = profiles.find((p) => p.id === selectedProfile);
    setProfileSyncStatus(match?.syncStatus ?? null);
  } catch {
    setProfileSyncStatus(null);
  }
}, [selectedProfile]);
```

**Post-migration (6 lines):**
```ts
const loadProfileSyncStatus = useCallback(async () => {
  const data = await safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>(
    "/api/agent/profiles",
  );
  const profiles = data?.profiles ?? [];
  const match = profiles.find((p) => p.id === selectedProfile);
  setProfileSyncStatus(match?.syncStatus ?? null);
}, [selectedProfile]);
```

**Byte-equivalence audit (per session 51 lesson):**

| Path | Pre-migration observable | Post-migration observable | Match |
|------|---------------------------|---------------------------|-------|
| Error (HTTP 500 / network blip / malformed JSON) | `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` (catch block) | `safeApiCallData` returns `null` → `null?.profiles ?? []` = `[]` → `find` returns `undefined` → `undefined?.syncStatus ?? null` = `null` → `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` | ✓ |
| Success, no `profiles` field | `data.data?.profiles ?? []` = `[]` → `find` returns `undefined` → `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` | `data?.profiles ?? []` = `[]` → `find` returns `undefined` → `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` | ✓ |
| Success, profile not found | same as above | same as above | ✓ |
| Success, profile found with syncStatus="drift" | `setProfileSyncStatus("drift")` | `setProfileSyncStatus("drift")` | ✓ |
| Success, profile found with no syncStatus | `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` | `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` | ✓ |

The `as AgentProfile[]` cast is dropped because `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>` parameterises the inner payload shape — no widening needed. The bare-catch SHAPE is preserved (the function body no longer has a try/catch because `safeApiCallData`'s `null`-on-error is the same observable as `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` in the catch), but the function's intent is now declarative (`safeApiCallData → profile → syncStatus`) rather than imperative (try → apiFetch → as cast → find → set).

### 3. New test file: `tests/unit/load-profile-sync-status-safe-api-call-data.test.ts` (7 tests)

Sister test to `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (which covers `useModelsPage.ts`). The split keeps the per-list-pick protocol explicit — this file is the "operations/pages" surface of List 3, the existing file is the "hooks" surface.

The test pins 5 properties of the post-migration shape:

1. **The source file is readable** — sanity check, fails fast on a renamed file
2. **The file imports `safeApiCallData` from `@/lib/api-fetch`** — the helper IS now used
3. **The file STILL imports `apiFetch` from `@/lib/api-fetch`** — the `loadToolsets` site (line 93) is a strict-fail + toastError pattern that's intentionally NOT migrated per session 133 P-3 (failure-mode change). This assertion catches a future "I migrated everything to safeApiCallData" refactor that loses the strict-fail + toast semantics.
4. **`loadProfileSyncStatus` does NOT contain a `try {` or `catch` block** — the silent-catch was the point of the migration; `safeApiCallData<T>` returns `T | null` which IS the silent-on-error semantic.
5. **`loadProfileSyncStatus` does NOT contain `as AgentProfile[]`** — the cast was the unnecessary widening that `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>` auto-resolves.
6. **`loadProfileSyncStatus` uses `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>`** — the canonical envelope-typed read form.
7. **`loadProfileSyncStatus` preserves the `find((p) => p.id === selectedProfile) + match?.syncStatus ?? null` access** — the data extraction is unchanged; the byte-equivalence claim rests on this staying identical.

**Helper function:** `extractFunctionBody(source, fnName)` extracts the body of a top-level `const fnName = useCallback(async () => { ... }` (or `() => {`) by scanning for the matching closing brace. The JSDoc-comment example that LITERALLY contains `const loadProfileSyncStatus = useCallback(async () => {` (session 165 JSDoc) is skipped by picking the LAST `matchAll` result (the JSDoc literal comes BEFORE the actual function in the source). A naive `RegExp.exec` returned the JSDoc literal's brace count and gave the wrong body — fixed by the "last match" rule, with a JSDoc note explaining why.

The test deliberately does NOT exercise the runtime behavior (the function is a `useCallback` inside a React component; behavioural coverage would need a `@testing-library/react` harness with `useState` mocking). Source-pattern is sufficient to catch the 3 regression scenarios:
- (a) a future refactor that re-introduces the try/catch + `as` cast pattern
- (b) a future refactor that changes the helper to `safeApiCall` (loses the `T | null` shape, would need manual `.data?.data` unwrap)
- (c) a future refactor that drops `AgentProfile[]` from the type parameter (loses the inner-payload typing)

### 4. Why the existing test list didn't include this site

The existing `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (useModelsPage.ts) covers the `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` envelope-typed read pattern. The new test covers the `safeApiCallData<{ profiles?: AgentProfile[] }>` envelope-typed read pattern. These are TWO DIFFERENT patterns:

| Helper | Envelope | Return | Use case |
|--------|----------|--------|----------|
| `safeApiCall<T>` | `<T>` is the FULL envelope `{ ok, data: T, error? }` | `{ ok, data?: T, error? }` | write+access or read+toast (need `ok` + `error`) |
| `safeApiCallData<T>` | `<T>` is the INNER payload | `T \| null` | best-effort read (silent null on error) |

`loadProfileSyncStatus` is a best-effort read, so `safeApiCallData<T>` is the correct fit. The pre-migration `try/catch + apiFetch + as AgentProfile[]` form was a 9-line manual re-implementation of `safeApiCallData<T> → T | null` — the migration collapses 9 lines to 6 by using the existing helper.

### 5. What was rejected (out of scope)

- **The 5 `apiFetch + JSON.stringify` mutation sites in the operations pages** (agents/page.tsx:287, personalities/page.tsx:163, skills/page.tsx:192 + 244, tools/page.tsx implicit in `runSyncAction` calls). Migrating to `safeApiCall` would change the failure mode from throw to return-ok-error, requiring per-site `if (!ok) { toastError(...); }` rewrites. Per session 133's "What was rejected" section: *"Out of scope for AT LEAST identical results — defer to a future List 3 session that explicitly opts in to safeApiCall mutations."* Same verdict applies this session.
- **The 2 envelope-typed reads in `ModelSyncButtons.tsx:183` and `ModelEditor.tsx:118`.** Both are CORRECT shapes per the session 133 safe-api-call envelope-typed myth lesson (`apiFetch<{ data?: { ... } }>` is the canonical strict-fail + outer-try-catch form; the `data.data?.X` indirection is correct, not double-envelope). Leave them as-is.
- **The 1 `loadToolsets` strict-fail + try/catch + toastError site (line 93).** This is the canonical strict-fail + user-visible-error pattern — `safeApiCallData` would silently swallow the error and leave the user wondering why the toolsets panel is empty. Out of scope for byte-equivalence — the migration to `safeApiCall` (which auto-checks `ok: false` and toasts) is the right path but changes the failure mode. Defer.
- **The `PersonalitiesPage.loadPersonalities` Promise.all + outer try/catch (lines 258-274).** Two parallel best-effort reads where the OUTER try/catch surfaces a single "Failed to load personalities" error if EITHER fails. The migration would need a "all-or-null" variant of `safeApiCallData` (Promise.allSettled + null-on-any-fail) — out of scope for a single-site migration. Defer to a future session that adopts a `safeApiCallAll` helper if one is needed across the codebase.
- **The `ModelSyncButtons.fetchDiffs` silent-catch (lines 180-214).** The catch path synthesizes a fallback diff list (not just a "set null" — it generates `[{ id: "model-config", ...}, { id: "model-env", ...}]`). This is NOT a candidate for `safeApiCallData` because the catch does real work (synthesizes a list), not just swallows. The cleanest refactor here is to extract a `buildFallbackDiffs(direction, provider, modelIdString)` pure helper, but that's a new-helper extraction, not a helper adoption — defer to a future List 3 session.

### 6. Files

- `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 1 import extension (`safeApiCallData` added) + 1 site migration (`loadProfileSyncStatus` useCallback) + JSDoc block explaining the byte-equivalence audit. Net: +35 / -9 = +26 (mostly the JSDoc).
- `tests/unit/load-profile-sync-status-safe-api-call-data.test.ts` (NEW) — 7 it() blocks + 1 helper function (`extractFunctionBody`) + JSDoc history block. Net: +188 lines.

### 7. Verification

- **All 1952 unit tests pass** (270 suites, +1 new suite with +7 new tests in `load-profile-sync-status-safe-api-call-data.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean (after dropping the unused `code` variable that was the original line-comment-stripping shim; the function-body tests don't need it)
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session 51 lesson** — covered in §2 above; 5 paths enumerated, all match
- **The session 165 grep-H "intentional graceful-degradation" verdict is preserved** — the bare-catch shape is replaced with the `safeApiCallData → null` shape, but the OBSERVABLE behavior (silently set state to null, no toast) is byte-equivalent

### 8. Why this isn't a "MINED CLEAN" violation

Session 165's verdict was scoped to the "operations-page refactor level" (helper extractions, duplication, dead code). The 7-grep scan it codified is calibrated for that refactor class. Session 166's find is a HELPER ADOPTION — using an existing helper on a candidate site that was missed by the helper-EXTRACTION scan. This is a different refactor class.

The umbrella skill's mission charter says: *"We want to have a clean, performant, well-designed codebase ... ensuring our current implementation is effective."* Helper adoption is squarely in scope — the migration reduces 9 lines of imperative boilerplate to 6 lines of declarative helper call, with byte-equivalent observable behavior and zero external behavior change.

If a future session finds another helper-adoption candidate, the right move is to extend the audit protocol to include a "bare-catch + apiFetch + cast" scan (the inverse of grep H from session 165). For this session, the single-site find is documented as a "named OOS surface" so future audits know to look here.

### 9. Named OOS surface for future audits

**"bare-catch + apiFetch + as Cast" pattern in operations pages** — the session 165 grep H (`catch {`) hit pinned `tools/page.tsx:54` as "intentional graceful-degradation" without examining the SURROUNDING function body for helper-adoption opportunities. The 7-grep scan's grep H only checks for the presence of a bare-catch, not the presence of `apiFetch + as Cast` inside the same function body. **Future sessions extending the audit protocol should add grep I: `catch\s*\{\s*set\w+\(null\)` (or similar) combined with grep J: `as\s+\w+\[\]` inside the same function body, scoped to operations pages.**

### 10. "Next session should:"

1. **Run the new grep I + grep J pair on the other 3 operations pages** (agents, personalities, skills) to find more helper-adoption candidates. The 7-grep scan's grep H was the seed; grep I + J is the extension.
2. **Consider adding a `safeApiCallAll<T[]>` helper** if more than one Promise.all + outer-try-catch site emerges across the operations pages (currently 1 site: `PersonalitiesPage.loadPersonalities` lines 258-274). The helper would be a 5-line wrapper that returns the first-failure-or-all-success tuple, similar to the `safeApiCallData` envelope-unwrap pattern.
3. **The `ModelSyncButtons.fetchDiffs` fallback-diffs synthesis (lines 180-214)** is a different refactor class (pure-helper extraction, not helper adoption) — defer to a future List 3 session that wants to add a `buildFallbackDiffs` pure helper.
4. **Mission charter consideration** (per session 149 P-7) — the "mined clean at the operations-page refactor level" verdict is now confirmed, but the "mined clean at the helper-adoption level" verdict is NOT. The next session that wants to advance the mission should adopt the helper-adoption lens (greps I + J) as a new refactor class. If that lens also returns zero after a full pass across all 4 lists, the mission is at a true steady state.

## Session 165 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — Mode I fresh-audit returns zero + session 164 carryover closure

### 1. Session 164 carryover closure (commit `594d829`)

Session 164 (2026-06-12 earlier) created the `parseEnvFile` helper at `src/lib/env-file.ts` to consolidate the duplicated env-file parser from `src/lib/hermes-config-sync.ts` and `src/lib/hermes-import.ts`. The work was uncommitted at session start (per `git status` showing 2 modified files + 1 untracked helper + 1 untracked test file). Session 165 picked it up unchanged and committed:

| # | File | LOC | Notes |
|---|------|-----|-------|
| 1 | `src/lib/env-file.ts` | NEW (~70 lines) | `parseEnvFile` helper + `ENV_LINE_RE` regex, exported for `serializeEnvFile` in `@/lib/hermes-config-sync.ts` (which preserves blank lines and `#` comments verbatim — it needs the regex to identify keyval lines, not the full parser) |
| 2 | `src/lib/hermes-config-sync.ts` | +10 / -14 = -4 net | Drop private `parseEnvFile` + `ENV_LINE_RE`; import from `@/lib/env-file`. The `serializeEnvFile` sibling preserves comments, so it reaches for the shared `ENV_LINE_RE` to identify keyval lines. |

| 4 | `tests/unit/env-file.test.ts` | NEW (24 tests) | 24 cases covering the 4 parser branches (keyval / blank / comment / invalid) + CRLF + leading whitespace + value-with-`=` + duplicate keys + 6 `ENV_LINE_RE` cases |

**Why this was a real refactor (not a feel-productive rename):** the two pre-refactor sites had the same regex and same skip rules independently; the consolidation guarantees they stay in lockstep if a future .env dialect (e.g. multi-line values, export-prefixed declarations) needs support. The `serializeEnvFile` sibling in `hermes-config-sync.ts` shares the regex (not the parser) because it preserves comments and blank lines verbatim — the parser would lose that context.

### 3. The 1 hit on `safeApiCall<{ data?: { config: FallbackConfig } }>` is NOT a bug

The `useModelsPage.ts:433` site at the `persistFallbackConfigNow` function is the **canonical envelope-typed form** per session 141 + 162. The site has a 4-line JSDoc explaining: *"The route returns `{ data: { config: ... } }` (envelope). `safeApiCall<T>` does NOT unwrap — `data` is the full body — so the type is the envelope shape and the inner config is read via `res?.data?.config` (two indirections)."*

The umbrella skill has codified (per session 138 safe-api-call-envelope-unwrap-myth + session 141 revert): **`safeApiCall<T>` does NOT unwrap envelopes** — `safeApiCallData<T>` is the helper that DOES unwrap. The `useModelsPage.ts:413` site was migrated to `safeApiCallData` in session 133. The `:433` site is the **write+access** pattern (PUT then read the saved config from the response), which has a different failure mode (throw on write error, then read the response body). Migrating to `safeApiCallData` would change the failure mode — out of scope for byte-equivalence. **Verdict: PINNED, no action.**

### 4. The 1 bare-catch hit is INTENTIONAL graceful-degradation

The `tools/page.tsx:54` bare-catch is in the `loadProfileSyncStatus` useCallback, which sets `setProfileSyncStatus(null)` on error and intentionally suppresses the toast. Per session 163 P-7: *"`catch { setProfileSyncStatus(null); }` is deliberate — the drift banner is suppressed and no toast is shown by design."* **Verdict: PINNED, no action.**

### 2. Fresh List 3 audit — `toastError` migration in `viewSkill` (commit `defba64`)

The List 3 surface is mostly clean after sessions 91/95/96/100/103 (setErrorFromCaught/toastError sweeps), 127 (serverErrorFromCatch 6-site migration), 132 (ok() factory 3-site catch), 133 (safeApiCallData in useModelsPage), 142 (toastError 5 catch blocks in operations pages), 159 (logs page stale setX(messageFromError) closure), and 161 (case-insensitive substring search helper).

The ONE remaining silent-swallow catch in List 3 is the `viewSkill` function in `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx:259-272` (the Skills page 'view' expansion). The pre-fix code was:

```ts
setExpandedSkill(skill.name);
try {
  const d = await apiFetch(skillApiUrl(skill.name));
  setSkillContent(d.data?.content || "// No content");
} catch {
  setSkillContent("// Failed to load content");
}
```

The bare `catch {` swallows the actual error (e.g. "API returned invalid JSON (HTTP 500)", network failure, auth error) and shows the user a static placeholder. This is the canonical "feature is not working" anti-pattern that session 142 explicitly calls out as fixable.

**Fix:** bind `err` and add `toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to load skill content")`. The inline placeholder is preserved (so the expand panel still renders a meaningful fallback if the user dismisses the toast and the panel is still open); the toast is the user-visible acknowledgement. Pattern mirrors the sibling `openSkillEditor` (line 232-234) which already used `toastError` for the same catch path.

**New test:** `tests/unit/toast-error-skills-viewskill-source-pattern.test.ts` (7 tests). The file has 3 other `catch (e)`/`toastError` sites (`loadSkills`, `toggleSkill`, `openSkillEditor`) so a file-wide test would false-positive. The new test extracts the `viewSkill` function body via brace-counting and pins 3 things: (a) catch is bound (not bare), (b) `toastError(showToast, err, ...)` is called, (c) the EXACT pre-fix bare-catch fingerprint is absent. Pre-flight recipe in the test header documents the rg/sed commands future sessions should run before any revert.

**Negative-case verification:** `git stash` the page fix, re-run the new test → 2/7 fail (the bound-catch and toastError checks). The test is a real regression guard, not a permanent-pass.

**Behaviour change:** documented per session 142 P-142-1's 'feature is not working' exception: users now see the real fetch error message instead of the static "// Failed to load content" placeholder. Strict improvement (always-broken → works), not a semantic shift.

### Next session should

- `useModelsPage.ts:handleRefresh:290-319` — uses `safeApiCallData<...>` inside a `try/catch` that can never throw (the helper swallows errors internally). The catch block is dead code; the toast never fires for API errors. This is a pre-existing issue flagged in the carryover from previous List 3 audits. Fix shape: switch to `safeApiCall<...>` (the result-shaped variant) and check `if (!result.ok)` instead of try/catch. Behaviour change (silent → loud) requires explicit user direction.
- `personalities/page.tsx:loadPersonalities:258-274` — uses `apiFetch` + `Promise.all` + try/catch + envelope unwrapping. Could be migrated to `safeApiCallData<...>` but this would lose the 'fail loud' toast behavior. NOT a byte-equivalent refactor; would need explicit user direction.

### 2. Fresh List 3 audit — `filterByCaseInsensitiveSubstring` helper + 2-site migration (commit `e6f9337`)

The fresh List 3 audit found 2 sites with the inline case-insensitive substring-search pattern:

- `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx:56-63` — `filterBySearch(skills, search)` filters by `name` OR `description`
- `src/app/operations/personalities/page.tsx:331-340` — inline `useMemo` filter that searches `name` OR `prompt`, with an extra `|| p.name === activePersonality` "active personality is always visible" condition

Both migrated to a new generic helper in `src/lib/list-search.ts`:

```ts
filterByCaseInsensitiveSubstring(
  items,
  search,
  [(p) => p.name, (p) => p.prompt],   // field getters
  (p) => p.name === activePersonality, // optional alwaysMatch predicate
);
```

The helper centralises:
- The per-search `search.toLowerCase()` call (4 duplications removed across 2 sites)
- The empty-search pass-through (1 site returned `items` directly, 1 site returned `sortedPersonalities` — both now go through the helper's `[...items]` defensive-copy path)
- The `|| alwaysMatch` short-circuit (used by the personalities site for the active-personality UX)

**New tests:**
- `tests/unit/list-search.test.ts` (12 tests) — pins the helper's contract (empty/whitespace search, multi-field OR, case-insensitivity, null/undefined safety, alwaysMatch predicate)
- `tests/unit/list-search-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (7 tests) — sister source-pattern test scanning all 6 List 3 pages for the inline `.toLowerCase().includes(` pattern, asserting 0 matches. Self-tested by reverting the skills page: 1 of 7 tests fails as expected.

### 2. The migration

**Before (logs/page.tsx:92):**
```ts
} catch (err) {
  setActionMessage(messageFromError(err, "Delete failed (network error)"));
}
```

**After (logs/page.tsx:92):**
```ts
} catch (err) {
  setErrorFromCaught(setActionMessage, err, "Delete failed (network error)");
}
```

**Byte-equivalence:** `setErrorFromCaught(setX, err, fallback)` is `setX(messageFromError(err, fallback))` by construction (see `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:218-224`). Same string produced, same setter call, same fallback discipline, same exception type.

## Session 155 — List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings) — fix `/api/config` deep-merge bug, derive `modelDefaultsSchema` from `TASK_TYPES`, share `toModelEditorRecord`

Random pick: **List 4** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 4. Last touched in List 4: session 124 (serverErrorFromCatch in fs/git/branches). Carryover from session 154's "mined clean is version-dependent" lesson — session 154 flagged List 1 as mined clean, but the dead-`as RequestInit`-cast class was new work the per-list tests didn't catch. Session 155 applies the same lesson to List 4: a hand-listed zod schema + a local helper + a 6-month-silent shallow-merge bug.

### 1. Real bug: shallow-merge in `/api/config` PUT (6+ months silent)

**Symptom.** Any PUT to `/api/config` that touched a nested object (e.g. `personalities.default`) silently wiped every sibling key on that object. The route did `config[section] = { ...current, ...values }` — spread of the top level only. Any `personalities`-style field with 12 sub-keys (custom, archived, etc.) lost 11 of them on save.

**Why the bug went unfixed for 6+ months.** `tests/unit/config-deep-merge.test.ts` was a "regression test" named "Bug regression: config PUT shallow merge" — but the test **inlined a verbatim copy of the desired `deepMerge` function** inside the `describe` block and ran assertions on the inlined copy. The test passed green because it tested its own inlined function, not the actual route. The pre-flight #26 lesson ("test inlines a copy of the desired helper" anti-pattern) is the generalisation of this failure mode.

**Fix (3 files).**
- `src/lib/deep-merge.ts` (NEW) — extracted helper, 6-line pure function with JSDoc contract covering arrays, nested objects, null, and Date instances.
- `src/app/api/config/route.ts` — replaced shallow spread with `deepMerge(current, values)`. Added a comment explaining why shallow merge was the regression.
- `tests/unit/config-deep-merge.test.ts` (REWRITTEN) — now imports the real helper from `@/lib/deep-merge`. Added a "PATCH preserves sibling keys on a nested object (real PUT shape)" test that mirrors the actual PUT contract.
- `tests/unit/config-put-deep-merge.test.ts` (NEW) — exercises the actual `/api/config` PUT via `await import("@/app/api/config/route")` + mocked `fs` + mocked `hermes-agent-runtime` + mocked `db()`. Asserts on the post-merge `writeFileSync` call (indexed from the END to avoid the backup-then-write call-order trap).

**Pre-flight #26 — "test inlines the helper" anti-pattern (codified).** A regression test for a bug in code path X must EXERCISE code path X. If the test inlines a copy of the helper instead of importing the real one, the test is documenting intent in a sandbox, not locking the production code. Detection: `rg -nU --multiline "describe\\([^)]+\\)\\s*\\{[^}]*function\\s+\\w+\\(" tests/unit/` — a function declaration inside a describe block that's a near-duplicate of a known helper is the tell. Cross-reference: if the test file declares `function deepMerge(...)` AND does NOT import `deepMerge` from `@/lib/deep-merge`, it's self-referential.

**Pre-flight — `writeFileSync` mock call-order trap.** When the route does backup-then-write (the Control Hub pattern is `backupFile(paths.config, paths.backups)` followed by `writeFileSync(paths.config, content, "utf-8")`), the test's `mockWriteFileSync.mock.calls[0]` is the **backup copy**, NOT the post-merge write. Assert on `mock.calls.at(-1)?.[1]` or filter by path. The session 155 new test uses `at(-1)` — the trap was a known failure mode from the canonical pattern.

### 2. Hand-listed 12 task booleans → derived from `TASK_TYPES`

`src/lib/api-schemas.ts` had a 12-line hand-listed zod shape for the `defaults` body field on the Models POST/PUT routes:

```ts
const modelDefaultsSchema = z.object({
  agent: z.boolean().optional(),
  hindsight: z.boolean().optional(),
  // ... 10 more hand-listed lines
}).strict();
```

This is the "drift from canonical source" anti-pattern. Adding a new task slot would have required 3 edits: `TASK_TYPES` in `hermes-providers.ts`, the `migration 006_models_credentials.sql` columns, and the `modelDefaultsSchema` shape. Two-of-three is a bug-in-waiting.

**Fix.** Extracted `buildModelDefaultsSchema()` that iterates `TASK_TYPES` and produces the same shape. The schema is now derived from the canonical source; the `TASK_TYPES` array in `hermes-providers.ts` is the single source of truth. `.strict()` preserved so unknown task-slot keys still 400.

**Lesson.** Any zod/JSON-schema field whose keys mirror a canonical list should derive from that list, not hand-list. The "Rule of Three" doesn't apply because the cost of hand-listing is the drift risk, not the verbosity.

### 3. Promote `toModelEditorRecord` to shared `types.ts` export

`src/components/models/ModelsTableSection.tsx` had a local `function toModelEditorRecord(m: ApiModel): ModelEditorRecord` that projected an API row down to the subset the editor form edits. The function is a textbook "shared helper that lives next to the first consumer" — exactly the kind of inline declaration that should be promoted before the 2nd consumer appears.

**Fix.** Moved to `src/components/models/types.ts` as a named export, deleted the local copy. `ModelsTableSection.tsx` now imports the shared one. Adding a future consumer (a context-menu "Edit" action, a bulk-edit, an admin row) reuses the same projection — and a new editable field is a one-line change in `ModelEditor.tsx` + one line in `types.ts`, not 3 sites.

- `npm run build` — clean.
- `npm test` — 254 suites / 1836 tests passing (0 failures). +2 new tests in the new `config-put-deep-merge.test.ts` file.
- Manual sanity: the 12-task-type boolean shape is byte-equivalent — the existing tests that POST `{defaults: {agent: true, hindsight: false, ...}}` still pass with the derived schema.
