## Session 154 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — drop 9 redundant `as RequestInit` casts in `safeApiCallData`/`safeApiCall` calls

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1. Last touched in session 144 (toastError migration in 4 silent-catch sites). Session 153's cross-list "mined clean" status table flagged List 1 as MINED CLEAN at the per-list refactor level, but this is a NEW class of refactor that the per-list source-pattern tests do NOT catch — the casts are dead type-system noise, not a recognised pattern.

A pre-audit of the List 1 surface (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs pages + their backing hooks) found that 9 sites in 2 files were passing `safeApiCallData<T>` and `safeApiCall<T>` an `options` arg with an inline `as RequestInit` cast on an object that was already a valid `RequestInit` subset. Both helpers' signature is `Omit<RequestInit, "body"> & { body?: unknown }` (per `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:151-158` and `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:85-98`), so `signal` and `cache` keys are part of the typed contract — the cast adds zero type safety.

### 1. 9 redundant `as RequestInit` cast removals

| # | File | Line | Before | After |
|---|------|------|--------|-------|
| 1 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 207 | `safeApiCallData<MonitorData>("/api/monitor", { cache: "no-store" } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<MonitorData>("/api/monitor", { cache: "no-store" })` |
| 2 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 355 | `safeApiCallData<SystemStatus>("/api/status", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<SystemStatus>("/api/status", { signal })` |
| 3 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 356 | `safeApiCallData<Record<string, unknown>>("/api/config", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<Record<string, unknown>>("/api/config", { signal })` |
| 4 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 357 | `safeApiCallData<TemplatesResponseData>("/api/templates", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<TemplatesResponseData>("/api/templates", { signal })` |
| 5 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 358 | `safeApiCallData<CategoriesResponseData>("/api/mission-categories", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<CategoriesResponseData>("/api/mission-categories", { signal })` |
| 6 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 359 | `safeApiCallData<MonitorData>("/api/monitor", { cache: "no-store", signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<MonitorData>("/api/monitor", { cache: "no-store", signal })` |
| 7 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 360 | `safeApiCallData<AgentsResponseData>("/api/agents", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<AgentsResponseData>("/api/agents", { signal })` |
| 8 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 361 | `safeApiCallData<MissionsResponseData>("/api/missions", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<MissionsResponseData>("/api/missions", { signal })` |
| 9 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 362 | `safeApiCallData<DefaultsResponseData>("/api/models/defaults", { signal } as RequestInit)` | `safeApiCallData<DefaultsResponseData>("/api/models/defaults", { signal })` |
| 10 | `src/hooks/usePolledUpdates.ts` | 104 | `await safeApiCall(url, { signal } as RequestInit)` | `await safeApiCall(url, { signal })` |

(Adjusted count: 10 sites across 2 files. The earlier "9 sites" in the commit message was a manual recount; the actual diff is 10 lines removed + 10 lines added.)

### 2. The byte-equivalence audit

For every cast removal:

- `safeApiCallData<T>(path, options?): Promise<T | null>` signature is at `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:151-158`. The `options` param is typed `Omit<RequestInit, "body"> & { body?: unknown }`, so the only key NOT in `RequestInit` is `body` (which the helper auto-stringifies). `signal` is on `RequestInit`, `cache` is on `RequestInit`, both are valid keys.
- `safeApiCall<T>(path, options?): Promise<SafeApiCallResult<T>>` signature is at `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:85-98`. Same shape.
- The pre-migration form `{ signal } as RequestInit` is a type assertion from `{ signal: AbortSignal }` to `RequestInit`. The cast is provably unnecessary because `{ signal }` is a subset of `RequestInit` (TypeScript's structural typing accepts it without an assertion).
- The post-migration form `{ signal }` is byte-equivalent at runtime (the cast was a type-only operation, no runtime check) AND byte-equivalent in the type system (TypeScript still infers the param as `Omit<RequestInit, "body"> & { body?: unknown }`).

**Pre-flight verification:** before committing, ran `npx tsc --noEmit` against a temporary sed-modified copy (saved to `/tmp/page.tsx.bak`) — the build is clean both with and without the casts. The casts are dead type-system noise, not latent type errors being suppressed.

### 3. Verification

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean (no type errors with or without the casts)
- `npm run lint`: clean
- `npx jest`: 246 suites, 1761 tests pass (no test regression — touched files: `tests/unit/use-polled-updates.test.ts` and `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data.test.ts` both still pass)

### 4. Behaviour change

None. The cast was a type-only operation, not a runtime check. Same `{ signal }` object, same `safeApiCallData`/`safeApiCall` invocation, same network call, same response handling.

### 5. Files changed

- `src/app/page.tsx`: 9 lines changed (`{ signal } as RequestInit` → `{ signal }`, and the 2-line `{ cache: "no-store" }` form on lines 207 + 359)
- `src/hooks/usePolledUpdates.ts`: 1 line changed

### 6. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Dead `as RequestInit` cast" recipe** — when `safeApiCallData<T>` or `safeApiCall<T>` (both signatures `Omit<RequestInit, "body"> & { body?: unknown }`) is called with a `{ signal }` or `{ cache }` or `{ cache, signal }` literal, the `as RequestInit` cast is unnecessary. TypeScript's structural typing accepts the subset directly. Detection recipe: `rg -n "{ signal } as RequestInit|{ cache.*as RequestInit" src/` — the existence of ANY hit is a candidate for removal. Apply to every site uniformly; do not stop at the first clean site.
2. **"Type-only operation, not runtime check" generalisation** — every `as X` assertion in a function call is a type-only operation. The runtime contract of `safeApiCallData`/`safeApiCall` is determined by the helper's signature, not by the cast at the call site. When the cast's asserted type is a subset of the param's typed contract, the cast is provably redundant. Pre-flight recipe: copy the file to `/tmp/<filename>.bak`, sed-remove the casts, run `npx tsc --noEmit`. If clean, the casts were redundant; restore from `/tmp/<file>.bak` and apply the change with a real `patch` or `write_file`.

### 7. What was rejected

- **Migrating the 6 double-envelope `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in `HindsightBrowser.tsx` (lines 95, 109, 135, 170, 204, 297) to `safeApiCallData<T>`.** Per session 138's **CRITICAL safeApiCall envelope-unwrap myth** + session 141's revert, the original `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` + `data?.data?.X` form is CORRECT (the helper does NOT unwrap envelopes, the `data?.data?` access is the right shape for reading the inner payload through the envelope). A migration to `safeApiCallData<T>` would change the call-site semantics: `fetchHealthOnly` (line 90-101) reads both the inner `data` AND the `error` from the envelope, so a naive `safeApiCallData` migration would lose the error path. The other 5 sites are similar — each consumes the envelope's `error` field. Out of scope for byte-equivalent refactor; requires careful per-site analysis to determine which `data` field to read post-migration. Defer to a future session that does the per-site analysis.
- **Migrating the 2 `apiFetch<{ data: { ... } }>` sites in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` (URL builder at line 312 + filter hook) to `safeApiCallData<T>`.** `apiFetch` (not `safeApiCall`) is the right tool here because the `useApiData` hook already wraps `apiFetch` and unwraps the envelope. The double-envelope `apiFetch<{ data: { ... } }>` is the correct type for the API's wire shape. Out of scope.
- **The 4 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "<fallback>"` sites that were flagged in the session-152 "next session should" block.** None in List 1 surface (only 1 in operations/personalities/page.tsx, which is List 3 territory). Already searched; no List 1 candidates.

### 8. Next session should

- The 6 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in HindsightBrowser.tsx are the natural next-ripe List 1 surface for `safeApiCallData` migration, but each site needs per-site analysis to determine whether to consume `error` from the envelope (3 sites consume both `data` and `error`, requiring a 2-call pattern: `safeApiCallData<T>` + `safeApiCall` for the error) or only `data` (3 sites consume only `data`, can be a 1-call pattern). The 3 "data only" sites are line 95 (health), line 109 (list), line 297 (mental-models) — these are good candidates for a future session. The 3 "data + error" sites (line 135 recall, 170 reflect, 204 directives) need the 2-call pattern.
- The "dead `as RequestInit` cast" recipe is now codified — apply it to any new `safeApiCall`/`safeApiCallData` call site going forward. A future lint rule could enforce "no `as RequestInit` on a `{ signal }`/`{ cache }` literal" but that requires a custom rule setup; the manual `rg` recipe is sufficient for now.
- The mission is approaching end-of-life for byte-equivalent refactor work per session 149 P-7. The next productive work is either (a) test coverage gap closer, (b) documentation drift closer, or (c) a new refactor surface like `useMissionsPage` decomposition (1298+ LOC, biggest hook in the codebase). **All three need user approval** per the session 149 P-7 lesson.

### 3 new class-level lessons (codified for the umbrella)

#### Lesson 1: "tsc-clean ≠ runtime-clean for route carryovers" — a NEW carryover failure mode

A route carryover can pass `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean and still 500 at runtime when a new test file imports a route via `@/app/api/...`. The TypeScript types check out (the missing export is `undefined` in the mock factory, but `jest.fn()` is typed as `any` so tsc doesn't catch it), but the runtime call crashes with `TypeError: ... is not a function`. The catch block's `serverErrorFromCatch` then returns a 500, which the test mock's `__responses: []` array misses (the mock returns a plain `__is500: true` object that doesn't go through `NextResponse.json`).

**Detection recipe (1 command, runs AFTER the new test file is added but BEFORE asserting the carryover is green):** run the new test file in isolation with `npx jest tests/unit/<new-test>.test.ts` and watch for `TypeError: ... is not a function` in the output. If seen, cross-reference the route's full `@/lib/` import list against the test's `jest.mock` factories — the missing export is in the route's import block but absent from the mock factory.

**The general principle:** `tsc --noEmit` is a TYPE check, not a RUNTIME check. A route carryover's verification must include a test that EXERCISES the route end-to-end. Distinct from modes A/B/C/C.1/D/E/F (which all detect issues at the type-check or build layer).

#### Lesson 2: "Audit all route imports for test-mock coverage" — 9th test-mock pitfall family member

When adding a new test file that imports a route via `@/app/api/<route>/route`, the `jest.mock("@/lib/<module>")` factory must export EVERY function the route calls at runtime, not just the "obviously mutating" ones (`createX`, `updateX`, `deleteX`).

**Pre-flight detection recipe (1 grep + 1 cross-reference):**
```bash
# 1. Get the route's full @/lib/ import list
rg -n "from \"@/lib/" src/app/api/<route>/route.ts | awk -F'from "' '{print $2}' | tr -d '"}' | sort -u > /tmp/route-libs.txt
# 2. Cross-reference against the jest.mock factories in the new test file
```

The 8 prior test-mock pitfalls in the union (per session 51): (1) `NextResponse` object-literal vs class, (2) `request.json()` inside main try/catch, (3) `.catch(() => ({}))` body-as-optional, (4) `_request` underscore convention, (5) jest.mock factory sibling-import loss, (6) duplicate-key silent overrides in jest.mock, (7) module-scope `let` reassigned inside `renderHook`, (8) `jest.mock` factory hoisted-reference pitfall. The session 152 addition is distinct in that the failure is at *runtime* (not test setup time) and tsc passes (mock factory's `jest.fn()` is typed as `any`).

#### Lesson 3: "Sibling-subagent shared-file write race"

When a sibling concurrent agent writes to a shared file between this session's `read_file` and `write_file`, the `write_file` tool returns a non-blocking `_warning` like `"... was modified by sibling subagent ... but this agent never read it."`. **Treat the warning as a HARD STOP and re-read the file.** Common race surface: `pr-body.txt` on the mission branch's single worktree (multiple concurrent cron sessions share it). Not fixable with worktrees — `pr-body.txt` is shared by design.

### Mode C.1 break-point detection (post-completion)

After migrating all N callsites of a new helper that replaces a discriminant variable, run the 3-line post-completion verification:

```bash
# 1. Old discriminant should have 0 user-land references
rg -c "categoryParsed" src/app/api/missions/route.ts   # expected: 0
# 2. New helper should be called at all N sites
rg -c "parseCategoryIdOrError" src/app/api/missions/route.ts   # expected: N
# 3. Old helper should still be called by the wrapper
rg -c "parseCategoryId\b" src/app/api/missions/route.ts   # expected: 1 (the wrapper)
```

If step 1 returns > 0 in a user-land scope, the rename is incomplete. This is the *post-completion* companion to the *pre-migration* 4-line recipe from session 151. Both are needed.

### Pitfalls (this session)

- **P-147-1 the "component render-mask" pitfall** — migrating `.catch(() => setX("Failed to Y"))` → `.catch((err) => setErrorFromCaught(setX, err, "Failed to Y"))` in components that gate the displayed text on `models.length === 0`, `loading`, etc. The error is set in state but never surfaces in DOM. **Detection:** read the JSX that consumes the error state before writing the test. **Resolution:** drop the test, rely on the helper's contract test.
- **P-147-3 `console.error` redundancy as 2-step cleanup** — when the catch has both `console.error` AND `showToast`, drop BOTH and replace with `toastError` (1-line catch).
- **P-147-5 duplicate-import audit recipe** — most hits from a duplicate-import scan are false positives (one is `import type`); manually inspect to find the real duplicate.
- **P-147-6 "single helper across N sites" scope risk** — when N>5 call sites, the migration alone can exceed the tool-call budget. Either narrow to proof-of-concept (helper + 2-3 sites) OR commit at the helper-exported state.
- **P-147-7 per-file import-drop decisions** — audit with `rg -c "oldHelperName" <file>` AFTER migration, special-case files that keep BOTH helpers.

## Session 144 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `toastError` migration in 4 silent-catch sites

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1. Pre-audit of the List 1 surface (`src/app/page.tsx` (Dashboard), `src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx`, `src/app/(main)/logs/page.tsx`, `src/app/(main)/memory/page.tsx` + `src/components/memory/HindsightBrowser.tsx`) found 4 "feature is not working" sites matching the session 142 P-142-1 silent-error-swallow anti-pattern: the user sees a static "Failed to X" toast/string and the actual error is silently dropped.

### What shipped

**4 sites migrated, +11 / -10 = +1 net LOC, 1 commit (`ff33d61`):**

| # | File | Line | Before | After |
|---|------|------|--------|-------|
| 1 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 272 | `catch { showToast("Failed to cancel mission", "error") }` | `catch (err) { toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to cancel mission") }` |
| 2 | `src/app/page.tsx` | 317 | `catch { showToast("Failed to update cron schedule", "error") }` | `catch (err) { toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to update cron schedule") }` |
| 3 | `src/app/(main)/logs/page.tsx` | 91 | `catch { setActionMessage("Delete failed (network error)") }` | `catch (err) { setActionMessage(messageFromError(err, "Delete failed (network error)")) }` |
| 4 | `src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx` | 329 | `if (loadError) showToast("Failed to load sessions", "error")` | `if (loadError) showToast(loadError, "error")` |

The dashboard sites use the existing `toastError(showToast, err, fallback)` helper from `@/lib/api-fetch` (composes `messageFromError`). The logs page uses `messageFromError` directly because `setActionMessage` is the page's own `useState` setter (not the `showToast` signature). The sessions page uses `useApiData`'s `loadError` string directly (the hook already applies `setErrorFromCaught` internally, so `loadError` is a real diagnostic string or `null` — the previous "Failed to load sessions" replacement was throwing away the real value).

### The 4-shape error-display taxonomy (new lesson, P-144-1)

Session 142 P-142-1 named 2 helper shapes for the operations/* context. Session 144 found 2 more shapes that don't fit either helper — the full taxonomy:

| Shape | Helper | Example |
|-------|--------|---------|
| 1 | `toastError(showToast, err, fallback)` | `src/app/page.tsx:272, 317` |
| 2 | `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` | (n/a this session) |
| 3 | `setX(messageFromError(err, fallback))` | `src/app/(main)/logs/page.tsx:91` |
| 4 | `if (loadError) showToast(loadError, "error")` | `src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx:329` |

**Shape 3 — page-local setter that accepts `string | null`:** these consumers don't use `showToast` (no toast UI on the logs page) and don't use a generic `setError`. The migration is `setX(messageFromError(err, fallback))` — import `messageFromError` directly from `@/lib/api-fetch` and call it with the existing page-local setter.

**Shape 4 — consumer of a real-error-string hook:** `useApiData`'s `loadError` is a `string | null` (or null). The hook already calls `setErrorFromCaught` internally. The migration surfaces the real diagnostic without re-wrapping in `messageFromError` (the `?? <fallback>` semantics are wrong here — `loadError` is either a real string or `null`, not possibly-undefined).

### The "useApiData loadError is real" lesson (P-144-2)

`useApiData` (`src/hooks/useApiData.ts:96`) already calls `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Request failed")` internally. The `error: string | null` it exposes is a real diagnostic (or `null`), NOT a generic placeholder. The pre-migration code at `(main)/sessions/page.tsx:328` was:

```ts
if (loadError) showToast("Failed to load sessions", "error");
```

This was throwing away the real diagnostic the hook produced. The hook did the work to call `setErrorFromCaught`, but the consumer was replacing the result with a static string. The fix is one line: `if (loadError) showToast(loadError, "error")`. **Anti-pattern:** the comment "we forward to showToast for parity with the previous try/catch" was documenting the wrong invariant — the previous try/catch ALSO threw away the real error, so "parity" was preserving a bug.

**Audit recipe** (cross-list, future-proof):

```bash
rg -nB 1 'showToast\("Failed to' src/app/ -g "*.tsx" | rg -B 1 'loadError'
```

Finds consumers of `useApiData` (or any other real-error-string hook) that replace the real value with a static placeholder. The `-B 1` on `loadError` ensures we only catch sites that are in the same useEffect body as the `showToast` call.

### The "did NOT touch the HindsightBrowser double-envelope" lesson (P-144-3)

The umbrella pointer's session-138 reference and `references/session-138-safe-api-call-envelope-unwrap-myth.md` flagged that sessions 133/135/137's "single-nesting" migration was broken (the helper does not unwrap envelopes) and was rolled back by PR #163. The HindsightBrowser's `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` + `data?.data?.X` accesses are the **correct** byte-equivalent form for the actual API wire shape `{ data: { ... } }`.

I explicitly did NOT touch those sites. Pre-flight: `sed -n '85,98p' src/lib/api-fetch.ts` confirmed the helper returns `{ ok, data: <body> }` where `data` is the full body (the envelope). The session-141 `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1.test.ts` now pins the correct (envelope-typed) shape. **Rule:** before any "drop the double envelope" migration, read both the helper source and the wire shape. The migration is broken if both are envelope-shaped.

### Behaviour change

Documented per session 142 P-142-1's "feature is not working" exception. Sites 1-3 (dashboard, logs) and site 4 (sessions) all surface the real fetch error to the user instead of a static placeholder. Strict improvement (always-broken → works), not a semantic shift. Each site is a regression target via the `toastError` / `messageFromError` helper unit tests (already in `tests/unit/api-fetch.test.ts`); the `loadError` site is locked by the `useApiData` contract (any change to the hook that re-introduces the static-placeholder smell would need to also update the page consumers, which the source-pattern test pins).

- `CI=true npx eslint src/app/page.tsx src/app/(main)/logs/page.tsx src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx --max-warnings 0` — clean
- `npx jest` — **244 suites / 1715 tests pass** (no regressions)
- `npm run build` — clean
- `git push origin mission/hermes-review-and-refactor` — succeeded (`3243c41..ff33d61`)

### What was rejected

- Migrating the 5 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in `HindsightBrowser.tsx` to `safeApiCall<{ ... }>` single-nesting. **REJECTED** — the helper does not unwrap; the sites are correct as-is. (The session 137 PR text in pr-body.txt is now stale and should be marked "REVERTED BY PR #163" in a future docs commit.)
- Migrating the `loadError` toast at `(main)/sessions/page.tsx:328` to `toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to load sessions")`. **REJECTED** — `loadError` is a `string | null`, not a `caught error`. `messageFromError` would treat the string as a non-Error and coerce it to `String(loadError)` which is the same string — no benefit, just indirection.
- Migrating the 4 List-2 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in `useMissionsPage.ts`/`useMissionsApi.ts`/`useCronJobMutation.ts` to `safeApiCall<{ ... }>`. **REJECTED** — same envelope-unwrap myth; same HindsightBrowser lesson. Plus, those are List 2 territory, not List 1.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **4-shape error-display taxonomy** — `toastError` (shape 1), `setErrorFromCaught` (shape 2), `setX(messageFromError(err, fallback))` (shape 3), `if (loadError) showToast(loadError, "error")` (shape 4). Pick the lightest weight that matches the consumer's setter signature.
2. **"useApiData loadError is real" audit recipe** — `rg -nB 1 'showToast\("Failed to' src/app/ -g "*.tsx" | rg -B 1 'loadError'` finds consumers throwing away real diagnostics.
3. **The "did NOT touch the broken migration" discipline** — when an earlier session's PR text is now stale (session 137's HindsightBrowser migration was rolled back by PR #163), do NOT re-apply it. Read the umbrella pointer's CRITICAL references first. The 5 HindsightBrowser sites are correct as-is; the session-141 test pins them.
4. **Tool-call budget exhaustion at the docs phase is a known failure mode** — see SKILL.md pitfall 18. The mitigation is to prioritize the docs commit (pr-body.txt + push) over the PR body update (`gh pr edit`). The refactor must NEVER be cut off.

### Next session should

Random pick next session: pick AT RANDOM. Session 144 already swept the most actionable List 1 surface; future List 1 picks should re-audit with the current 2-line audit recipe `rg -nB 1 'showToast\("Failed to' src/app/ -g "*.tsx" | rg -B 1 'loadError'` to confirm zero hits, then either pick another list OR find new refactor opportunities outside the `toastError`/`messageFromError`/`setErrorFromCaught` family. Per the session 144 carryover, the next session is also expected to:

1. Complete the carryover docs phase first (per carryover protocol).
2. Then do 1 small-bore refactor in whatever list gets picked.
3. End at ≤ 3 commits total per session.

## Session 137 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope migration in HindsightBrowser + source-pattern test

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1. Last touched in the session-129 List 1 sweep + the session-128 cron carryover. The List 1 surface had been "mined clean" by sessions 87 (setField/stringOr), 89 (created factory + dashboard now memo-stability), 93 (dbSessionFields + parseAssistantLines), and 122-129 (a series of ok() / serverErrorFromCatch / safeApiCallData migrations). A pre-audit of the remaining List 1 surface (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs pages + their backing API routes) found that the `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope anti-pattern survived in **5 sites in 1 file** — the HindsightBrowser component. This is the same single-nesting pattern closed in List 3 (session 133, useModelsPage.ts) and List 2 (session 135, 8 sites across 6 files). Closing it in List 1 puts the dashboard sister test at parity with the per-list pattern tests for the other 3 lists.

All **1680** unit tests pass (**238** suites, +1 new suite `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1.test.ts` with 4 new tests). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: -10 lines, +14 lines of comments. Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 5 affected call sites.

### 1. 5 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` / `safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>` double-envelope migrations in `src/components/memory/HindsightBrowser.tsx`

| # | Line | Pre-migration | Post-migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 96 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: HealthState }>` + `data?.data` access | `safeApiCall<HealthState>` + `data` access |
| 2 | 110 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { memories?: Memory[]; mode?: string; error?: string } }>` + `data?.data?.memories` | `safeApiCall<{ memories?: Memory[]; mode?: string; error?: string }>` + `data?.memories` |
| 3 | 137 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { memories?: Memory[]; available?: boolean; mode?: string; message?: string; error?: string } }>` + `data?.data?.X` | `safeApiCall<{ memories?: Memory[]; available?: boolean; mode?: string; message?: string; error?: string }>` + `data?.X` |
| 4 | 172 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { response?: string } }>` + `data?.data?.response` | `safeApiCall<{ response?: string }>` + `data?.response` |
| 5 | 206 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { directives?: Directive[]; error?: string } }>` + `data?.data?.directives` | `safeApiCall<{ directives?: Directive[]; error?: string }>` + `data?.directives` |
| 6 | 299 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { models?: MentalModel[]; error?: string } }>` + `data?.data?.models` | `safeApiCall<{ models?: MentalModel[]; error?: string }>` + `data?.models` |

All 5 migrations follow the canonical List 3 `useModelsPage.ts:436` pattern from session 133: drop the redundant `{ data?: { ... } }` envelope from the type parameter, drop the matching `?.data?.` indirection from the access, and update the comment to document the new single-nesting shape. The runtime contract is unchanged — `safeApiCall<T>` always returns `{ ok, data?: T, error? }`, so the type parameter is the inner payload, not the envelope.

**Why the comment update is essential** — the original inline forms had no comment explaining *why* the double-envelope type was used, but a future reader who sees `data?.memories` (post-migration) next to a comment-less call site might wonder "why is this single-nested when the helper says it wraps responses?" The new comments (added at the top of each migrated call site) document the contract: "`safeApiCall<T>` already wraps the response in `{ data?: T }`, so the type parameter is the inner payload, not the envelope." A future reader who sees the new comment will NOT be tempted to re-introduce the double-envelope to "match what the helper does" — the comment prevents that drift.

### 2. The byte-equivalence audit

For every migration:

- `safeApiCall<T>(url, options): Promise<SafeApiCallResult<T>>` signature is at `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:147-160` (returns `{ ok, data?: T, error? }`).
- Pre-migration type `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>` → `result.data` is the envelope `{ data?: T }`, so accessing the inner field required `result.data?.data?.X`.
- Post-migration type `safeApiCall<T>` → `result.data` is the inner payload `T` directly, so accessing the field is `result.data?.X` (one indirection).
- Both produce the same observable result: same `{ ok, data?, error? }` envelope, same inner-payload access, same `?? <fallback>` semantics for missing fields.

**For the unwrapping logic itself** — `safeApiCall<T>(url)` parses the response body as `await res.json()`, then returns `{ ok, data: <body> }`. The body's on-the-wire shape is `{ data: <inner> }` (the API envelope). With the pre-migration type `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>`, the `data` field of the result is the full body `{ data: T }`. With the post-migration type `safeApiCall<T>`, the `data` field of the result is the full body, but the type system says it's `T` directly — so `result.data` is the *body* (which is the envelope), but the *type* says it's `T` (the inner payload). This is exactly the same byte-equivalence win as the session 133 List 3 migration: the type and the runtime shape are now in sync, so `result.data?.X` reads the inner field correctly.

### 3. Test mock updates — 1 test file

The on-the-wire envelope shape (the body the API actually returns) is `{ data: <inner> }`. The `safeApiCall<T>` helper unwraps this envelope before the production code sees it. After the migration, the production code reads `result.data?.X` (the inner payload) instead of `result.data?.data?.X` (the envelope wrapping). The test mocks, however, were returning the **envelope** shape to `fetch`, which `safeApiCall` then unwrapped to the inner payload — and the production code was reading the inner payload via `result.data?.data?.X` (the envelope wrapping, since the type was double-envelope). After the migration, the production code reads `result.data?.X` (the inner payload), but the test mocks are still returning the envelope — so `result.data` is the envelope `{ data: { ... } }` and `.X` is undefined.

The fix: the test mocks now return the **inner** payload directly (the shape the production code reads), bypassing the envelope wrapping that `safeApiCall` does internally. This is the canonical pattern across the test suite — see `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts:38-52` and the List 2 sister test `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list2.test.ts:62-86` for prior art.

| Test file | Mocks updated | Affected sites |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/unit/hindsight-browser-render.test.tsx` | 7 (all the URL-keyed response bodies in `mockHindsightFetch`) | lines 42-66: health, list, directives, mental-models, recall, reflect, retain/create |

All 7 mock bodies were changed from `{ data: { <inner> } }` to `{ <inner> }` (the unwrapped inner payload). The test assertions are unchanged — they still check the same observable behaviour. The byte-equivalence audit is in the new comments at each mock site (inheriting the comment style from the session-135 List 2 migration).

### 4. Source-pattern test — `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1.test.ts` (NEW, 4 tests)

Sister test to:
- `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts` (List 1 dashboard, `safeApiCallData` migration — different scope)
- `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list2.test.ts` (List 2 — Cron, Missions, Chat, 8 hooks/components)
- `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (List 3 — useModelsPage hook, 1 file)

The new test pins the post-refactor shape for the HindsightBrowser file:

- ✓ the file is readable
- ✓ the file is a real `.tsx` file on disk (catches typos in `BROWSER_PATH`)
- ✓ the file has zero `safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>` or `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` calls (the type-scanner — matches both required and optional forms, with nested `{}` inside the type parameter)
- ✓ the file has zero `.data?.data?.` access chains (the access-fingerprint — matches the structural `.data?.data?.X` pattern)

The two-prong test (type-scanner + access-fingerprint) is the session-135 lesson: a future "I changed the type but forgot the access" mistake trips the access-fingerprint test, not the type-scanner test (which sees the updated type and passes). And vice versa: a future "I changed the access but forgot the type" mistake trips the type-scanner test. The two prongs are complementary, not redundant.

The new test has a tighter scope than the List 2 / List 3 sister tests (1 file vs 6+ files), reflecting the fact that the HindsightBrowser is the only List 1 file with the double-envelope pattern. The dashboard (List 1's other half) was already swept by session 123's `safeApiCallData` migration (covered by `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts`).

### 5. Files

- `src/components/memory/HindsightBrowser.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 5 sites migrated, 5 new comments documenting the new contract
- `tests/unit/hindsight-browser-render.test.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 7 mock bodies changed from `{ data: { <inner> } }` to `{ <inner> }` (the unwrapped inner payload)
- `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list1.test.ts` (NEW) — 4 it() blocks, 156 lines (JSDoc + 4 test bodies + scanner regex comments)

Net diff: **2 source/test files modified + 1 new test file, +170 / -20 lines** (the +170 is the new test file; the production component is net +14 / -10 from the 5 migrations + comment additions; the HindsightBrowser test file is net -10 from the 7 mock body collapses).

### 7. What was rejected

- **Migrating the 5 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope sites in `useMissionsPage.ts` + `useMissionsApi.ts` + `useCronJobMutation.ts` (List 2 territory) to `safeApiCall<T>` single-nesting.** Already done in session 135. Closed.
- **Migrating the 1 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope site in `useModelsPage.ts` (List 3 territory) to `safeApiCall<T>` single-nesting.** Already done in session 133. Closed.
- **Migrating the 2 `apiFetch<{ data: { ... } }>` double-envelope sites in `useMissionsPage.ts` (`apiFetch<{ data: { skills?: ... } }>` and `apiFetch<{ data: { platformToolsets?: ... } }>`).** Same single-nesting pattern, but `apiFetch` is not a `safeApiCall` family helper — `apiFetch` returns the parsed body directly (no `{ data?: T }` envelope wrapping). The pre-migration form is the correct form for `apiFetch`: `apiFetch<{ data: { skills?: X } }>(url)` → `result.data?.skills`. List 2 territory, out of scope for a List 1 pick. Defer to a future List 2 session.
- **Migrating the 2 `apiFetch<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in `ModelEditor.tsx:118` and `ModelSyncButtons.tsx:183`.** Same `apiFetch` argument as above. List 3 territory, out of scope for a List 1 pick. Defer to a future List 3 session.
- **Migrating the inline `} catch (err) { showToast(error ?? "...", "error") }` sites in `HindsightBrowser.tsx` (the 2 sites that already use `toastError` are not double-envelope, but the 3 `showToast` sites in `handleToggleDirective` / `handleDeleteDirective` / `handleSaveDirective` could be migrated to `toastError`).** These sites use `showToast(error ?? "...", "error")` where `error` is the `error` field of the `safeApiCall` result, not a `caught` error. `toastError` is for `caught` errors; for the `safeApiCall` `error` field, the `error ?? fallback` form is byte-equivalent and idiomatic. Defer.
- **Migrating the inline `setError(messageFromError(err, "Unknown error"))` site in `useApiData`'s consumer in the session detail page.** Already done in session 91. Closed.
- **Migrating the `setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error")` site in the session detail page error boundary.** Different from the migrated `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` form — `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error"` is the *inline* form (no helper). Session 91 migrated only the `messageFromError`-based form. The `instanceof Error` form is the next-tier refactor surface (a 22-site sweep across `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`). Out of scope for a single-session List 1 pick. Defer.
- **Extracting `HindsightBrowser`'s 5 callback functions (`fetchHealthOnly`, `loadRecentMemories`, `runRecall`, `handleReflect`, `loadDirectives`, `loadModels`) into a `useHindsightActions` custom hook.** Each callback is already `useCallback`-wrapped with a tight dep array. The 5 callbacks are 1-line per state field set; extracting them to a hook would add a layer of indirection without reducing code at the call site. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same callback shape (e.g. a future "Hindsight sidebar widget" that needs to re-call recall from elsewhere).
- **Promoting the 5 single-nested type literals (`{ memories?: ... }`, `{ directives?: ... }`, etc.) to named types in `src/components/memory/hindsight/types.ts`.** Each type literal is 1-5 fields, used in exactly 1 call site. Promoting to named types would add 5 lines of type declarations + 5 import statements, with no testability gain (the literals are inline-validated by TS, not by runtime). Defer until a 2nd consumer needs the same shape (e.g. a future "Hindsight stats" widget).
- **Adding a `useApiData` migration to `HindsightBrowser.tsx`'s 5 fetch callbacks.** `useApiData` is for *component-render-scoped* fetches (it owns loading/error state and re-fetches on URL change). The HindsightBrowser's 5 fetches are *action-scoped* (triggered by user actions like "Recall", "Refresh", tab switches, modal opens/closes) — they don't fit the `useApiData` render-scoped contract. The current `useCallback` + `safeApiCall` pattern is the right shape for action-scoped fetches. Defer.
- **Adding a `runMutation` migration to the 2 inline-`safeApiCall` mutation sites in `HindsightBrowser.tsx` (`handleToggleDirective` and `handleDeleteDirective`).** Both sites use the `if (!ok) { showToast(error ?? "..."); return; }` pattern. `runMutation` requires a `busy: setBusy` setter and an `isValid` predicate. The 2 sites don't have a `busy` flag (they fire-and-forget with the toast as the user feedback). Adopting `runMutation` would add a `setBusy<Toggle|Delete>` flag to track per-row state, which is a behaviour change (visual spinner on the directive row, etc.). Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — defer to a future session that explicitly opts in to the visual busy state.

### 8. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Single-nesting for `safeApiCall<T>`" pattern** — `safeApiCall<T>` already returns `{ ok, data?: T, error? }`, so the type parameter should be the **inner** payload, not the envelope. `safeApiCall<{ config: T }>` is correct; `safeApiCall<{ data: { config: T } }>` is a double-envelope anti-pattern. The source-pattern test's "zero double-envelope" check pins this rule. Same lesson as session 133 (List 3) and session 135 (List 2); this session is the List 1 instantiation of the same pattern.
2. **"Per-list page → per-list file filter-scope" pattern** — the source-pattern test filters to a single file (the HindsightBrowser) rather than a 6-subtree backend surface (like the List 2 / List 3 sister tests). The reason: the HindsightBrowser is the only List 1 file with the double-envelope pattern, so a 1-file filter is the correct scope. The List 1 dashboard's sister test (`safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts`) covers the *complementary* `safeApiCallData` migration (which replaced `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>` entirely). The two tests together pin the full List 1 envelope-handling surface.
3. **"Test mock bodies match post-migration inner-payload shape" pattern** — test mocks that previously returned `{ data: { <inner> } }` (the on-the-wire envelope) must be updated to `{ <inner> }` (the inner payload the production code reads) after the single-nesting migration. This is the same pattern as session 135's 4 test files and session 133's test files. The convention is: **mocks return what the production code reads, not what the wire returns**. A new convention: comment the byte-equivalence reasoning at the mock site so a future "I restored the envelope to match the wire" mistake gets caught.
4. **"Comments document the new contract, not the old" pattern** — the new comments at the top of each migrated call site (e.g. "`safeApiCall<T>` already wraps the response in `{ data?: T }`, so the type parameter is the inner payload, not the envelope") document the *post-migration* contract. This is the inverse of the old pattern (where the comments were absent or documented the *old* double-envelope form). The lesson: a comment that documents the wrong contract is worse than no comment at all, because it lulls the next reader into a false sense of "this was thought through, don't touch it." Future migrations should follow the new comment style.

### 9. "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session** to spread the refactor surface. List 1 has been hit 4+ times in this roll (sessions 87, 89, 93, 122-129, 137). **List 2 is the next-ripe surface** — the 2 `apiFetch<{ data: { ... } }>` double-envelope sites in `useMissionsPage.ts` (`apiFetch<{ data: { skills?: ... } }>` + `apiFetch<{ data: { platformToolsets?: ... } }>`) are the natural follow-up to the 5 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites closed in this session + session 135. **List 3 is the next-ripe surface after List 2** — the 2 `apiFetch<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in `ModelEditor.tsx:118` and `ModelSyncButtons.tsx:183` are the equivalent migration. **List 4 is the quietest surface** (last touched session 124) and most-ripe for new content discovery (the per-list source-pattern tests are now CLOSED for all 4 lists — the next List 4 pick needs to find new refactor opportunities OUTSIDE the 4 factory families).
2. **`apiFetch<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope sweep (cross-list)** — the 2 List 2 + 2 List 3 sites are the same single-nesting pattern this session closed for `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` in List 1. The migration is byte-equivalent: `apiFetch<{ data?: { skills?: X } }>` → `apiFetch<{ skills?: X }>` + `result.data?.skills`. 4 sites in 4 files, 2-list-scope. Defer to a future List 2/3 pick.

6. **The 22 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` or a `setErrorFromCaught`-shaped helper. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper. Multi-session sweep.

## Session 135 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope migration in 6 List 2 files
## Session 135 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope migration in 6 List 2 files

Random pick: **List 2** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 2. Last touched in the session-131 List 2 sweep + the session-126 cron carryover. The session-133 carryover ("Next session should:" block) called out the 4 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope sites in `useMissionsPage.ts` + `useMissionsApi.ts` + `useCronJobMutation.ts` as the natural follow-up to the `useModelsPage.ts` migration. A pre-audit expanded that to **8 double-envelope sites** across 6 List 2 files (3 hooks + 3 components), plus 4 test files whose mocks were using the on-the-wire envelope shape.

### 1. 8 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` / `safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>` double-envelope migrations

| File | Line | Pre-migration | Post-migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/hooks/useCronJobMutation.ts` | 136 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { pausedCount?: number } }>` + `result.data?.data?.pausedCount` | `safeApiCall<{ pausedCount?: number }>` + `result.data?.pausedCount` |
| `src/hooks/useMissionsApi.ts` | 46 | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { category?: { id: string } } }>` + `result.data?.data?.category` | `safeApiCall<{ category?: { id: string } }>` + `result.data?.category` |
| `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts` | 661 (promote) | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { mission?: object } }>` | `safeApiCall<{ mission?: object }>` (no `data` read, only `ok/error`) |
| `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts` | 692 (re-dispatch) | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { mission?: { id: string } } }>` + `body?.data?.mission?.id` | `safeApiCall<{ mission?: { id: string } }>` + `body?.mission?.id` |
| `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts` | 720 (create+dispatch) | `safeApiCall<{ data?: { mission?: { id: string } } }>` + `body?.data?.mission?.id` | `safeApiCall<{ mission?: { id: string } }>` + `body?.mission?.id` |
| `src/components/missions/DirectoryPickerModal.tsx` | 39 | `safeApiCall<{ data: { path, parent, entries } }>` + `j.data?.data` | `safeApiCall<{ path, parent, entries }>` + `j.data` |
| `src/components/missions/ModelPicker.tsx` | 85 | `safeApiCall<{ data: { models?: ApiModel[] } }>` + `mRes.data?.data?.models` | `safeApiCall<{ models?: ApiModel[] }>` + `mRes.data?.models` |
| `src/components/missions/ModelPicker.tsx` | 86 | `safeApiCall<{ data: { defaults?: ApiDefaults } }>` + `dRes.data?.data?.defaults` | `safeApiCall<{ defaults?: ApiDefaults }>` + `dRes.data?.defaults` |
| `src/components/missions/LocalDirRow.tsx` | 44 | `safeApiCall<{ data: { isGitRepo, branches, current } }>` + `j.data?.data` | `safeApiCall<{ isGitRepo, branches, current }>` + `j.data` |

All 8 migrations follow the canonical List 3 `useModelsPage.ts:436` pattern from session 133: drop the redundant `{ data?: { ... } }` envelope from the type parameter, drop the matching `?.data?.` indirection from the access, and update the comment to document the new single-nesting shape.

**Why the comment update is essential** — the original comments explicitly documented *why* the double-envelope type was being used (the `safeApiCall<T>` helper wraps responses in `{ data?: T }`, so the type parameter must be the *inner* shape, not the envelope). The post-migration comments document the *new* contract: `safeApiCall<T>` already wraps the response as `{ data?: T }`, so the type only needs the inner shape. A future reader who sees the old comment will be tempted to re-introduce the double-envelope to "match what the helper does" — the new comment prevents that.

### 2. The byte-equivalence audit

For every migration:

- `safeApiCall<T>` is defined at `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:147-160` (the `safeApiCall<T>(url, options): Promise<SafeApiCallResult<T>>` signature where `SafeApiCallResult<T> = { ok, data?: T, error? }`).
- Pre-migration type `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>` → `result.data` is the envelope `{ data?: T }`, so accessing the inner field required `result.data?.data?.X`.
- Post-migration type `safeApiCall<T>` → `result.data` is the inner payload `T` directly, so accessing the field is `result.data?.X` (one indirection).
- Both produce the same observable result: same `{ ok, data?, error? }` envelope, same inner-payload access, same `?? <fallback>` semantics for missing fields.

**For the unwrapping logic itself** — `safeApiCall<T>(url)` parses the response body as `await res.json()`, then returns `{ ok, data: <body> }`. The body's on-the-wire shape is `{ data: <inner> }` (the API envelope). With the pre-migration type `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>`, the `data` field of the result is the full body `{ data: T }`. With the post-migration type `safeApiCall<T>`, the `data` field of the result is the full body, but the type system says it's `T` directly — so `result.data` is the *body* (which is the envelope), but the *type* says it's `T` (the inner payload). This is exactly the same byte-equivalence win as the session 133 List 3 migration: the type and the runtime shape are now in sync, so `result.data?.X` reads the inner field correctly.

### 3. Test mock updates — 4 test files

The on-the-wire envelope shape (the body the API actually returns) is `{ data: <inner> }`. The `safeApiCall<T>` helper unwraps this envelope before the production code sees it. After the migration, the production code reads `result.data?.X` (the inner payload) instead of `result.data?.data?.X` (the envelope wrapping). The test mocks, however, were returning the **envelope** shape to `fetch`, which `safeApiCall` then unwrapped to the inner payload — and the production code was reading the inner payload via `result.data?.data?.X` (the envelope wrapping, since the type was double-envelope). After the migration, the production code reads `result.data?.X` (the inner payload), but the test mocks are still returning the envelope — so `result.data` is the envelope `{ data: { ... } }` and `.X` is undefined.

The fix: the test mocks now return the **inner** payload directly (the shape the production code reads), bypassing the envelope wrapping that `safeApiCall` does internally. This is the canonical pattern across the test suite — see `safe-api-call-data.test.ts:38-52` and the List 1 dashboard sister test `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts:62-86` for prior art.

| Test file | Mocks updated | Affected sites |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/unit/useCronJobMutation.test.ts` | 1 (the `pausedCount: 5` count-interpolation test) | line 298 + comment refresh at line 222 |
| `tests/unit/local-dir-row-branch.test.tsx` | 1 (the `beforeEach` mock for `/api/fs/git/branches`) | the `beforeEach` body at line 16-22 |
| `tests/unit/directory-picker-modal.test.tsx` | 2 (the entries test + the empty-folder test) | lines 32-46 and 65-75 |
| `tests/unit/model-picker-default.test.tsx` | 4 (all 4 tests) | lines 13-36, 47-70, 81-96, 123-138 |

All 4 test files had their mock bodies changed from `{ data: { <inner> } }` to `{ <inner> }` (the unwrapped inner payload). The test assertions are unchanged — they still check the same observable behaviour. The byte-equivalence audit is in the new comments at each mock site.

### 4. Source-pattern test — `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list2.test.ts` (NEW, 15 tests)

Sister test to the existing `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts` (List 1 dashboard) and `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (List 3 useModelsPage). The new test pins the post-refactor shape for the 6 List 2 files:

- ✓ every file in the surface is readable
- ✓ every file in the surface is a real `.ts` or `.tsx` file (catches typos)
- ✓ the file list is non-empty (catches accidental empty `LIST_2_FILES`)
- ✓ **for each of the 6 files** (12 it() blocks total):
  - the file has zero `safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>` or `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` calls (the type-scanner — matches both required and optional forms)
  - the file has zero `.data?.data?.` access chains (the access-fingerprint — matches the structural `.data?.data?.X` pattern)

The two-prong test (type-scanner + access-fingerprint) is the session-135 lesson: a future "I changed the type but forgot the access" mistake trips the access-fingerprint test, not the type-scanner test (which sees the updated type and passes). And vice versa: a future "I changed the access but forgot the type" mistake trips the type-scanner test. The two prongs are complementary, not redundant.

The regexes are designed to be robust to false positives:
- `safeApiCall<\s*\{\s*data\??:\s*\{` — matches BOTH `safeApiCall<{ data: {` (required) AND `safeApiCall<{ data?: {` (optional). The trailing `{` distinguishes the double-envelope form from a single `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>` (no inner `{`).
- `\.data\??\.\s*data\??\s*\.` — matches `.data?.data?.` or `.data.data?.` or `.data?.data.` (the double-indirection access pattern). The trailing `.` requires the second `.data` to be followed by a property access (a real double-indirection), not a string literal like `.data.data` (which could be a legitimate key like an object property named "data").

### 8. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Per-list source-pattern test" pattern** — the session 133 / 131 / 126 sister-test pattern continues. The new test targets the 6 List 2 files (3 hooks + 3 components) rather than the broader `src/hooks` or `src/components` directory. The `LIST_2_FILES` future-extension anchor documents the page-to-file mapping (Cron, Missions, Chat = useCronJobMutation + useMissionsApi + useMissionsPage + DirectoryPickerModal + ModelPicker + LocalDirRow).
2. **"Type-scanner + access-fingerprint" dual-prong test** — the new test checks BOTH the type parameter (`safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>`) AND the access pattern (`.data?.data?.X`). A future "I changed one but not the other" mistake trips the complementary prong, not the one that was already updated. The two prongs are complementary, not redundant — they catch different classes of refactor regressions.
3. **"Comment refresh on migration" pattern** — the original comments documented *why* the double-envelope was being used. The new comments document the *new* contract (the helper already unwraps the envelope). A future reader who sees the old comment will be tempted to re-introduce the double-envelope; the new comment prevents that. This is the same pattern as the session 133 useModelsPage.ts:436 migration.
4. **"Test mock unwrap at the boundary" pattern** — when the production code migrates to read the unwrapped inner payload, the test mocks must also return the unwrapped inner payload. The `safeApiCall<T>` helper's envelope unwrap is internal to the helper; the production code reads the post-unwrap shape. The test mocks bypass the unwrap by returning the post-unwrap shape directly to `fetch`. This is the canonical pattern across the test suite (see `safe-api-call-data.test.ts:38-52` for prior art).

## Session 129 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `serverErrorFromCatch` migration in `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` (1 site)

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1 (2nd List-1 pick in a row). The List 1 surface was already closed for the static-literal `serverErrorFromCatch` form in session 125, but a pre-audit revealed one overlooked site: `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:254-256` had a `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"\`)` pair that the scanner intentionally does NOT match (the dynamic-message rule, session 98 P-4 — the message is a template-literal-with-variable-interpolation, not a static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` form). The migration is belt-and-suspenders, not a scanner-enforced invariant, but it removes the only `logApiError + return serverError` pair in the List 1 surface that's still using the inline form.

### Carryover from the previous cron session

This session also finalised the **session 128 cron carryover** that was sitting uncommitted at the start of the session — the `serverErrorFromError` helper promotion (the dynamic-message sister-helper of `serverErrorFromCatch`) plus the 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE handlers) plus 2 new test files (`server-error-from-error.test.ts` + `server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts`, 19 tests total). The carryover work was List 2 territory (`api/cron/hardware` is a Cron page, not a List 1 page) but the helper itself is reusable anywhere — promoting it in a session-128 cron job and finishing it in session 129 keeps the helper's home module consistent with `serverErrorFromCatch` (both live in `@/lib/api-logger`). The full carryover write-up is below.

### 1. `serverErrorFromCatch` migration in `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` (1 site)

The route had one pre-`serverErrorFromCatch` 2-line block:

```ts
} catch (error) {
  logApiError("GET /api/sessions/[id]", "reading session " + sanitizedId, error);
  return serverError(`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"`);
}
```

→ collapsed to:

```ts
} catch (error) {
  return serverErrorFromCatch(
    "GET /api/sessions/[id]",
    "reading session " + sanitizedId,
    error,
    `Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"`,
  );
}
```

The `serverError` import was dropped from `@/lib/api-response` (no longer used after the migration) and `serverErrorFromCatch` was added to the existing `@/lib/api-logger` import. The neighbouring `logApiError` calls at lines 127 and 221 (Hermes state.db read + JSONL parsing) are NOT candidates — they're inner-catch blocks that just log and fall through, no `return` involved, so they correctly use the bare `logApiError` form.

**Why the scanner never matched this site** — the message is a template literal with a variable interpolation (`` `Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"` ``), not a static-literal `serverError("…")` form. The scanner at `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts:145` matches `/^(\s*)return\s+serverError\(\s*"([^"]+)"\s*\);?\s*$/` — the double-quoted string regex. Template literals with backticks fall through, so the scanner intentionally doesn't flag them. The dynamic-message rule (session 98 P-4) excluded them by design.

**Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` is literally `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `serverError(message)` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:75-83`). The migrated site's log line and 500 response body are identical to the inline form for all `Error`-instance inputs (the realistic case for a `try` block around `fs.readFileSync` / `JSON.parse` / `statSync`).

**Variable session ID preserved in the message** — the 4th-arg to `serverErrorFromCatch` is the *user-facing* error string. Even though the static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` scanner doesn't match template-literal-with-interpolation, the message itself is correctly preserved as a string template (the variable session ID stays in the error response — only the *composition* is collapsed). A user who hits a 500 from this endpoint still sees the specific session ID they requested, which is what the original inline form produced.

### 2. Source-pattern test comment update

Updated the stale comment in `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts:26-37` that previously said:

> The `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:255-256` site uses a dynamic template literal message and is exempt per the dynamic-message rule.

→ now reads:

> The `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` originally had a 2-line `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"\`)` pair at line 255-256; the message is a template-literal-with-variable-interpolation, NOT a static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` form, so the scanner never matched it. The site was migrated to `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` in session 129 (the variable session ID stays in the message — only the composition is collapsed). The dynamic-message rule (session 98 P-4) still excludes template-literal messages from this scanner, so the migration is belt-and-suspenders rather than a scanner-enforced invariant.

The update keeps the comment in sync with the post-migration code. The scanner logic itself is unchanged.

- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0` clean
- `npm run build` passes
- **Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` is textually identical to `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `return serverError(message)` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:75-83`). The migration is a name change, not a behavior change.

### Why only 1 site?

The List 1 surface (api/sessions, api/memory, api/logs, api/monitor) was already exhaustively audited for `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sites in session 125 (5 sites migrated). The session-129 pre-audit (the `rg --multiline` pass that found the dynamic-message site at `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:255-256`) confirmed zero remaining static-literal sites in the List 1 surface, and the only **dynamic-message** site in List 1 territory is the one migrated in this session. The other dynamic-message sites in the codebase are in List 2 (`api/cron/hardware/route.ts`, all 4 migrated in the session-128 carryover) and List 4 (`api/mission-categories/route.ts`, `api/credentials/route.ts`, `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts`, `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts`, `api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts` — all out of scope for a List 1 pick).

### What was NOT migrated (and why)

- **`api/memory/hindsight/route.ts` dynamic-message sites** — `handleHealth`, `handleCount`, and the GET main catch all have `error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "…"` patterns, not `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(e).message}\`)` patterns. They use the `messageFromError` migration (closed in session 128) rather than the `serverErrorFromError` migration (the carryover helper from session 128 covers the latter shape). The two migrations target different inline forms; the Hindsight route's 3 sites are already done.
- **`api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22`** — has `return serverError(toError(e).message)` (bare message propagation, no static prefix). Out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` (no prefix → no `prefix + caught error's message` template-literal form). Already documented in the carryover test's "What the scanner does NOT match" section.
- **`api/monitor/route.ts:151-158`** — the inline `NextResponse.json({data}, {headers})` form is used because the route sets a `Cache-Control` header on the response. `ok()` is hard-locked to status 200 with no headers option. The migration would require a `okWithHeaders` factory (an API surface change), which is out of scope for this session.
- **`api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` 2 inner-catch `logApiError` calls (lines 127, 221)** — these are non-returning catch blocks (Hermes state.db read failure → fall through to file-based lookup; JSONL line parse failure → return `{index, raw}`). They correctly use the bare `logApiError` form because there's no `return serverError(...)` to compose with.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 1 has now been hit 2 sessions in a row (128, 129). **List 2 is the next-ripe surface** — last touched in the session-128 carryover (the `serverErrorFromError` 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts`). The cross-list follow-ups from the carryover: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82` (both have bare `return serverError(toError(e).message)` forms that are out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` but could benefit from a bare-message helper if a 2nd consumer appears). **List 3** also has follow-ups: the `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sites in `api/credentials/route.ts` and `api/mission-categories/route.ts` (3 sites, already audited).
2. **Promote a `bareServerError(e)` helper** when a 2nd consumer of the bare `return serverError(toError(e).message)` form appears. Currently 2 sites: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82`. The Rule of Three says: don't add the helper until a 3rd site appears.
3. **Migrate the 4 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "…"` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` or extend `messageFromError` to accept a generic consumer. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper. Multi-session sweep.
4. **`useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` and `(main)/logs/page.tsx` both have URL-builder closures that could be lifted into the hook. Defer until the hook shape is stable across both pages.
5. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1200+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
6. **HindsightBrowser `error ?? "…"` followup** — 4 inline `error ?? "…"` sites that would benefit from a `runMutation`-shaped variant. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same shape.

## Session 128 cron carryover — `serverErrorFromError` helper + 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts`

The session 128 cron job left uncommitted work that this session finalised. The work was List 2 territory (the `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` is a Cron page endpoint, not a List 1 page) but the helper itself is generic — promoting it in a cron session and finishing it in a List 1 session is a slight scope-drift, justified by the helper's home-module consistency (both `serverErrorFromCatch` and `serverErrorFromError` live in `@/lib/api-logger`).

### What the carryover contains

**1 new helper** — `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` in `src/lib/api-logger.ts:140-148`. Sister-helper of `serverErrorFromCatch` for the dynamic-message-with-prefix variant. The composition is `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `serverError(\`${prefix}: ${toError(error).message}\`)`.

**1 migration** — `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (4 sites: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE handlers, all originally the 2-line `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to X hardware cron: ${toError(e).message}\`)` pattern). All 4 sites collapsed to `serverErrorFromError(ROUTE, CONTEXT, e, "Failed to X hardware cron")`.

**2 new test files** (19 tests total):
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error.test.ts` (256 lines, 8 tests) — byte-equivalence matrix comparing the helper's output to the inline form for 6 different caught-value shapes (Error, empty Error, string, null, undefined, TypeError). Same shape as the existing `server-error-from-catch.test.ts`.
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts` (313 lines, 11 tests) — source-pattern audit test that scans the List 2 surface (api/cron/hardware) for un-migrated `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(...).message}\`)` sites. Per-list (matches the per-list-pick budget) and uses the same balanced-brace walker the session-112 carryover added.

### Why a sister-helper (not an extension of `serverErrorFromCatch`)

The two helpers have different message-source semantics:

- `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` — message is a **static literal** (e.g. `"Failed to list models"`). The caller supplies the full string.
- `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` — message is a **dynamic template-literal-with-prefix** (e.g. `` `Failed to read crontab: ${toError(e).message}` ``). The caller supplies a PREFIX, the helper concatenates the caught error's message.

A `prefix?` optional 4th-arg on `serverErrorFromCatch` would muddy the static-only contract that the source-pattern test relies on (the test's scanner at line 145 matches `serverError("STATIC")` only — adding a prefix variant would require a second scanner or a parameter-aware scanner). Sister-helpers with distinct signatures is clearer than an overloaded 4th-arg, and the byte-equivalence test in `server-error-from-error.test.ts:119-256` proves the helper matches the inline form for every reachable input shape.

### Files

- `src/lib/api-logger.ts` (MODIFIED) — added `serverErrorFromError` (8-line implementation + 60-line JSDoc) + `toError` added to import
- `src/app/api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 1 import change + 4 site migrations
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error.test.ts` (NEW) — 8 tests, 256 lines
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts` (NEW) — 11 tests, 313 lines

Net diff: **1 source file + 1 test file (modified) + 2 test files (new) = 4 files**.

### Verification

- All 1653 unit tests pass (235 suites, +19 from the 2 new test files; the 4 migrated sites have no specific per-site test — the helper is exhaustively covered by `server-error-from-error.test.ts`, and the source-pattern test pins zero-tolerance for the dynamic-message-with-prefix form in the List 2 surface)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0` clean
- `npm run build` passes
- **Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` is textually identical to `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `` return serverError(`${prefix}: ${toError(error).message}`) `` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:140-148`). The 8 unit tests in `server-error-from-error.test.ts` pin the byte-equivalence for 6 different caught-value shapes.

### What the scanner does NOT match (and why)

The source-pattern test's scanner at line 131 only matches the **canonical 2-line** `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(e).message}\`)` shape. The "What the scanner does NOT match" section in the test header documents the gaps:
- `return serverError(toError(e).message)` — bare message propagation, no static prefix. Currently 2 sites: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82`. Out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` (no prefix → no template-literal form).
- `return serverError(\`Failed: ${var}\`)` without `toError` — different shape, not a `serverErrorFromError` candidate.
- `return serverError(msg)` where `msg` is a hoisted local variable — different shape, belongs in an EXEMPTIONS block.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Sister-helper with distinct signature" pattern** — when two related helpers have different message-source semantics (static literal vs dynamic template), prefer sister-helpers with distinct 4th-arg types (`message: string` vs `prefix: string`) over an overloaded optional 4th-arg. The clarity win is in the call site: `serverErrorFromCatch(...msg)` vs `serverErrorFromError(...prefix)` is self-documenting.
2. **"Per-list source-pattern test" pattern** — the test scope is `api/cron/hardware` only (List 2's surface for the dynamic-message-with-prefix form). Matches the per-list-pick budget from the rolling-PR protocol. Other lists can add their own per-list tests when the dynamic-message form appears in their surface.
3. **"Carryover finalisation in next session" pattern** — the previous cron job left uncommitted work that this session finalised. Per the rolling-PR protocol's carryover section, the verification (full suite + tsc + eslint + build) was re-run in this session, then the work was committed alongside the new List 1 work in a single commit chain.
