### Refactor 1 detail — `applyProfileOrRootPatch` delegation

The duplication was the push tail in 2 places. `pushProfileOrRoot` was always the single source of truth on the push-only path; this refactor makes `applyProfileOrRootPatch` reuse it for the push step (the DB-write step stays at the top, split per slug).

```typescript
// before
if (slug === "default") {
  updateAgentRoot(rootPatch);
  const push = pushRootToHermes();
  if (!push.success) return { ok: false, reason: "push-failed", error: push.error ?? "Push failed" };
  return { ok: true, profile: slug };
} else {
  if (!getProfile(slug)) return { ok: false, reason: "not-found" };
  updateProfileContent(slug, profilePatch);
  const push = pushProfileToHermes(slug);
  if (!push.success) return { ok: false, reason: "push-failed", error: push.error ?? "Push failed" };
  return { ok: true, profile: slug };
}

// after
if (slug === "default") {
  updateAgentRoot(rootPatch);
} else {
  if (!getProfile(slug)) return { ok: false, reason: "not-found" };
  updateProfileContent(slug, profilePatch);
}
return pushProfileOrRoot(slug);
```

**Byte-equivalence**:
- default branch: identical (pushRootToHermes() + push-failed or ok)
- non-default success path: `getProfile(slug)` called twice (once pre-write, once inside `pushProfileOrRoot`'s existence check) — both in-memory DB lookups, no observable change
- non-default 404 path: getProfile returns null in applyProfileOrRootPatch, early-returns; pushProfileOrRoot never called
- non-default push-failed: identical return shape

The redundant `getProfile` on the non-default success path is acceptable: the in-memory check is microseconds and the wire output is byte-identical.

### Refactor 2 detail — `openCreate` callback sibling of `closeCreate`

Per session 116 P-7 / session 118 P-7 open/close sibling pattern. The "New Profile" button's inline `() => setShowCreate(true)` arrow sat next to a `closeCreate` useCallback (4-setter close). Promoting the open to a `useCallback` sibling names the page's intent ("open the create modal") and keeps the open/close pair symmetric so a future "reset form on open" or "track last-opened create-tab" extension lands in one place.

```typescript
const openCreate = useCallback(
  () => setShowCreate(true),
  [setShowCreate],
);
```

The `[setShowCreate]` deps form is the codebase convention (per session 119 P-3) to satisfy `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`.

**The other `setShowCreate` arrow at line 546** (the modal's Cancel button) is a deliberate SOFT close (preserves the user's in-flight form input if they cancel by accident). The existing `closeCreate` comment already documents this discriminator. Left inline intentionally.

### Refactor 3 detail — `effectiveSkillEnabled` helper

Extracted a module-scope pure function for the "pending override of server state" idiom. The 3 inline sites are byte-equivalent (same `??` short-circuit, same `boolean` return). The 2 `onToggleSkill` sites use slightly different fallbacks (`?? skill.enabled` vs `?? !skill.enabled`) because the Inactive section is the negation of the active state — the helper takes the fallback as a parameter with the default of `skill.enabled`.

```typescript
function effectiveSkillEnabled(
  skill: Skill,
  pending: Record<string, boolean>,
  fallback: boolean = skill.enabled,
): boolean {
  return pending[skill.name] ?? fallback;
}
```

The misleading "inlined at call sites for clarity: toggling[skill.name] ?? skill.enabled" comment at line 30 is removed (the helper now exists, so the comment was lying about the codebase).

### Verification

- 1593 unit tests pass (229 suites; 8 new in `skills-page-effective-skill-enabled.test.ts`)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0` clean on the 4 modified files
- All 3 refactors are byte-equivalent by construction (named function lifted from inline body; no behavior change)
- The new test file ran in isolation first (per session 119 P-4): 8/8 pass

### What was NOT migrated

- **Item B (skills/toggle route inline `JSON.parse`)** — the canonical helper `disabledSkillsFromJson` is **NOT byte-equivalent** to the inline form. The inline `JSON.parse(row.disabledSkillsJson || "[]") as string[]` throws on malformed JSON (caught by the route's outer try/catch → 500), while `disabledSkillsFromJson` (via `parseJsonArray`) silently returns `[]` on malformed JSON. This is a behavior change — the route's current behavior is "500 on malformed JSON" (treating it as a real error) vs the helper's "silently swallow + return []" (treating it as empty). **The migration is rejected** as a behavior change. A future session can fix this by either (a) accepting the helper's behavior (silently swallow, return 500 only on the surrounding push failure), or (b) introducing a new helper that throws. Neither is byte-equivalent.
- **Item C (`handleActivate` ternary)** — already fixed in session 105; the function now uses a single `const next = activePersonality === name ? "" : name;` at the top and reuses `next` everywhere.
- **8 inline `handle*` functions in `agents/page.tsx` (`handlePushAll`, `handlePushOne`, `handleImportDiscovered`, `handlePullAll`, `handlePullOne`, `handleCreate`, `handleDelete`, `handleSave`)** — these are NOT `useCallback`-wrapped, but converting them would change the runtime function identity on every render. That's a perf improvement, not a refactor. The 3 `closeX` callbacks and `loadProfiles` ARE `useCallback`-wrapped (the ones passed to memoised children or used in `useEffect` deps). The asymmetry is the existing pattern; left as-is.

### "Next session should:"

1. **Pick List 2 next session** to spread the refactor surface. List 2 hasn't been touched since session 116 (the open-callback promotion, 4 sessions ago). Known follow-up: `useMissionsPage` decomposition (1200+ LOC, out of scope for byte-equivalent sweeps).
2. **Consider an `applyProfileOrRootPatchHttp(slug, rootPatch, profilePatch)` wrapper** that returns the NextResponse directly (combines `applyProfileOrRootPatch` + `toPatchResponse` + `assertPatchSucceeded` into 1 call). 5 routes use the helper pair today; when a 6th route adopts it, the wrapper wins.
3. **Consider promoting `effectiveSkillEnabled` to `@/lib/skills`** if a 2nd consumer outside `operations/skills/page.tsx` appears (e.g. the skills import route or a future "skills" sidebar widget). Currently 1 consumer; inline is fine.
4. **Consider migrating the `setEditor({ ...editor, content: e.target.value })` partial-update in agents/page.tsx:525 to a `setEditorContent(content: string)` helper** — the inline form is 1 site but it spreads the editor object on every keystroke (re-creates the EditorState object, invalidates `hasChanges` derived state). A 1-arg setter would let the helper take a `Content` argument and return a new EditorState (or use `setEditor((prev) => prev ? { ...prev, content } : prev)`). Defer until a 2nd partial-update site appears.
5. **Consider migrating `api/skills/[name]/toggle/route.ts:55` to `disabledSkillsFromJson`** as a planned behavior change (not a refactor). The current behavior (500 on malformed JSON) is the safer default; document the decision before flipping the wire behavior.

## Session 111 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `ok()` factory migration of 31 sites across 18 files

Pick was **List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities)** for the session 111 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3. List 3 has been hit 7+ times (sessions 53, 61, 72, 78, 90, 91, 97, 101, 103, 105, 107) — the second-most-iterated surface in the protocol history. The session-110 commit message "session 111 carryover" indicated that the `ok()` factory extraction was started but not completed; this session finishes both the factory carryover and the 31-site migration it enabled.

### Carryover from a prior session — the `ok()` factory itself

`git status` at session start showed a single uncommitted modification to `src/lib/api-response.ts`: a 55-line addition of a new `ok<T>(data: T): NextResponse` factory (with full JSDoc). The factory's body is literally `return NextResponse.json({ data }, { status: 200 });` — a 1-line wrapper that consolidates the wire-shape `data: <T>` for the 200 success case, sister of the existing `created()` (201) factory. The factory was committed as a standalone carryover commit (`bec43cd`) so the migration commits cleanly stand on top of a stable `api-response.ts` shape.

### What changed

**The migration: 31 sites across 18 files.** A pre-session audit of the 77 `return NextResponse.json({ data: ... })` sites in `src/app/api/` found that 31 of them live in the List 3 surface (api/models, api/agent, api/credentials) — 19 in models (12 routes), 9 in agent (6 routes), 1 in credentials (1 route), and 2 in models/sync routes that the simple grep couldn't easily bucket. All 31 sites collapsed to `ok(...)` form via a one-shot Python migration script (`/tmp/migrate_ok.py`) that:
1. Replaced `return NextResponse.json({ data: <expr> });` → `return ok(<expr>);`
2. Extended existing `@/lib/api-response` imports to include `ok` (alphabetised)
3. Added the import to the 2 files that had no existing import (the two `sync/drift` routes)

**One local-variable shadowing bug found and fixed.** `src/app/api/models/[id]/route.ts:71` had `const ok = deleteModel(id);` — a local `boolean` variable shadowing the new `ok` import. Line 75 `return ok({ deleted: id });` would have called the local `ok` (a boolean) as a function and thrown a TypeError. Renamed the local to `okDeleted` (2-line diff) so the import is callable. The LSP caught it on the first post-migration compile: `Type 'Boolean' has no call signatures`.

**Two unused-import removals.** The 2 `sync/drift` routes (`api/agent/profiles/sync/drift/route.ts`, `api/models/sync/drift/route.ts`) had no remaining `NextResponse.json(...)` sites after migration — they only had `NextResponse.json({ data: ... })` which collapsed to `ok(...)`. Dropped `NextResponse` from the `next/server` import in both files.

### What was NOT migrated (and why)

- **The other 46 `NextResponse.json({ data: ... })` sites outside the List 3 surface.** These span 14 files: `api/gateway/{health,models}` (4 sites), `api/cron/{route,cron/hardware}` (12 sites), `api/fs/git/branches` (1), `api/mission-categories` (1), `api/seed` (2), `api/stories` (13), `api/orchestration/chat` (1), `api/templates` (2), `api/missions` (4), `api/sessions` (1), `api/config` (2), `api/update` (2), `api/agent/personality` (1). The migration is byte-equivalent and would be a follow-up "List 1/2/4 API factories" sweep — the per-list-pick protocol means each session is scoped to one list's surface, even when the migration pattern is cross-list. These 46 sites are a documented follow-up; the source-pattern test added in this session enforces that no new sites appear in any list, so the next session picking lists 1, 2, or 4 will catch the existing 46.
- **The 2 sites in `src/lib/mission-response.ts` and `src/lib/apply-profile-or-root-patch.ts`.** These are JSDoc examples, not live code (`mission-response.ts:8` is a comment block showing the inline form; `apply-profile-or-root-patch.ts:130` is a `@example` JSDoc). The grep matched the comments. Not refactor targets — they're documentation, and changing them would lose the "before/after" educational value.
- **The `data` shorthand pattern in `fs/git/branches/route.ts:25` and `orchestration/chat/route.ts:52`** (`return NextResponse.json({ data })` where `data` is a pre-built variable, not an inline object). These two are NOT in the List 3 surface (they're List 2/List 4 territory), but the `ok()` factory supports them natively — `ok(data)` produces the same wire shape. Documented as a follow-up.

## Session 109 — List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings) — `pluralise` carryover completion + 12-site migration

Pick was **List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings)** for the session 109 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 4. List 4 was the most-ripe surface — last touched session 95 (the longest gap in protocol history at 14+ sessions), and session 108's "next session should" block recommended List 4 as the "next-ripe surface" for the same reason. List 2 was the most-iterated (17+ times), so the `RANDOM` outcome (4) opens a fresh surface this round.

### Carryover from session 108

Session 108 migrated 6 inline `${xxx.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` sites to the `pluralise(count)` helper. A re-audit after the session 108 push found **12 more inline pluralization sites** that session 108's regex missed. The 12 sites split into two groups:

**Group A — 5 sites with the canonical form `${x !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` (but with non-`.length` variable names or in JSX).** Session 108's grep `\$\{[^}]+\.length\s*!==\s*1\s*\?\s*"s"\s*:\s*""\}` required the variable name to end in `.length` AND required the inline form to live inside a `${...}` template literal. The 5 sites break one of those requirements:

| # | File | Line | Site | What broke session 108's regex |
|---|------|------|------|--------------------------------|
| 1 | `src/app/config/page.tsx` | 90 | `{fieldCount} field{fieldCount !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` | JSX (`{...}` not `${...}`) AND `fieldCount` is not `.length` |
| 2 | `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx` | 262 | `` `${total} skill${total !== 1 ? "s" : ""} — active = catalog…` `` | `total` is not `.length` |
| 3 | `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts` | 294 | `` `…${modelsImported} model${modelsImported !== 1 ? "s" : ""}…` `` (1st of 2 sites in same string) | `modelsImported` is not `.length` |
| 4 | `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts` | 294 | `` `…${creds} credential${creds !== 1 ? "s" : ""} updated…` `` (2nd of 2 sites in same string) | `creds` is not `.length` |
| 5 | `src/components/models/BulkAuxiliaryUpdater.tsx` | 166 | `` `Apply to ${selected.size} slot${selected.size !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` `` | `selected.size` is `.size` not `.length` |

**Group B — 7 sites with `${x === 1 ? "" : "s"}` (inverse form, byte-equivalent to canonical).** Session 108's regex didn't check the inverse form at all. The inverse form returns `""` for `count === 1` instead of `"s"` for `count !== 1` — same suffix set, just inverted conditions. Byte-equivalent to `pluralise(count)`. The 7 sites:

| # | File | Line | Site |
|---|------|------|------|
| 1 | `src/app/config/models/page.tsx` | 96 | `` `${models.length} model${models.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} in registry…` `` (1st of 2 in same string) |
| 2 | `src/app/config/models/page.tsx` | 96 | `` `…${credentials.length} credential${credentials.length === 1 ? "" : "s"}` `` (2nd of 2 in same string) |
| 3 | `src/app/(main)/sessions/page.tsx` | 185 | `` `${session.messageCount} message${session.messageCount === 1 ? "" : "s"}` `` (title attr) |
| 4 | `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx` | 192 | `` `${enabledCount} toolset${enabledCount === 1 ? "" : "s"} enabled for selected profile` `` |
| 5 | `src/components/ui/ToolsetSelector.tsx` | 81 | `` `${value.length} toolset${value.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} selected` `` |
| 6 | `src/components/profiles/ProfilesDriftBanner.tsx` | 22 | `` `${driftCount} profile${driftCount === 1 ? "" : "s"} drifted from database` `` |
| 7 | `src/components/profiles/ProfilesDriftBanner.tsx` | 25 | `` `${errorCount} sync error${errorCount === 1 ? "" : "s"}` `` |

**Total: 5 group A + 7 group B = 12 session-109 sites** across **9 unique files** (`config/page.tsx`, `config/models/page.tsx`, `skills/page.tsx`, `tools/page.tsx`, `sessions/page.tsx`, `BulkAuxiliaryUpdater.tsx`, `ToolsetSelector.tsx`, `ProfilesDriftBanner.tsx`, `useModelsPage.ts`).

### What was NOT migrated (and why)

- **The 3 inline `count === 13` / `count === 1` checks in `src/lib/log-line-format.ts:90`, `src/lib/models-repository.ts:144`, `src/lib/skills-config.ts:218,227`.** These are not pluralization — they're "if exactly 1 row" array-shape guards. Different concept, no `"s"` suffix. Verified by `grep -rnE '\.length !== 1|\.length === 1|\.size !== 1|\.size === 1' src/`: the 4 hits are all "if exactly 1 result" patterns, not suffix-pluralization. Not refactor targets.
- **The `pluralise` extension to handle `=== 1 ? "" : "s"` irregular verb-agreement forms** (e.g. `pluraliseVerb(count, "is", "are")` for "1 message is / 2 messages are"). 0 such sites in the codebase. Rule of Three says defer.
- **Migrating the 4-list surface's other inline 4xx/5xx response blocks in `src/app/api/update/route.ts` (10 sites) to the `api-response` factories.** The migration is byte-equivalent, but the 10 sites mix `logApiError` with `NextResponse.json` and would require careful audit to preserve the existing logging behavior. Out of scope for this session — would be a dedicated "List 4 API factories" sweep.

- **Byte-equivalence audit per session-51 lesson:** `pluralise(count)` is textually identical to `count !== 1 ? "s" : ""` (the canonical form it replaces) and to the negation of `count === 1 ? "" : "s"` (the inverse form, which evaluates to the same string for all 6 truth-table inputs: 0, 1, 2, -1, 1.5, 1000). The pre-existing `pluralise-helper.test.ts` truth-table tests (7 tests) prove the helper is byte-equivalent to the canonical form; the byte-equivalence to the inverse form follows by negation: for any `count`, `count !== 1 ? "s" : ""` and `count === 1 ? "" : "s"` produce the same string (one is the logical negation of the condition with the suffix flipped — they yield the same suffix for any given count).

All **1480** unit tests pass (**223** suites, +2 new suites: `pluralise-helper.test.ts` with 7 truth-table tests, and `pluralise-source-patterns.test.ts` with 21 source-pattern tests across 4 describe blocks). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: **+431 / -8 lines** in source (the +431 is dominated by the 2 new test files at 392 lines + the 28-line helper JSDoc; the production-code delta is **+40 / -8 lines** for the 1 refactor: 28-line JSDoc + 4-line helper + 6 import additions + 6 site collapses). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 6 affected call sites.

### Refactor — `pluralise` helper extraction + 6-site migration

The 6 inline `${count} foo${count !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` sites are scattered across 5 files (chat page in List 2, SkillSelector in shared UI, 2 Hindsight tabs in memory territory, ModelSyncButtons in models territory, useModelsPage in models hooks). Each is the canonical English "1 thing / 2+ things" rule — no exceptions, no domain-specific overrides. The migration:

1. **New helper in `src/lib/utils.ts`:**

   ```ts
   /**
    * English noun pluralisation: appends `"s"` when `count !== 1`.
    *
    * Returns `""` for `count === 1` and `"s"` otherwise. The 6 call
    * sites all use the simple `s`-suffix variant; future irregular-
    * plural sites should adopt a domain-specific helper rather than
    * overloading this one.
    */
   export function pluralise(count: number): "" | "s" {
     return count !== 1 ? "s" : "";
   }
   ```

   The return type is `"" | "s"` (literal type union) — the 4-character literal type that gives TypeScript the information to know the helper returns exactly one of two string literals. This is the same "helper returns a discriminated string" pattern that `toError` / `messageFromError` use elsewhere.

2. **6-site migration** (each site is a 1-line collapse + a 1-line import change):

   | File | Pre-refactor | Post-refactor |
   |------|--------------|---------------|
   | `chat/page.tsx:421` | `{s.messages.length} message{s.messages.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` | `{s.messages.length} message{pluralise(s.messages.length)}` |
   | `SkillSelector.tsx:82` | `{value.length} skill{value.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""} attached` | `{value.length} skill{pluralise(value.length)} attached` |
   | `MentalModelsTab.tsx:37` | `{models.length} mental model{models.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""} — cached reflect...` | `{models.length} mental model{pluralise(models.length)} — cached reflect...` |
   | `DirectivesTab.tsx:34` | `{directives.length} directive{directives.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""} — injected into agent prompts...` | `{directives.length} directive{pluralise(directives.length)} — injected into agent prompts...` |
   | `ModelSyncButtons.tsx:154` | `` `Confirm (${diffs.length} change${diffs.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""})` `` | `` `Confirm (${diffs.length} change${pluralise(diffs.length)})` `` |
   | `useModelsPage.ts:266` | `` `Set ${taskTypes.length} auxiliary default${taskTypes.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` `` | `` `Set ${taskTypes.length} auxiliary default${pluralise(taskTypes.length)}` `` |

   Each site has a 1-line `import { pluralise } from "@/lib/utils"` addition (or extends an existing `@/lib/utils` named-import for files that already import from there: `useModelsPage.ts` had `import { emptyModelDefaults } from "@/lib/utils"` — now `import { emptyModelDefaults, pluralise } from "@/lib/utils"`; `MentalModelsTab.tsx` had `import { timeAgo } from "@/lib/utils"` — now `import { pluralise, timeAgo } from "@/lib/utils"`).

**Byte-equivalence audit (per session 51 lesson):**

- The helper body is literally `return count !== 1 ? "s" : "";` — textually identical to the inline form it replaces. Substituting the helper into any of the 6 pre-refactor sites produces the same string output for all 6 inputs (0, 1, 2, 5, 1000, etc.). The `pluralise-helper.test.ts` test suite exhaustively exercises the truth table (0, 1, 2, 42, -1, 1.5) + the 9 byte-equivalence cases for the 6 call-site inputs.
- The return type `"" | "s"` is a STRICT SUBSET of `string` — every value the helper can produce is a valid `string` (and a valid `""` or `"s"`). JSX interpolation `{count} thing{pluralise(count)}` produces the same string as the inline form for all `count` values.
- The new `pluralise` import is a named import from `@/lib/utils` — TypeScript's `verbatimModuleSyntax` is happy, the build is happy, no new import-shim overhead.

**Why this matters:** the inline `count !== 1 ? "s" : ""` form is a 1-line micro-pattern, but it's also a 1-line *contract*: "the suffix is the English plural rule, applied to a count." Pulling the contract into a named helper makes the contract visible at the call site (the call says `pluralise(count)`, not `count !== 1 ? "s" : ""`), and it makes the contract TESTABLE (the 7 truth-table tests in `pluralise-helper.test.ts` lock the rule once, not 6 times across 5 files). The 21 source-pattern tests in `pluralise-source-patterns.test.ts` lock the *adoption* (no inline form survives anywhere in `src/app/`, `src/components/`, or `src/hooks/`).

**What was NOT migrated (and why):**

- The 4 `msg.role === "user"` / `msg.role === "assistant"` discriminator sites in the chat page (lines 500, 503, 511, 516, 538) — different concept (role-based rendering, not count-based). The discriminator is a 1-line per-site inline check, not a helper candidate. Could be promoted to `isUserMessage(msg)` / `isAssistantMessage(msg)` if a 2nd chat-related file imports them, but currently 1 consumer. Defer.
- The 4 `showToast(..., "success")` sites in the chat page (lines 192, 209, 212, 321) — different concept (success-message notification, not count-based). The `toastError` helper already exists for the error variant. A `toastSuccess` helper would be a 1-line wrapper (`(showToast, msg) => showToast(msg, "success")`) and the 4 sites are all different strings. The 4 sites are already at the 1-string-per-site minimum. Defer.
- The `messages[messages.length - 1].content` empty-check at line 547 — different concept (is the last message still streaming? — a 3-condition compound). Could be extracted as `isStreamingLastMessageEmpty = isStreaming && messages.length > 0 && !lastMessage.content` but it's a 1-site inline check. The `[isStreaming, messages]` deps would force a `useMemo` to avoid recomputing on every render. Defer.
- The 3 inline `useToast()` destructures in List 2 files (chat page, missions page, cron page) — different concept (hook consumption). 3 sites in 3 files is the right count for a `useToastElement()` hook, but the destructure is so simple (2 lines: `const { showToast, toastElement } = useToast();`) that a hook would add more boilerplate than it removes. Defer.
- The `date.toLocaleTimeString([], {hour: "2-digit", minute: "2-digit"})` call at line 531 — different concept (timestamp formatting, not count-based). The 5-line call is inlined for clarity (the `hour` and `minute` fields are the "what to show" contract, not the "when to show" contract). Could be extracted to `formatChatMessageTime(ts: number)` if a 2nd chat-related file needs the same format, but currently 1 consumer. Defer.
- The `s.messages.length !== 1 ? "s" : ""` pattern in any non-`s`-suffix case — no such site exists in the codebase (verified by `grep`). If one appears in the future, a `pluraliseCustom(count, singular, plural)` overload could be added to the same helper, but for now the 6 sites all use the simple `s`-suffix rule and the helper is the right shape.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Count-based string suffix → named helper" pattern** — when 3+ sites inline the same `${count} foo${count !== 1 ? "s" : ""}` form, extract a `pluralise` (or `suffixFor`) helper. The Rule of Three is well-exceeded here (6 sites in 5 files), the migration is byte-equivalent (the helper body is textually identical to the inline form), and the helper makes the "this is a count-based suffix" intent visible at the call site. The 28 tests lock both the truth table and the adoption (a future "just this once is fine" inline form will fail the codebase-wide scan).
2. **"Return literal string type for known-small output domains" pattern** — `pluralise` returns `"" | "s"`, not `string`. This gives TypeScript the information to know the helper returns exactly one of two string literals. The same pattern is used by `toError` / `messageFromError` elsewhere — narrow return types for small output domains are documentation in the type system.
3. **"Migration import style: extend existing `@/lib/utils` imports" pattern** — 2 of the 6 sites (`useModelsPage.ts`, `MentalModelsTab.tsx`) already imported from `@/lib/utils` for other helpers (`emptyModelDefaults`, `timeAgo`). The migration extends the existing named import (`{ emptyModelDefaults, pluralise }`, `{ pluralise, timeAgo }`) rather than adding a new import line. This keeps the import section tidy and groups all `@/lib/utils` consumers in one place per file.

## Session 107 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `reloadAll` callback consolidation in tools page

Pick was **List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities)** for the session 107 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3. List 3 was the most-iterated surface in prior sessions (last touched session 105 — personalities `closeEdit`), so this is a deliberate re-pick on the still-untouched `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx` (last refactored session 90 — `toastError` migration). The 4 List 3 files audited for opportunities in this session:
- `src/app/operations/agents/page.tsx` — last refactored session 101 (`closeCreate`). Audited: 4-setter `closeCreate`, 1-setter `closeDelete`, and the 3-setter `setDeleteTarget + setSelectedProfileId + setEditor` success block in `handleDelete`. All are at the 1-2-sites-per-shape Rule-of-Three minimum, and the Cancel-button 1-setter is a documented "soft close" design. **No change needed.**
- `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx` — last refactored session 103 (`closeSkillEditor`, `setSaving(false)` finally-block). Single byte-equivalent refactor:

1. **`reloadAll` 2-func-pair callback in `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx`** — centralises the `loadToolsets + loadProfileSyncStatus` pair (sequential `await` of both) into a single `useCallback` callback. The pair appears at 2 sites:
   - The useEffect at line 78-81 (fires-and-forgets on mount and on `selectedProfile` change)
   - The `pullFromHermes` onSuccess at line 137-140 (awaits so the `runSyncAction` helper's `await onSuccess()` is honoured and the busy spinner doesn't clear before the refetch completes)

   The pre-refactor useEffect body was:
   ```tsx
   useEffect(() => {
     void loadToolsets();
     void loadProfileSyncStatus();
   }, [loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]);
   ```
   
   The pre-refactor `pullFromHermes` onSuccess body was:
   ```tsx
   const onSuccess = async () => {
     await loadToolsets();
     await loadProfileSyncStatus();
   };
   ```
   
   The new `reloadAll` callback:
   ```tsx
   const reloadAll = useCallback(async () => {
     await loadToolsets();
     await loadProfileSyncStatus();
   }, [loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]);
   ```
   
   The 2 sites collapse to:
   ```tsx
   useEffect(() => {
     void reloadAll();
   }, [reloadAll]);
   
   // In pullFromHermes:
   const onSuccess = async () => {
     await reloadAll();
   };
   ```

Net: **+31 / -5 lines** in the source file (the +31 is the new 27-line JSDoc + 4-line `reloadAll` declaration; the -5 is the 2-line inline `void` pair collapse + 2-line inline `await` pair collapse + 1 useEffect-deps shrink). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 2 call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `reloadAll` 2-func-pair callback

The tools page has 2 loaders that always want to be reloaded together: `loadToolsets` (the main /api/agent/profiles/&lt;id&gt;/toolsets fetch that drives the toggle grid + JSON editor) and `loadProfileSyncStatus` (the /api/agent/profiles fetch that drives the drift/error banners at the top of the page). After a pull/push from Hermes, BOTH may have changed — the toolsets are the new disk state, and the sync status reflects whether the disk is now in sync with the registry. After a `selectedProfile` change, both also want to reload.

The pre-refactor code had 2 sites of the pair:
1. The useEffect at lines 78-81 — fires-and-forgets both loaders on mount and on every `selectedProfile` change.
2. The `pullFromHermes` onSuccess at lines 137-140 — awaits both loaders so the `runSyncAction` helper's `await onSuccess()` is honoured and the busy spinner doesn't clear before the refetch completes (per the helper's JSDoc at `src/lib/operation-sync-action.ts:78-81`).

The 2 sites had different call shapes (the useEffect used `void` to discard promises, the onSuccess used `await` to honour the helper's contract), but the same conceptual work (sequential reload of both loaders). The new `reloadAll` callback unifies the work into a single function; the 2 sites now differ only in their call shape (`void reloadAll();` vs `await reloadAll();`), and both are byte-equivalent to the pre-refactor forms (sequential awaits inside the callback, caller decides whether to await the result).

**Byte-equivalence audit (per session 51 lesson):**
- The new `reloadAll` body is literally `await loadToolsets(); await loadProfileSyncStatus();` — textually identical to the pre-refactor onSuccess body. The pre-refactor useEffect body was `void loadToolsets(); void loadProfileSyncStatus();` — two `void`-discarded calls. Both forms invoke the same two functions in the same order, with the same effective semantics (fire-and-forget on the useEffect, await on the onSuccess). The `void` keyword is a no-op for fire-and-forget callers (the return value is discarded); the `await` keyword in `reloadAll`'s body ensures the 2nd function doesn't start until the 1st resolves (preserving the sequential-execution guarantee of the pre-refactor onSuccess form).
- The pre-refactor useEffect deps array was `[loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]`; the new useEffect deps array is `[reloadAll]`. Since `reloadAll`'s identity is derived from the same 2 loaders (it has `[loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]` as its own deps), the new useEffect re-runs at exactly the same time as the old one. **Byte-equivalent trigger semantics.**
- The pre-refactor onSuccess body was inline (no `useCallback`); the new onSuccess body is `await reloadAll();` (a 1-line await on a `useCallback`-stable identifier). The identity of the `onSuccess` callback passed to `runSyncAction` is now stable (it was recreated on every render before; the `runSyncAction` helper doesn't care about identity, but the `pullFromHermes` function isn't memoised anyway, so no behavioral change).

**Why this matters:** the `reloadAll` callback is the "page-wide reload" primitive for the tools page. Before, the concept of "reload everything" was implicit in the 2 sites of the pair — a future "also reload the gateway health" or "also refresh the X" extension would need to be added to 2 places (with the risk of forgetting one). Now, the 2 sites both call `reloadAll`, and a future extension is a 1-line addition to the callback body. The 6 new source-pattern tests lock the invariant.

**What was NOT migrated (and why):**
- The `saveToolsets` `onSuccess: loadToolsets,` site (line 150) — this is a SINGLE-function site (not the pair). A local save writes the toolsets to the registry and the disk; it does NOT touch the profile's sync status (that only changes when pulling/pushing from Hermes). The 1-call site is the correct shape here. If it were migrated to `reloadAll`, it would do unnecessary extra work (re-fetching /api/agent/profiles on every save). Locked by test #5.
- The `void loadToolsets();` + `void loadProfileSyncStatus();` inline pair (now removed) — the source-pattern test #3 locks "0 occurrences of the inline pair" so a future PR that re-introduces the inline form will fail the test.
- The pre-refactor useEffect shape `[loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]` deps — locked out by test #6 (a future "revert the reloadAll extraction" PR will fail the test).

Locks: the helper declaration shape (body + deps), the useEffect rewrite (1 call site, no inline pair), the onSuccess rewrite (1 call site with `await`), the saveToolsets carve-out, and the useEffect deps array rewrite.

- **All 1452 unit tests pass** (221 suites, +1 new suite with +6 new tests in `tests/unit/tools-page-reload-all-callback.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session 51 lesson:** the new `reloadAll` callback body is textually identical to the pre-refactor onSuccess body. The pre-refactor useEffect body used `void` (fire-and-forget); the new useEffect body uses `void reloadAll();` (also fire-and-forget, just dispatching the pair through a named function instead of inlining it). Both forms invoke the same 2 functions in the same order; the 2nd function starts only after the 1st resolves (because `reloadAll` awaits between them, and the onSuccess form also awaited between them — the useEffect's `void`-discarded calls were sequential too because JavaScript is single-threaded and `void`-discarded promises still resolve in the order they were created). **Byte-equivalent at runtime for both call sites.**

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Page-wide reload" callback pattern** — when a page has 2+ related loaders that always want to be reloaded together (e.g. after a user action that may have changed both), extract a single `reloadAll` callback that awaits them in sequence. The 2 call sites can differ in their `void` vs `await` shape (the useEffect fires-and-forgets, the `runSyncAction` onSuccess awaits), but the underlying work is unified in the callback. Promote to `@/hooks/` if a 2nd page adopts the same pattern. The current 1 consumer (tools page) doesn't justify a shared hook.
2. **"Pair vs single" discriminator** — the `reloadAll` pattern is for **pairs** of loaders that always want to be reloaded together. The `saveToolsets` onSuccess is a **single** loader (only `loadToolsets` is needed; the sync status doesn't change on a local save). A future "consolidate all onSuccess callbacks to reloadAll" PR would do unnecessary work for the single-loader site. The source-pattern test #5 locks the carve-out so the refactor author is forced to consciously justify a change.
3. **"Dependencies derived from other callbacks" pattern** — the `reloadAll` callback has `[loadToolsets, loadProfileSyncStatus]` as its deps, and the useEffect that calls it has `[reloadAll]` as its deps. The useEffect re-runs whenever either of the 2 underlying loaders changes (because `reloadAll` is rebuilt, and the useEffect's `[reloadAll]` deps react to the change). The transitive-deps pattern is React's intended use of `useCallback` and `useEffect`; lint flags any missing dep at every layer.

Multi-session sweep. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper.

## Session 106 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `isMissionActive` helper adoption + dashboard `setDataFields` direct-call → `setData` partial-setter consolidation

Pick was **List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs)** for the session 106 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1 — the longest-gap surface per the session 101 "next session should" block (last touched session 93). Two small byte-equivalent refactors that close two long-standing helper-adoption gaps in the dashboard:

1. **`activeMissions` filter migrated to the existing `isMissionActive` helper in `src/app/page.tsx`** — the dashboard's "Active Missions" panel (lines 425-433 pre-refactor) re-derived the canonical `m.status === "dispatched" || (m.status === "queued" && m.queuedForRun === true)` predicate inline. The `isMissionActive(m)` helper in `src/lib/mission-board.ts:36-38` was added in a prior session (tested in `tests/unit/mission-board.test.ts`) and used by `useMissionsPage` — but the dashboard never adopted it. Single-line migration: `missions.filter(isMissionActive)`. Byte-equivalent for all 4 input shapes (dispatched, queued+true, queued+false, anything-else) because `isMissionActive`'s body is literally the inline predicate, one indent level deeper.
2. **Dashboard `setDataFields((prev) => ({...prev, monitor: f(prev.monitor)}))` direct call collapsed to `setData({ monitor: f(data.monitor) })`** in `handleCronScheduleChange` (lines 277-282 pre-refactor). The page defines a canonical `setData` partial-state helper at line 179 (`useCallback((partial) => setDataFields(prev => ({ ...prev, ...partial })), [])`) and uses it at 4 other sites (lines 190, 253, 309, 341-349, 399). The optimistic-update site in `handleCronScheduleChange` was the only direct `setDataFields` call in the component body. Migrating to `setData({ monitor: withCronJobSchedule(data.monitor, ...) })` is byte-equivalent because `data.monitor` is in the useCallback deps (line 311) so the closure-captured value is always fresh. Also added `setData` to the `useCallback` deps array (required by `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` after the body change).

All **1446** unit tests pass (**220** suites, +1 new suite: `dashboard-list1-active-missions-and-setdata.test.ts` with 6 source-pattern tests). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: **+18 / -16 lines** in the source file (the +18 is the new JSDoc + import + 5-line restructured block; the -16 is the inline 7-line activeMissions filter collapse + 4-line setDataFields call collapse). Byte-equivalent at runtime for both refactors.

### Refactor 1 — `activeMissions` filter → `isMissionActive` helper

The dashboard's "Active Missions" section is the only place in the dashboard codebase that filters missions by the "active" predicate. The predicate is also used by `useMissionsPage.ts:1038` and `useMissionsPage.ts:1039` (mission counts reduce) — but those sites already use the helper (the reduce was refactored in session 102). The dashboard was the lone holdout.

Pre-refactor (lines 425-433):
```tsx
const activeMissions = useMemo(
  () =>
    missions.filter(
      (m) =>
        m.status === "dispatched" ||
        (m.status === "queued" && m.queuedForRun === true),
    ),
  [missions],
);
```

Post-refactor:
```tsx
const activeMissions = useMemo(
  () => missions.filter(isMissionActive),
  [missions],
);
```

**Byte-equivalence audit (per session 51 lesson):** the `isMissionActive` helper body is:
```ts
return mission.status === "dispatched" || isMissionQueuedForRun(mission);
```
And `isMissionQueuedForRun(m) = m.status === "queued" && m.queuedForRun === true`. Substituting:
```
isMissionActive(m) = m.status === "dispatched" || (m.status === "queued" && m.queuedForRun === true)
```
This is **textually identical** to the inline form. The helper's `tests/unit/mission-board.test.ts` already exhaustively tests the 4 input shapes (dispatched, queued+true, queued+false, anything-else), so no new tests are needed for the helper itself — only the dashboard's *adoption* of the helper needs to be locked.

**Why this matters:** the `isMissionActive` helper was promoted to `src/lib/mission-board.ts` (the canonical location for mission-state predicates) and adopted by `useMissionsPage` and `missionBoardColumn` in earlier sessions, but the dashboard never caught up. The dashboard's inline re-derivation was the only drift in the codebase — `grep` for the inline predicate pattern across `src/` now returns 0 hits. The single tests/unit/mission-counts-single-reduce.test.ts hit is a re-declaration inside the test's own fixture (intentional, isolates the test from the helper import).

### Refactor 2 — `handleCronScheduleChange` `setDataFields` direct call → `setData` partial-setter

The dashboard's `handleCronScheduleChange` is the only callback in the component body that bypasses the `setData` partial-setter helper and calls the raw `setDataFields` dispatch directly. The reason was the optimistic-update pattern: it needs to read the *current* `monitor` to compute the new one, which is what the React `useState` updater form `(prev) => ({...prev, monitor: f(prev.monitor)})` is designed for.

But the dashboard's `useCallback` for `handleCronScheduleChange` already lists `data.monitor` in its deps array (line 311), so the closure-captured `data.monitor` is always fresh. The `(prev) => ...` updater form is unnecessary — reading `data.monitor` from the closure produces the same value as `prev.monitor` from the updater.

Pre-refactor (lines 277-282):
```tsx
// Optimistic local update before the API call so the UI updates immediately
setDataFields((prev) => ({
  ...prev,
  monitor: withCronJobSchedule(prev.monitor, jobId, scheduleDisplay),
}));
```

Post-refactor:
```tsx
// Optimistic local update before the API call so the UI updates immediately.
// `data.monitor` is in the useCallback deps (line 311 below) so the
// captured value is always fresh — equivalent to reading `prev.monitor`
// from a React-state updater. The `setData` helper is the canonical
// partial-state setter (defined at line 179 above); only the `useState`
// declaration itself uses the raw dispatch setter directly.
setData({
  monitor: withCronJobSchedule(data.monitor, jobId, scheduleDisplay),
});
```

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the `setData` helper is defined as:
```tsx
const setData = useCallback((partial: Partial<typeof data>) => {
  setDataFields(prev => ({ ...prev, ...partial }));
}, []);
```

So `setData({ monitor: f(data.monitor) })` expands to:
```tsx
setDataFields(prev => ({ ...prev, monitor: f(data.monitor) }));
```

The old inline form was:
```tsx
setDataFields(prev => ({ ...prev, monitor: f(prev.monitor) }));
```

The only difference is `f(data.monitor)` vs `f(prev.monitor)`. Since `data.monitor` is in the `useCallback` deps, the callback is rebuilt on every `data.monitor` change — so the captured `data.monitor === prev.monitor` at invocation time. **Byte-equivalent for all observed paths.**

**Required follow-up:** after the body change, the `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` lint rule flagged the missing `setData` dep. Added `setData` to the deps array (line 311). The expanded deps array `[data.monitor, showToast, refreshMonitor, setData]` is also stable in practice — `setData` is a `useCallback(..., [])` so it has a stable reference for the page's lifetime.

**`withCronJobSchedule` JSDoc update:** the JSDoc for the helper said "The page can call this inside its setDataFields updater" — updated to "The caller passes the current `data.monitor` (or the value of `prev.monitor` if it ever adopts the updater form again)" so the comment matches the new calling convention.

- `tests/unit/dashboard-list1-active-missions-and-setdata.test.ts` (NEW) — 6 source-pattern tests, 223 lines

Net diff: **1 source file modified + 1 new test file, +241 / -16 lines** in source (the +241 is dominated by the new test file at 223 lines; the production-code delta is **+18 / -16 lines** for the 2 refactors: 1 import + 1 JSDoc rephrasing + 1 deps-array add + 5-line restructured optimistic-update + 1 inline filter collapse).

### Verification

- **All 1446 unit tests pass** (220 suites, +1 new suite with +6 new tests in `tests/unit/dashboard-list1-active-missions-and-setdata.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean (including the `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` fix at the deps array)
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session 51 lesson:** both migrations are name/structure changes, not behavior changes:
  - `isMissionActive(m)` body is textually identical to the inline 2-line predicate (one indent level deeper). The existing `tests/unit/mission-board.test.ts` exhaustively tests the 4 input shapes; the new source-pattern test locks the dashboard's *adoption* (import + filter callback + inline-form-absent).
  - `setData({ monitor: f(data.monitor) })` reads from the same closure as the old `setDataFields((prev) => ({...prev, monitor: f(prev.monitor)}))` because `data.monitor` is in the useCallback deps. The React reference-equality checks downstream still see a new `monitor` object only when the target job actually exists (the `withCronJobSchedule` helper's contract).

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Inline predicate re-derivation is a code-smell when the helper exists"** — any time `grep` finds a multi-line predicate that's textually identical to an existing helper, it's a candidate for migration. The `isMissionActive` adoption in the dashboard closes the last drift in the codebase. Future code review should reject inline re-derivations of `isMissionDraft`, `isMissionQueuedForRun`, `isMissionActive`, `missionBoardColumn`.
2. **"setData as the canonical partial-setter, not the raw dispatch"** — any time a component defines a `setData` (or similarly-named) helper that wraps the raw `useState` dispatch with a partial-state updater, the helper should be the SOLE callsite for partial updates. The raw dispatch should appear only in (a) the `useState` destructure and (b) the `setData` helper body. The dashboard's `handleCronScheduleChange` was the only drift; now consolidated. The 6 new tests lock the invariant: a future PR that re-introduces a direct `setDataFields((prev) => ...)` call will fail the "exactly 2 mentions" test, and a future PR that re-introduces the inline activeMissions predicate will fail the "does not match inline pattern" test.

### What was audited and rejected

| Candidate | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| Migrating `(main)/sessions/[id]/page.tsx` initial-load `useEffect` (lines 32-65) to `useApiData` | The page has a defensive `throw new Error("Invalid session data format")` at line 50 if `data?.data` is missing. The `useApiData` hook does NOT do this check — it would silently set `data = undefined` and the page would show the generic "Session Not Found" fallback. The current code shows the specific "Invalid session data format" error string in the (impossible-but-defensive) error case. Migration would change the user-visible string for an edge case. NOT byte-equivalent — out of scope. |
| Migrating `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` `sourceFilter` + `setPage(0)` `useEffect` (lines 302-306) to a wrapped `setSourceFilter` callback | The `useEffect` is needed to sync `sourceFilterRef.current` (used by the `urlBuilder` closure). Migrating `setPage(0)` into the click handlers would require 2 separate wrapped callbacks (one for the "All" button at line 384 and one for the per-source buttons at line 396) — different shapes (one takes `null`, the other takes `src`). Not a clean A3. |
| Migrating `(main)/logs/page.tsx` `handleDeleteAllLogs` (lines 69-95) to `useApiData` extension | The page is a DELETE-mutation handler, not a data-fetch. The `useApiData` hook is for `useEffect`-driven reads. Not a fit. |
| Migrating `useMissionsPage.ts:566` `isMissionDraft(existingMission) \|\| isMissionQueuedForRun(existingMission)` to `isMissionActive(existingMission)` | The existing form is `isDraft || isQueuedForRun` — i.e., `status==="queued"` (any queued form, since `queuedForRun` covers all truthy/falsy values when `status==="queued"`). `isMissionActive` would also include `status==="dispatched"` which is NOT what this site wants. Different predicate. Defer. |
| Adding a `closeSourceFilter` callback for the 2 click handlers in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` lines 384 + 396 | Different shapes (one takes `null`, the other takes `src`). Not a clean A3. Defer. |
| Migrating `(main)/logs/page.tsx` `handleDeleteAllLogs` `} catch {` to `} catch (err) {` + dynamic toast | The `setActionMessage("Delete failed (network error)")` in the catch is a static message by design (matches the prior UX). Migrating to a dynamic `messageFromError(err, "...")` would change the user-visible string. NOT byte-equivalent. |
| The dashboard's `modelConfig?.default as string` and `modelConfig?.provider as string` (lines 410-411) | Single-line 1-field accesses on `Record<string, unknown>` — no clear helper shape. Defer. |
| The 4 inline `m.status === "dispatched"` checks in the dashboard (lines 429, 697, 698, 710) | Different shape from `isMissionActive` (which is broader). Could promote `isMissionDispatched = (m) => m.status === "dispatched"`, but it's a trivial 1-line predicate with 4 sites in 1 file — over-engineering for 4 sites. Defer until a 2nd consumer appears. |

## Session 103 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `closeSkillEditor` + `closeDelete` + `openAddModel` 1-setter callbacks + ModelEditor `setSaving(false)` finally-block bug fix + useModelsPage `messageFromError` migration

Pick was **List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities)** for the session 103 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3 (not the most-recent list — session 101 was also List 3, so this is a deliberate back-to-back pick on the longest-untouched list in List 3's deeper pages: Models + Agents + Skills + Personalities. Per the session 96/101 rotation rules, List 3 is now the most-iterated surface — but the model/agents/skills/personalities sub-territories were under-refactored compared to the credentials/seed/personalities work that dominated sessions 90/91/97.). Five refactors:

1. **`closeSkillEditor` 1-setter callback in `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx`** — A3 page-local modal close pattern that consolidates 3 sites of `() => setEditingSkill(null)` (modal `onClose`, modal Cancel button, load-failure path in `openSkillEditor`'s catch block) into a `useCallback(() => setEditingSkill(null), [])`. Empty deps (useState setters are stable). Same A3 single-setter close pattern that session 100's audit documented (the same one used for `closeAgentModal` / `closeSystemModal` in the cron page).
2. **`closeDelete` 1-setter callback in `src/app/operations/agents/page.tsx`** — A2 (2 sites) page-local modal close pattern that consolidates 2 of the 3 `() => setDeleteTarget(null)` sites (modal `onClose`, modal Cancel button). The 3rd site (the `setDeleteTarget(null);` statement in `handleDelete`'s success path) is intentionally NOT migrated — it lives next to a 2-setter `if (selectedProfileId === target) { setSelectedProfileId(null); setEditor(null); }` block (a 3-setter success block), and threading the target into the helper would force a 3-setter discriminated signature that doesn't fit the 1-setter close pattern. Documented in the JSDoc.
3. **`openAddModel` 1-setter callback in `src/app/config/models/page.tsx`** — A2 (2 sites) callback that consolidates 2 sites of `() => setEditing(null)` (the page header Add Model button + the empty-state CTA inside `ModelsTableSection` via the `onAddModel` prop). The 3rd `setEditing(...)` site (`onEdit={setEditing}`) is a different shape (edit-mode-open with a `ModelEditorRecord` arg) and is left as a direct binding. The 4th `setEditing` site (`onClose={() => setEditing(undefined)}`) is the close form, also left inline.
4. **`ModelEditor` `setSaving(false)` missing-finally bug fix in `src/components/models/ModelEditor.tsx`** — the `handleSubmit` catch block had `setSaving(false)` but the success path did NOT. Currently invisible because the parent's `onSaved()` → `setEditing(undefined)` unmounts the modal — but if the parent ever deferred unmount OR the modal was reused for a 2nd edit without remount, the saving spinner would stay stuck on the success path. Moved `setSaving(false)` into a `finally { ... }` block (the canonical pattern used by `toggleSkill` in skills page and `runFallbackMutation` in `useModelsPage`). Byte-equivalent for all observable parent paths (the unmount happens after the try block, before the user can observe the saving state).
5. **`useModelsPage` verbose `toError(err).message || String(err)` migration to `messageFromError(err, String(err))` in `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts`** — the verbose form was the last remaining `toError` call site in the codebase outside `api-fetch.ts` itself (per the session 90/91 migration carryover). The verbose form is byte-equivalent to the helper form (the body of `messageFromError` IS `toError(err).message || fallback`), so the migration is a name change, not a behavior change. Dropped the `toError` import (the verbose form was the only consumer).

Net: **+474 / -22 lines** (the +474 is dominated by the 5 new test files; the -22 is the 4 inline close-form collapses + 1 import drop + 1 verbose-form collapse). ### Refactor 1 — `closeSkillEditor` 1-setter callback in skills page

The Edit Skill modal has 3 sites of the inline `() => setEditingSkill(null)` form:

| Site | Location | Was | Now |
|------|----------|-----|-----|
| 1 | Modal `onClose` (X-button / overlay click) | `() => setEditingSkill(null)` | `closeSkillEditor` |
| 2 | Modal Cancel button (footer) | `() => setEditingSkill(null)` | `closeSkillEditor` |
| 3 | `openSkillEditor` catch block (load failure) | `setEditingSkill(null);` (statement) | `closeSkillEditor();` (call) |

The page now exposes:

```tsx
const closeSkillEditor = useCallback(() => setEditingSkill(null), []);
```

And uses it at the 3 sites. Empty deps (useState setters are stable). The 4th `setEditingSkill(...)` site at `openSkillEditor` line 187 (`setEditingSkill(skill.name);`) is the OPEN-edit form (different intent), left as a direct setter call.

**Why A3 here is correct:** the 3 sites have the same lifecycle role (close the editor modal). Adding a future "clear editContent on close" or "reset editOriginal" reset is now a 1-place edit, not a 3-place edit. This is the same single-setter close pattern that session 100's audit documented for `closeAgentModal` / `closeSystemModal` / `closeComposer`.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper calls `setEditingSkill(null)` — exactly what the 3 inline sites did. The 5 new tests in `tests/unit/close-skill-editor-setter-callback.test.ts` lock the helper shape (useCallback with empty deps, 1-setter body, 3+ identifier occurrences, 1 inline form in code = the helper itself).

### Refactor 2 — `closeDelete` 1-setter callback in agents page

The Delete Profile modal has 3 sites of `setDeleteTarget(null)`:

| Site | Location | Was | Now |
|------|----------|-----|-----|
| 1 | Modal `onClose` (X-button / overlay click) | `() => setDeleteTarget(null)` | `closeDelete` |
| 2 | Modal Cancel button (footer) | `() => setDeleteTarget(null)` | `closeDelete` |
| 3 | `handleDelete` success path (3-setter block) | `setDeleteTarget(null);` (statement) | **LEFT INLINE** (intentional) |

Sites 1+2 are migrated to `closeDelete`. Site 3 is intentionally NOT migrated — it lives next to:
```ts
setDeleteTarget(null);
if (selectedProfileId === target) {
  setSelectedProfileId(null);
  setEditor(null);
}
```
a 3-setter success block. Threading `target` into the helper (`closeDelete(target: string)`) would force a 3-setter discriminated signature that doesn't fit the 1-setter close pattern. The discriminator between the 1-setter modal close (sites 1+2) and the 3-setter success path (site 3) is the same HARD-vs-SOFT pattern that session 100 locked for the agents page's `closeCreate` 4-setter vs 1-setter Cancel button (session 101's session-100-followup tests).

The 4 new tests in `tests/unit/close-delete-setter-callback.test.ts` lock:
- The helper shape (useCallback with empty deps, 1-setter body)
- The 2-site migration (helper identifier ≥ 3 times, bare-statement `setDeleteTarget(null);` exactly 1 time = the handleDelete success path)
- The 3-setter success block in `handleDelete` (anti-A3 lock — prevents a future "migrate to closeDelete()" PR from breaking the target-conditional)

### Refactor 3 — `openAddModel` 1-setter callback in models page

The Models page has 4 sites of `setEditing(...)`:

| Site | Shape | Intent | Now |
|------|-------|--------|-----|
| 1 | `() => setEditing(null)` (page header Add Model button) | Open CREATE mode | `openAddModel` |
| 2 | `() => setEditing(null)` (ModelsTableSection onAddModel prop) | Open CREATE mode | `openAddModel` |
| 3 | `onEdit={setEditing}` (ModelsTableSection onEdit prop) | Open EDIT mode (passes a `ModelEditorRecord`) | **LEFT AS DIRECT BINDING** |
| 4 | `() => setEditing(undefined)` (Modal onClose) | Close modal | **LEFT INLINE** (different shape: `undefined` ≠ `null`) |

The page now exposes:
```tsx
const openAddModel = useCallback(() => setEditing(null), []);
```

And uses it at sites 1+2. Sites 3+4 are intentionally left as direct setter calls / inline — they have different shapes and different intents.

**Why site 3 is `onEdit={setEditing}` (not `() => setEditing(record)`):** the ModelsTableSection's `onEdit` prop has the signature `(record: ModelEditorRecord) => void`. The page's `setEditing` setter has the same signature (it accepts a `ModelEditorRecord` OR `null` OR `undefined`). Passing the setter directly is a perfect function-pointer binding — no wrapper needed.

**Why site 4 is `() => setEditing(undefined)` (not `() => setEditing(null)`):** the close form must use `undefined` to distinguish "no modal" from "create modal" (`null` means create). Migrating the close form to `openAddModel` would re-OPEN the modal in create mode instead of closing it. Different shape, different intent.

The 4 new tests in `tests/unit/open-add-model-setter-callback.test.ts` lock:
- The helper shape (useCallback with empty deps, 1-setter body, `null` arg)
- The 2-site migration (helper identifier ≥ 3 times, 1 inline form = the helper itself)
- The close-modal `() => setEditing(undefined)` site is preserved (anti-A3 lock)
- The `onEdit={setEditing}` direct binding is preserved (anti-A3 lock)

### Refactor 4 — ModelEditor `setSaving(false)` finally-block bug fix

The `handleSubmit` catch block had `setSaving(false)` but the success path did NOT:

```ts
// Before:
try {
  // ... save ...
  onSaved();
} catch (err) {
  setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Save failed");
  setSaving(false);  // ← only reset on FAILURE
}

// After:
try {
  // ... save ...
  onSaved();
} catch (err) {
  setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Save failed");
} finally {
  // ← reset on BOTH success and failure
  setSaving(false);
}
```

**Why this is a real bug (not just cosmetic):** the current success path relies on the parent's `onSaved()` callback → `setEditing(undefined)` to unmount the modal, which clears the `saving` state as a side effect of unmount. This works in the current code path. But if the parent ever deferred the unmount (e.g. to show a "Saved!" toast before closing), OR if the modal was reused for a 2nd edit without remount, the saving spinner would stay stuck on the success path.

**Why "AT LEAST identical results" still holds:** the only observable difference is the moment the saving state is reset. In the current code, the success path resets `saving` to `false` as a side effect of unmount. In the new code, the success path resets `saving` to `false` in the `finally` block, BEFORE the unmount happens (the `onSaved()` call inside the try block triggers the parent's `setEditing(undefined)` which schedules an unmount, but the `finally` block runs synchronously after the try block returns, before React processes the unmount). Net effect: `saving` is reset to `false` either way, the user never sees the spinner after the save completes. Byte-equivalent for all observable parent paths.

**Canonical pattern:** this is the same shape used by `toggleSkill` in skills page (`try { ... } catch (err) { ... } finally { setToggling((prev) => { ... }) }`) and `runFallbackMutation` in `useModelsPage` (`try { ... } catch (err) { ... } finally { /* setBusy(false) */ }` — well, `runSyncAction` does this in the lib helper, not the hook). The single canonical place to reset the busy flag, not duplicated in success/failure branches.

The 4 new tests in `tests/unit/model-editor-finally-saving.test.ts` lock:
- The catch block contains `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Save failed")` (no regression of the existing migration from session 91)
- A `finally` block contains `setSaving(false)`
- The catch block does NOT contain `setSaving(false)` (the old bug shape)
- The order: `setSaving(true) < onSaved() < catch < finally`
- `setSaving(false)` appears exactly ONCE in the file (the single source of truth invariant)

### Refactor 5 — `useModelsPage` `messageFromError` migration

The auto-import catch block had the verbose `toError(err).message || String(err)` form:

```ts
// Before:
await apiFetch("/api/models/import", { method: "POST" }).catch((err) => {
  const msg = toError(err).message || String(err);
  console.warn("Model auto-import failed — showing cached data:", msg);
});

// After:
await apiFetch("/api/models/import", { method: "POST" }).catch((err) => {
  const msg = messageFromError(err, String(err));
  console.warn("Model auto-import failed — showing cached data:", msg);
});
```

Plus dropped the `toError` import from the `api-fetch` import block (the verbose form was the only consumer).

**Why this is byte-equivalent:** `messageFromError(e, fallback)` is literally `toError(e).message || fallback` (per `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:40`). So `messageFromError(err, String(err))` is identical to `toError(err).message || String(err)`. The migration is a name change, not a behavior change.

**Why this matters:** the verbose form is the only `toError` call site left in the codebase outside `api-fetch.ts` itself (per the session 90/91 migration carryover). The 9 `setError(messageFromError(err, ...))` migrations in session 91 closed the `setError` side, but the `.catch((err) => { ... console.warn(... toError(err).message ...) })` pattern was not on the session 90/91 audit radar. This session closes the last 1 site.

The 4 new tests in `tests/unit/use-models-page-message-from-error.test.ts` lock:
- The auto-import catch block uses `messageFromError(err, String(err))` (not the verbose form)
- The file does NOT contain `toError(err).message` anywhere
- The `toError` import is NOT in the `@/lib/api-fetch` import block
- The `messageFromError` import IS in the `@/lib/api-fetch` import block (regression guard)

### What was audited and rejected

| Candidate | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| Migrating `} catch {` form in `loadSkills` (line 110) and `loadProfiles` (line 130) to `} catch (err) {` + `toastError(showToast, err, "...")` | These sites use the static "Failed to X" toast text deliberately (matches the existing UX). Migrating to `toastError` would change the toast to use the actual error message, which is a behavior change beyond "AT LEAST identical results". The `} catch {` form is the pre-existing convention for static toasts. |
| Migrating `} catch (err) { toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to load file"); }` in agents `openFile` to `} catch {` | Same as above — the toast text is the dynamic error message, which is the current behavior. No change needed. |
| Migrating the 4 `showToast(messageFromError(err, "..."))` sites in `useModelsPage` lines 294, 397, 449, 488 to `toastError(showToast, err, "...")` | All 4 sites already use `toastError`. No change needed. |
| Adding `useCallback` to the agents page's `doSync` function (line 67) | `doSync` is called from 5 inline `void doSync(...)` sites. Each site has a different `successMessage` / `errorMessage` / `body`. The function is already a thin wrapper around `runSyncAction` (session 55's helper). Adding `useCallback` would be cosmetic — no behavioral change. Defer. |
| Migrating the agents page's `handleSave` (line 229) `} catch {` to `} catch (err) {` + dynamic toast | The current static "Failed to save file" toast is intentional. No change. |
| Migrating the personalities page's `loadPersonalities` (line 257) `} catch {` to `} catch (err) {` + dynamic toast | Same as above. Static "Failed to load personalities" toast is intentional. |
| Adding a `closeProfile` callback for the 3 sites of `setSelectedProfileId(profile.id)` in agents page (lines 203, 314) | Lines 203 and 314 are different shapes (one is `setSelectedProfileId(null)` in the delete success path, the other is `setSelectedProfileId(profile.id)` in a click handler). Not a clean A3 — defer. |
| Adding a `closeEditEntry` callback for `setEditingFallbackEntry(null)` in models | Only 1 site (the modal onClose in ModelsPage line 182). Not A3. Defer. |
| Migrating `handleFallbackConfigChange`'s inline `useCallback` body to extract the timer-clearing logic | The 3-line timer-clearing is already a single function. Defer. |
| The `setSaveStatus("saving")` 4-state machine in agents `handleSave` | This is a discriminated UI state machine (`idle | saving | saved | error`), not a setter-pair. No migration candidate. |
| The `confirm("Delete model ...")` call in `useModelsPage.handleDelete` (line 203) | A standard native confirm — not a duplicate of any other confirm call. No migration candidate. |
| The `confirm("Remove this fallback model?")` call in `FallbackChainList` (line 135) | Same as above. The 2 confirm() calls have different prompts, so a `confirmAction(prompt, onConfirm)` helper would be over-engineering for 2 sites with different text. Defer. |
| The `if (!confirm("X")) return; onY();` pattern (2 sites in models components) | Same as above — different text per site, different `onY` callback per site. A shared helper would need `(prompt, onConfirm) => ...` signature, which is a thin wrapper over the native confirm. Rule of Three says defer. |

- **Byte-equivalence audit per session-51 lesson:** all 5 refactors are name/structure changes, not behavior changes:
  - `closeSkillEditor`, `closeDelete`, `openAddModel` are 1-setter `useCallback`s with empty deps — the same function reference as the inline form, just consolidated.
  - The ModelEditor `finally` block runs in the same order as the old `setSaving(false)` in the catch block, just on a different code path (success vs failure). The net observable effect is identical: `saving` is reset to `false` after the try block completes.
  - `messageFromError(err, String(err))` is literally `toError(err).message || String(err)`. The migration is a name change.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"A2/A3 1-setter close callback" pattern** — the 3 helpers added in this session (closeSkillEditor, closeDelete, openAddModel) all follow the same shape: 1-setter `useCallback(..., [])` that wraps a `() => setX(null)` form. The 2+ site rule is the discriminator (A2 = 2 sites, A3 = 3 sites). Both are valid; the session 100 audit's "A3 in the loose sense" (3+ places where the same lifecycle decision is duplicated) is what we're locking here.
2. **"Discriminated close-pattern anti-A3"** — when a modal has a 1-setter close form (X-button / overlay click / Cancel button) AND a 3+ setter success block (save success path), the success block stays inline. Threading the success path's state into the close helper would force a discriminated signature that doesn't fit the 1-setter close pattern. Document in JSDoc and lock with a test that asserts the success block's exact shape.
3. **"Finally-block busy reset" pattern** — when a callback has `setBusy(true)` followed by a try/catch, the `setBusy(false)` MUST live in the finally block, not the catch block. The success path that relies on parent unmount to clear the busy state is a latent bug — if the parent ever defers unmount or reuses the component, the busy state stays stuck.
4. **"`messageFromError` is the only `toError` call site that should exist outside `api-fetch.ts`" pattern** — the verbose `toError(err).message || fallback` form is byte-equivalent to `messageFromError(err, fallback)`, but the helper form has a name and JSDoc. After this session, the only `toError` call sites in the codebase are inside `api-fetch.ts` itself (the helper's implementation). All consumers use `messageFromError`, `toastError`, or `setErrorFromCaught`.

This is the same source-pattern test approach used by `tests/unit/use-missions-page-toolset-prune.test.ts` and `tests/unit/cron-populate-collapse.test.ts`.

### Refactor 1 — `closeCreate` 4-setter callback in `src/app/operations/agents/page.tsx`

The New Agent Profile modal had the same 2-setter-pair shape as the cron modals and the missions `closeComposer`, but extended to 4 setters (the modal also clears 3 form fields on close, not just the visibility flag). The audit grep:

```bash
rg -n "setShowCreate\(false\);.*setCreateName" src/app/operations/agents/
```

returns 2 sites: line 162-165 in `handleCreate.onSuccess` and line 485 in the modal's `onClose` prop. The pattern is identical (same 4 setters, same order, same default values), making it a perfect A3 candidate for the same callback treatment.

The agents page now exposes:

```tsx
const closeCreate = useCallback(() => {
  setShowCreate(false);
  setCreateName("");
  setCreateDescription("");
  setCreateCloneFrom("default");
}, []);
```

And uses it at the 2 sites:

```tsx
// In handleCreate.onSuccess (replaces the inline 4-setter block):
onSuccess: async () => {
  closeCreate();
  await loadProfiles();
},

// In the modal:
<Modal
  open={showCreate}
  onClose={closeCreate}
  title="New Agent Profile"
  ...
>
```

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper calls the 4 setters in the same order, with the same values, as the inline form. The 5 new tests in `tests/unit/close-create-setter-callback.test.ts` lock all 3 contracts (helper shape, deps array, call-site count) so a future "fix" cannot silently change behaviour.

**Why the Cancel button is left as a 1-setter SOFT close (anti-A3):** the modal's Cancel button at line 492 uses `onClick={() => setShowCreate(false)}` — only 1 setter, no form clear. This is a **deliberate UX choice** to preserve the user's in-flight form input if they hit Cancel by accident (and want to re-open the modal and continue). The HARD close (X-button, overlay click, success path) clears the form. Migrating the Cancel button to `closeCreate()` would break this HARD-vs-SOFT distinction by forcing all 3 close paths through the same clearing logic. Session 100's "discriminated close patterns" anti-A3 reference applies: when a SOFT close and a HARD close live on the same modal, they're not duplicates; they're a deliberate UX discriminator.

The 5th test in `close-create-setter-callback.test.ts` (`"preserves the discriminated SOFT close on the Cancel button (1-setter only)"`) locks this invariant — a future "tidy up" that migrates the Cancel button to `closeCreate()` will fail the test, forcing the agent to justify the UX change before merging.

### Refactor 2 — `closeComposer` source-pattern unit tests (session 100 follow-up)

Session 100 added the `closeComposer` helper to `useMissionsPage` and consolidated the page's `handleCloseCreate` callback to use it, but the session was iteration-capped before the unit tests were written. This session writes the missing tests in the same source-pattern style as the existing `tests/unit/use-missions-page-toolset-prune.test.ts` and `tests/unit/cron-populate-collapse.test.ts` (which use file-read + regex-assert to lock shape invariants without coupling to React's `useCallback` behaviour).

The 6 tests in `tests/unit/close-composer-setter-pair.test.ts` lock the helper's contract:

| # | Test | What it locks |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `declares closeComposer as a useCallback that calls both setters in the right order` | The helper body — `setEditingId(null)` then `setShowCreate(false)` in that exact order, inside a `useCallback(..., [])` |
| 2 | `uses empty deps array (useState setters are stable)` | The deps array must be `[]` — adding deps would force unnecessary re-renders |
| 3 | `is returned from the hook's public API (returned in the destructure object)` | The helper must be in the hook's return object so the page can consume it as `vm.closeComposer` |
| 4 | `replaces both inline 2-setter sites in handleCreate success branches` | The 2 success branches of `handleCreate` must call `closeComposer()` — no inline form |
| 5 | `page consumes the helper as vm.closeComposer (not the inline 2-setter form)` | The page must NOT contain the inline 2-setter form, AND must bind `closeComposer` from the hook |
| 6 | `page threads the helper to 3 onClose sites (Sheet + footer + form)` | The 3 `onClose` call sites (Sheet, MissionComposerActions footer, MissionCreateForm) all use the same `handleCloseCreate` reference |

The source-pattern test approach was chosen over importing the hook for two reasons:

1. **`closeComposer` is not exported** — it's a hook-internal helper returned from the public `useMissionsPage` API. Importing the hook to test it would require a full React render harness, which is overkill for a 2-setter pure function.
2. **The byte-equivalence claim is about the *shape* of the helper** (which setters it calls, in which order, with which values), not about React's `useCallback` behaviour. Source-pattern tests lock the shape invariant without coupling to React internals.

Migrating would require changing `} catch {` to `} catch (err) {` which is a shape change. The static "Failed to X" message is intentional (matches the existing UX). |
