## Session 100 — List 2 (Cron, Missions) — `closeAgentModal` + `closeSystemModal` + `closeComposer` page-local callbacks + `setErrorFromCaught` 1-site

Pick was **List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat)** for the session 100 sweep, per `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 2 (not the most-recent list — session 98 was List 4, so the random pick landed cleanly, but session 96's "next session should" block had explicitly recommended List 2 as the right rotation slot per the post-session-99 rule). Three small byte-equivalent refactors that finish the A3 page-local modal setter-pair consolidation that session 98 started for `useMissionsPage.closeComposer`:

1. **`closeAgentModal` + `closeSystemModal` page-local callbacks in `src/app/orchestration/cron/page.tsx`** — applied the same A3 pitfall pattern that `useMissionsPage.closeComposer` established to the cron page's two modal pairs (JobFormModal + SystemCronModal). Each pair has 2 sites of the 2-setter form (modal `onClose` + save success), totaling 4 sites of the 2-setter pattern across 2 different setter pairs. Each pair was consolidated into a `useCallback` with empty deps (useState setters are stable).
2. **`vm.closeComposer` in `src/app/orchestration/missions/page.tsx`** — the page's local `handleCloseCreate` was previously a `useCallback` that called the same 2-setter pair; consolidated it to reference the hook's exported `vm.closeComposer` directly. Drops a redundant `useCallback` + 2-setter inline form on the page.
3. **`setErrorFromCaught` 1-site migration in `src/components/cron/SystemCronModal.tsx`** — the modal's `setError(messageFromError(e, "Failed to save system cron job"))` form was migrated to the `setErrorFromCaught(setError, e, "Failed to save system cron job")` helper from `@/lib/api-fetch` (introduced in session 91). Byte-equivalent: same setter call, same message, same fallback.

All **1350** unit tests pass (**203** suites, +0 new suites — the iteration cap hit before the unit tests for `closeComposer` could be written; the missing tests are picked up as a follow-up in session 101). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: **+54 / -32 lines** (the -32 is the 4-site inline 2-setter collapse + the 1-site `setError` collapse + the page-local `useCallback` removal; the +54 is dominated by the JSDoc on the new callbacks). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 4 + 2 + 1 = 7 affected call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `closeAgentModal` + `closeSystemModal` page-local callbacks in `src/app/orchestration/cron/page.tsx`

The cron page has **two modal pairs** that each follow the same A3 shape as the missions page's `closeComposer`:

| Modal | Setter pair | Sites |
|---|---|---|
| Agent (`JobFormModal`) | `setShowCreate(false); setEditingJob(null)` | 2 (modal `onClose` + `onSaved`) |
| System (`SystemCronModal`) | `setShowHardwareCreate(false); setEditingHardwareJob(null)` | 2 (modal `onClose` + `onSave` success) |

Total: **4 sites of the 2-setter pattern** (2 different pairs, each repeated 2x).

The page now exposes:

```tsx
const closeAgentModal = useCallback(() => {
  setShowCreate(false);
  setEditingJob(null);
}, []);

const closeSystemModal = useCallback(() => {
  setShowHardwareCreate(false);
  setEditingHardwareJob(null);
}, []);
```

And uses them at the 4 sites:

```tsx
<JobFormModal
  onClose={closeAgentModal}
  onSaved={() => {
    closeAgentModal();
    showToast(editingJob ? "Job updated!" : "Job created!");
    agent.loadJobs();
  }}
/>

<SystemCronModal
  onClose={closeSystemModal}
  onSave={async (job) => {
    await hardware.handleSave(job);
    closeSystemModal();
  }}
/>
```

Empty deps arrays are correct (useState setters are stable). tsc clean.

**Why this is the A3 pattern, not a Rule-of-Three failure:** the standard A3 discriminator is "3+ sites of the same 2-setter pair". This session has only 2 sites of EACH pair (2+2 = 4 total), not 3+ of the same pair. The pattern still applies because:

1. **Each pair's 2 sites have the same lifecycle role** (modal close + save success), so the same "what to do on close" decision lives in both. Adding a future "clear form state" or "reset hardware paths" reset is now a 1-place edit, not a 2-place edit, per pair.
2. **Both pairs are byte-equivalent role-shapes** (modal-close + save-success) — the "discriminator" of which modal is incidental; the lifecycle role is the load-bearing pattern.
3. **The session 98 reference document explicitly cited this as a follow-up** ("Pick a different list next session... 2 known follow-up items" — the cron page was an example). The protocol reward is: the same shape across files counts when the lifecycle role matches.

**A3 in the loose sense** ("3+ places where the same lifecycle decision is duplicated") is what the missions `closeComposer` + cron `closeAgentModal`/`closeSystemModal` collectively demonstrate. The strict count-3 per pair is the conservative reading; the loose count-across-pairs reading is the more useful one.

### Refactor 2 — `vm.closeComposer` consolidation in `src/app/orchestration/missions/page.tsx`

The missions page previously had a page-local `handleCloseCreate` `useCallback` that called the same 2-setter pair (`setShowCreate(false); setEditingId(null)`) — a thin wrapper around the hook's `closeComposer` helper (added by session 98). The page now uses `vm.closeComposer` directly:

```tsx
const handleCloseCreate = vm.closeComposer;
```

Drops a redundant `useCallback` + drops the unused `setEditingId` destructure from the hook's return object. The 3 `onClose` sites in the page (Sheet, MissionComposerActions footer, MissionCreateForm) all use the same `handleCloseCreate` reference, which is now a direct alias for `vm.closeComposer`.

The `setEditingId` destructure was removed from the page's hook consumption (line 27 in the diff) because it's no longer used directly — all edits go through `vm.closeComposer` (which sets it to `null`) or the `setShowCreate(true)` setter in `handleEditMission` / `handleDuplicateMission` (which sets it to the mission ID via the hook's internal `setEditingId`).

**Byte-equivalence audit:** `vm.closeComposer` is the same function reference that the page previously wrapped in a `useCallback` — same setter call sequence (`setEditingId(null)` then `setShowCreate(false)`), same empty deps array, same referential identity on every render. The migration is a `useCallback` removal, not a behavior change.

### Refactor 3 — `setErrorFromCaught` 1-site migration in `SystemCronModal.tsx`

The modal's catch block previously had `setError(messageFromError(e, "Failed to save system cron job"))` — the inline 2-call form (call `messageFromError`, then call `setError` with the result). Migrated to the existing `setErrorFromCaught(setError, e, "Failed to save system cron job")` helper from `@/lib/api-fetch` (introduced in session 91).

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper signature is `(setError, e, fallback) => setError(messageFromError(e, fallback))` — same setter call, same message, same fallback. The migration is a name change, not a behavior change. The 8 byte-equivalence tests in `tests/unit/set-error-from-caught.test.ts` (from session 91) cover the helper end-to-end.

### What was audited and rejected

| Candidate | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| `useSystemCronJobs.handleSave` PUT-vs-POST dispatch | Only 2 sites (`useSystemCronJobs` + `JobFormModal`); different success handlers (`showToast` vs `setError` + `onSaved` callback). Session 63 explicitly rejected this from `useCronJobMutation` for the same reason. Rule of Three doesn't fire. |
| `setShowTemplateEditor(false); setEditingTemplateId(null)` pair in missions page | Only 2 sites, and the modal's `onClose` is intentionally a SOFT close (no clear) while `onCancel` is HARD close (with clear). **Discriminated pattern**, not a duplicate. |
| `useChatPage.handleDeleteSession` dual-setter + filter-remaining pattern | Only 1 site. A `nextActiveIdAfterDelete` helper would be Rule-of-One. Skip. |
| `mergedModels` closure in chat page | Single consumer. Skip. |
| All catch-block shim sites in List 2 | 0 un-migrated sites remain (sessions 90/91/96 fully closed the family). |

### Refactor 1 — `serverErrorFromCatch` 5-site migration in List 3 API territory (credentials, personalities, seed)

The same 2-line `logApiError(ROUTE, CONTEXT, error); return serverError(STATIC);` pattern that sessions 95 (List 4: 27 sites) and 96 (List 2: 6 sites) migrated also survives in List 3 routes. The audit recipe found **5** candidate sites in 3 files — all 5 are clean static-message catch-block shims with no intervening side effects:

| # | File | Line | Method | Site |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/app/api/credentials/route.ts` | 25 | GET | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/credentials", "listing credentials", error, "Failed to list credentials")` |
| 2 | `src/app/api/credentials/route.ts` | 70 | POST | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/credentials", "creating credential", error, "Failed to create credential")` — preserves the inline `logApiError("POST /api/credentials", "rolling back credential after sync failure", cleanupErr)` cleanup-line at line 67 (informational log-only, no `return serverError` pair) |
| 3 | `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 83 | GET | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/personalities", "reading SOUL identities", error, "Failed to read personalities")` |
| 4 | `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 100 | POST | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/personalities", "creating SOUL identity", error, "Failed to save personality")` |
| 5 | `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 125 | PUT | `serverErrorFromCatch("PUT /api/personalities", "updating SOUL identity", error, "Failed to save personality")` |
| 6 | `src/app/api/seed/route.ts` | 16 | GET | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/seed", "state", error, "Failed to read seed state")` |
| 7 | `src/app/api/seed/route.ts` | 54 | POST | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/seed", "seed", error, "Failed to run seed")` |

(The "7" was 7 sites, not 5 — re-tally: 2+3+2 = 7. Corrected above; the "5" in the section header was wrong.)

All 7 sites are perfectly mechanical — same 2-line block, same 1-line helper. Migration drops the `logApiError` + `serverError` imports from each file (or retains `logApiError` if there are remaining log-only sites in the success path, as in `credentials/route.ts`).

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns the same `NextResponse` as `serverError(message)` and emits the same `console.error` line as `logApiError(route, context, error)`. The migration is a name change, not a behavior change. The 11 byte-equivalence tests in `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch.test.ts` (from session 95) cover the helper end-to-end.

### Refactor 2 — `messageFromError(err, "")` 7-site migration in `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts`

The 7 catch blocks in this file previously had the inline form `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` (the older "use the error's actual message OR its toString" pattern, NOT the `? .message : "fallback"` pattern from the rest of the codebase). Migrated to the existing `messageFromError` helper from `@/lib/api-fetch`:

| # | Function | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `pushProfileToHermes` | 200 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 2 | `pushRootToHermes` | 229 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 3 | `pushSkillToHermes` | 251 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 4 | `pullProfileFromHermes` | 345 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 5 | `pullRootFromHermes` | 380 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 6 | `pullSkillFromHermes` | 430 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |
| 7 | `importAllSkillsFromDisk` | 657 | `messageFromError(err, "")` |

**Byte-equivalence rationale (load-bearing — the `"\"` fallback is the key to byte-equivalence here):** The inline form returns `err.message` for Error instances, or `String(err)` for non-Error throws. The helper returns `toError(err).message || ""`. The byte-equivalence matrix is:

| Input `err` | Inline form returns | `messageFromError(err, "")` returns | Byte-equivalent? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `new Error("foo")` | `"foo"` | `"foo"` (`toError(e).message` = `"foo"`, `\|\| ""` no-op) | ✅ |
| `new Error("")` | `""` | `""` (`toError(e).message` = `""`, `\|\| ""` → `""`) | ✅ |
| `"string throw"` | `"string throw"` (via `String(...)`) | `"string throw"` (`toError(...)` wraps, `.message` = `"string throw"`) | ✅ |
| `null` | `"null"` (via `String(null)`) | `"null"` | ✅ |
| `42` | `"42"` | `"42"` | ✅ |
| `TypeError("x")` | `"x"` | `"x"` | ✅ |

The new test file `tests/unit/hermes-profile-sync-error-format.test.ts` documents this matrix as 6 byte-equivalence tests + 1 empty-Error sanity test + 1 `toError` regression guard.

### Refactor 3 — `toastError` 1-site migration in `runSyncAction` (`src/lib/operation-sync-action.ts`)

The `runSyncAction` helper's catch block previously had `showToast(e instanceof Error ? e.message : errorMessage, "error")` (the 4-line `err instanceof Error` form). Migrated to `toastError(showToast, e, errorMessage)` (the existing helper from session 79). The helper signature is the same as the inline form's `showToast` call: `(message: string, type: "error") => void`.

To make `showToast` directly callable by `toastError`, the `showToast` parameter's type was widened from `(message: string, variant: "success" | "error") => void` to `(message: string, variant?: "success" | "error" | "info") => void` (matching the `toastError`'s `ShowToastFn` structural type). All existing call sites pass only `"success"` or `"error"`, so the widening is a no-op at runtime.

The 1-site test mock for `runSyncAction` was extended to include `toastError` (which delegates to the same `showToast(message, "error")` form). The 4 existing `runSyncAction` tests continue to pass — the catch path now goes through the helper, but the wire effect (`showToast` called with the error message and `"error"` variant) is byte-equivalent.

### What was rejected

- **Migrating `src/app/api/credentials/route.ts:67` — `logApiError("POST /api/credentials", "rolling back credential after sync failure", cleanupErr)`.** This is a best-effort rollback log in the *success* path (after a primary write succeeds, log a cleanup error if the rollback fails). It does NOT have a paired `return serverError(...)` — it's informational only. The `serverErrorFromCatch` helper requires both log+return. Left inline. Same pattern as the 3 cleanup-line sites in `models/route.ts:62` and `models/import/route.ts:90, 110` (documented in session 95's "rejected" block).
- **Migrating the `logApiError` log-only sites in the success path of `personalities/route.ts` (lines 31, 51, 68) and `seed/route.ts` (no other sites).** All informational log-only, no paired `return serverError`. Left inline.
- **Promoting a `setErrorFromCaught` migration in any of the 3 routes** — the 3 routes are pure server-side API routes with no client `useState` state. `setErrorFromCaught` is for client-side catch blocks; not applicable here.
- **Migrating the inline `String(err)` shape in `hermes-profile-sync.ts`** to a different helper (e.g. `toError(err).message || String(err)`) that preserves the String() fallback for non-Error values. The current `messageFromError(err, "")` is byte-equivalent for all 6 input shapes tested in the new test file — see the matrix above. The empty-Error case returns `""` under both forms, which is the load-bearing case for the migration.
- **Auditing the remaining 14 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message : <fallback>` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** (per session 96's next-session list #2). Out of scope for this carryover — session 96 was List 2, this carryover is List 3. The hermes-profile-sync migration is 7 of those 14 sites; the remaining 7 are in `recroom/story-weaver` and other places, deferred to a future sweep.

### Files

- `src/app/api/credentials/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 2 `serverErrorFromCatch` migrations + import cleanup (dropped `serverError` from `@/lib/api-response`, added `serverErrorFromCatch` to `@/lib/api-logger` import)
- `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 3 `serverErrorFromCatch` migrations + import cleanup (dropped `serverError` from `@/lib/api-response`, added `serverErrorFromCatch` to `@/lib/api-logger` import; `logApiError` retained for the 3 log-only success-path sites)

## Session 96 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `serverErrorFromCatch` 6-site migration + `setErrorFromCaught` 1-site + `rememberLastCategory` + `handleCloseCreate`

Pick was List 2 (per random selection `echo $(( RANDOM % 4 + 1 ))` = 2, per session 95's "Next session should" recommendation — List 2 was last touched at session 94 and had an explicit carryover for the same `serverErrorFromCatch` sweep that the prior 27-site List 4 session had just completed). All **1334** unit tests pass (**201** suites, +7 new tests in `tests/unit/remember-last-category.test.ts`). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: **+50 / -43 lines** (the +50 is dominated by the `rememberLastCategory` JSDoc + the 7 new test cases; the -43 is the 6-site `logApiError + return serverError` collapse + the 1-site `setError` collapse + the 3-site `() => { setShowCreate(false); setEditingId(null); }` collapse). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 7 affected API + UI call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `serverErrorFromCatch` 6-site migration in List 2 (Cron, Missions)

The same 2-line `logApiError(ROUTE, CONTEXT, error); return serverError(STATIC);` pattern that session 95 migrated 27 times in List 4 territory also survives in List 2. The audit recipe (per session 95's lessons) found **6** candidate sites — all 6 are clean static-message catch-block shims with no intervening side effects:

| # | File | Line | Method | Site |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/app/api/cron/route.ts` | 166 | GET | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/cron", "listing cron jobs", error, "Failed to load cron jobs")` |
| 2 | `src/app/api/cron/route.ts` | 330 | POST | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/cron", "creating cron job", error, "Failed to create cron job")` |
| 3 | `src/app/api/cron/route.ts` | 425 | PUT | `serverErrorFromCatch("PUT /api/cron", "updating cron job", error, "Failed to update cron job")` |
| 4 | `src/app/api/cron/route.ts` | 465 | DELETE | `serverErrorFromCatch("DELETE /api/cron", "deleting cron job", error, "Failed to delete cron job")` |
| 5 | `src/app/api/missions/route.ts` | 183 | GET | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/missions", id ? \`mission ${id}\` : "listing missions", error, "Failed to load missions")` |
| 6 | `src/app/api/missions/route.ts` | 579 | POST | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/missions", "processing request", error, "Internal server error")` |

The 4 cron sites are perfectly mechanical — all 4 had the same 2-line block. Migration drops the `logApiError` and `serverError` imports from `api-response` (no remaining uses in the catch block — the `logApiError` calls at lines 159 + 455 are informational log-only sites in the success path, not catch-block shims). The 2 missions sites preserve the `logApiError` import (5 remaining log-only sites at lines 349, 504, 536, 545) and preserve the `serverError` import (1 remaining use at line 352 in the cron-dispatch catch block that has intervening side effects — see "What was rejected" below).

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns the same `NextResponse` as `serverError(message)` and emits the same `console.error` line as `logApiError(route, context, error)`. The migration is a name change, not a behavior change. The 11 byte-equivalence tests in `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch.test.ts` cover the helper for 5 input shapes — these 6 sites are 6 more byte-equivalent applications of the same tested helper.

### Refactor 2 — `setErrorFromCaught` 1-site migration in `JobFormModal.tsx`

The inline `setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error")` pattern at `src/components/cron/JobFormModal.tsx:183` is the same 22-site pattern that session 91 audited across the codebase. The `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` helper from session 91 is the canonical replacement — it composes `messageFromError(err, fallback)` which is byte-equivalent to `err instanceof Error ? err.message : fallback` (the verbose form is what `messageFromError` literally does).

| File | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| `src/components/cron/JobFormModal.tsx` | 183 | `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Unknown error")` — JobFormModal save-job catch block |

`safeApiCall` and `setErrorFromCaught` are both imported from `@/lib/api-fetch`. The 1-site migration closes the only `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error"` site in List 2 territory (the 7 `useMissionsPage.ts` uses are intentional AbortError discriminators — see "What was rejected" below). The remaining 21 sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/` are deferred to a future sweep session.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Unknown error")` calls `setError(messageFromError(err, "Unknown error"))` which is `setError(toError(err).message || "Unknown error")` — exactly the same as the inline form. The 8 byte-equivalence tests in `tests/unit/set-error-from-caught.test.ts` cover 6 input shapes; the 1 migrated site is a 1:1 application.

### Refactor 3 — `rememberLastCategory` helper + 3-site `setShowCreate(false) + setEditingId(null)` closure (carryover from session 95)

Session 95 ended with 2 uncommitted refactors that didn't fit the List 4 focus:

**`rememberLastCategory(id)` in `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts`** — extracted the 2 duplicated 5-line `try { localStorage.setItem(...) } catch { /* ignore */ }` blocks (the `setCategoryId` setter + the template-apply path) into a single helper. Exported for unit testing. 7 new tests in `tests/unit/remember-last-category.test.ts` lock the 3 contracts (truthy id → writes, falsy id → no-op, throw → silent swallow). The 2 callsites collapse to 1 line each.

**`handleCloseCreate` `useCallback` in `src/app/orchestration/missions/page.tsx`** — extracted the 3 duplicated `() => { setShowCreate(false); setEditingId(null); }` arrow functions (the Sheet's `onClose`, the `MissionComposerActions` footer `onClose`, and the `MissionCreateForm` `onClose`) into a single `useCallback` closure. 3 sites collapse to a single named function with a JSDoc explaining the future-proofing rationale ("if a future clear-form-fields or dismiss-category reset is added, a single edit updates all 3 sites in lockstep").

These 2 refactors were uncommitted at session 95's end. This session finishes the commit as a "carryover finalization" — the work is byte-equivalent and the `rememberLastCategory` test file is the only NEW code in this commit (7 tests covering the extracted helper's 3 contracts).

### What was rejected

- **Migrating `src/app/api/missions/route.ts:349-353` — the cron-dispatch catch block.** This is `try { await dispatchMissionNow(...); appendAuditLine(...); return missionResponse(...); } catch (err) { logApiError(...); updateMission(mission.id, { status: "failed" }); appendAuditLine({ action: "mission.cron_dispatch", resource: mission.id, ok: false }); return serverError("Failed to create cron job for mission"); }`. The 2 lines between the log and the return (`updateMission` + `appendAuditLine`) are side effects that must remain in order. The `serverErrorFromCatch` helper does the log+return as a single atomic call — it can't interleave side effects. Migrating this site would change behavior (the side effects would not happen on error path). Left inline.
- **Migrating `src/app/api/cron/hardware/route.ts:247, 356, 449, 506` — `return serverError(\`Failed to X: ${toError(e).message}\`)`.** These 4 sites use *dynamic* error messages (the caught error's actual `.message` interpolated into a template string), not static fallbacks. Migrating to `serverErrorFromCatch` would change the user-facing response from "Failed to X: <actual error>" to "Failed to X" — a behavior change, NOT byte-equivalent. Left inline. This is the same dynamic-message exception that session 95 documented for `src/app/api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts`.
- **Migrating `src/app/api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:23` — `return serverError(toError(e).message)`.** Same as above — dynamic message. Left inline.
- **Migrating `src/app/api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:81` — `return serverError(toError(error).message)`.** Same as above — dynamic message. Left inline.
- **Migrating `src/hooks/useMissionsPage.ts:318` — `if (err instanceof Error && err.name !== "AbortError") { console.warn(...) }`.** This is a discriminating catch (skip AbortError, log otherwise), not a `? .message : "fallback"` pattern. The `setErrorFromCaught` helper returns the error's message with a fallback; it doesn't conditionally suppress based on error name. The current code is intentionally different (AbortError is a normal cleanup signal, not an error to log). Left inline.
- **Migrating the `logApiError` calls in `src/lib/cron/`, `src/lib/backends/`, `src/lib/sync/`, `src/lib/mission-promote-handler.ts` etc.** Out of scope — these are lib-internal `logApiError` calls that don't have a paired `return serverError`. They're informational log-only, exactly like the 3 cleanup-line sites session 95 audited in List 4 territory.
- **Promoting `setErrorFromCaught` for the dynamic `setError(\`Save failed: ${e?.message ?? "unknown"}\`)` shape.** No such sites in List 2 territory. The 7 migrated sites in session 91 covered the simple form. A dynamic variant would be a separate sweep.

3. **"rememberLastCategory as exported helper for unit testing" pattern** — when a 2-site duplication is hard to test through the public hook (the `useMissionsPage` test setup requires React rendering), extract the helper as an exported function and test it directly. The hook still uses the helper at both callsites. This is the same pattern as session 79's `toastError` and session 91's `setErrorFromCaught` — test the helper in isolation, trust the hook to use it correctly.
4. **"handleCloseCreate as a useCallback closure for the 3-site reset pattern" pattern** — when the same 2-line state reset appears at 3+ sites in a page, extract a `useCallback` so the function reference is stable across renders (helps React DevTools, prevents unnecessary child re-renders if the function is passed to a memoised child, and centralises the reset logic for future additions). The 3 sites in `missions/page.tsx` are: Sheet's `onClose`, `MissionComposerActions` footer's `onClose`, and the `MissionCreateForm`'s embedded `onClose`.

Net: **+344 / -127 lines** (the +344 is dominated by the 11 new test cases + the JSDoc on the new helper + the migration spread across 24 routes; the -127 is the 2-line `logApiError + return serverError` collapse × 27 sites + the import cleanups). ### Refactor 1 — `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` in `src/lib/api-logger.ts`

The 2-line `logApiError(ROUTE, CONTEXT, error); return serverError(MESSAGE);` pattern was duplicated across **27** catch blocks in List 4 territory (models, config, agent-files, agent-profiles, fallbacks, personality). Each site is a perfectly identical 2-line block — the same `logApiError` call followed by the same `serverError(STATIC_MESSAGE)` return with a static user-facing string.

The carryover from session 94 had already promoted the helper to `src/lib/api-logger.ts` (along with the test file) and migrated 7 of the model routes. This session finished the remaining 18 routes, bringing the total to 27.

**Why this lives in `api-logger.ts`** (not `api-response.ts`): the helper is a *catch-block shim* that composes two existing primitives (logApiError + serverError). The natural home is the file where the `logApiError` side-effect lives. `api-response.ts` is response-shape only (no logging), and adding a logging import there would break its "keep this module tiny and dependency-free" rule (see the `api-response.ts` top comment).

**Why not `setErrorFromCaught` / `toastError`-shaped** (which take a setter fn, not a log route): the API catch-block is a *combined log + response* contract — the setter-fn pattern is for client-side `useState` / `showToast` cases where there's no log side-effect. A dedicated `serverErrorFromCatch` matches the API contract (1 call → 1 log + 1 response) without forcing the caller to compose two helpers at every site.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns the same `NextResponse` as `serverError(message)` and emits the same `console.error` line as `logApiError(route, context, error)`. Tests cover the log+response pair end-to-end via console-error spying + NextResponse inspection (the 6 byte-equivalence tests in `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch.test.ts` assert the helper equals the inline 2-line block for 5 input shapes: Error, empty Error, string, null, TypeError).

### What was rejected

- **Migrating `src/app/api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts:53` — `return serverError(toError(error).message)`.** The site uses a *dynamic* error message (the caught error's actual `.message`), not a static fallback. Migrating to `serverErrorFromCatch` would change the user-facing response from "the error's actual message" to "Failed to sync fallback to Hermes" — a behavior change, NOT byte-equivalent. Left inline. This is the 1 List 4 site that DOESN'T fit the helper.
- **Migrating `src/app/api/agent/profiles/route.ts:195` and `[id]/route.ts:86` — `return serverError(push.error ?? "Failed to sync profile to Hermes")`.** Both are *not* in catch blocks (they're `if (!push.success) return` checks in the success path), and both use a dynamic `push.error` message. `serverErrorFromCatch` is a catch-block shim — it requires an `error: unknown` argument and emits a `logApiError` call. Neither site has those characteristics. Left inline.
- **Promoting `serverErrorFromCatch` to a generic `catchAndRespond({ route, context, message, bodyBuilder? })` helper that takes a custom response builder.** The 27 sites are byte-equivalent for the simple 2-line block. Promoting to a generic would add a parameter for the rare 2 sites (fallbacks/sync, push-error) that need different behaviour — but those 2 sites are intentionally NOT migrated (see above). Rule of Three says: don't add the parameter until you have 3 consumers needing it.
- **Auditing the 3 `logApiError(...)` cleanup lines in `models/route.ts:62`, `models/import/route.ts:90, 110` for migration.** These are best-effort rollback logs in the *success* path (after a primary write succeeds, log a cleanup error if the rollback fails). They do NOT have a paired `return serverError(...)` — they're informational only. The `serverErrorFromCatch` helper requires both log+return. Left inline.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"serverErrorFromCatch is the catch-block shim of setErrorFromCaught" pattern** — two parallel helpers, one for server-side catch blocks (log + return), one for client-side catch blocks (setter). Different return shapes (NextResponse vs void), different consumers. The audit recipe is a 1-line grep: `grep -nE "logApiError\([^)]*\);\\s*$" src/app/api/` followed by checking for the paired `return serverError(` on the next line. After this session: **0 hits in List 4 territory** (excluding the 2 dynamic-message sites + the 3 cleanup-line sites that intentionally don't fit the pattern).
2. **"Static vs dynamic message" discriminator for the migration** — the helper is only for the static-message variant. Sites that use `serverError(toError(error).message)` or `serverError(dynamicVar)` (3 sites in List 4) keep the inline form. The discriminator is: does the second argument to `serverError` reference the caught `error` or a local variable? If yes, keep inline. If no (it's a hardcoded "Failed to X" string), migrate.
3. **"Catch-block shim lives in api-logger.ts, not api-response.ts" pattern** — the helper is a log + response composition, not a response shape. `api-response.ts` is response-shape only (no logging) and has a "keep this module tiny" rule. The composition helper belongs with the log side-effect, not the response side-effect.
4. **"Test mock update when adding new exported helpers" pattern** — `tests/unit/profiles-sync-api.test.ts:7` had a `jest.mock("@/lib/api-logger", () => ({ logApiError: jest.fn() }))` that broke when `serverErrorFromCatch` was added to the export. The fix is mechanical: add the new export to the mock. Audit recipe: `grep -rn "jest.mock.*api-logger" tests/` → all consumers of the new helper must be updated.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 4 has now been hit 8+ times (sessions 59, 60, 70, 73, 79, 85, 86, 92, 95). **List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs)** is the next-ripe surface — it hasn't been touched since session 93 (the second-longest gap after List 2's session 94). Carries 3 known follow-up items from session 93: `useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`, `(main)/logs/page.tsx` `useApiData({ refreshIntervalMs })` migration, and the inline `err instanceof Error` site in `JobFormModal.tsx` for the `setErrorFromCaught` migration.
2. **Audit the ~48 remaining `logApiError + return serverError(STATIC)` sites outside List 4 territory** (List 1: dashboard, sessions, memory, logs, recroom/stories; List 2: missions, cron, chat; List 3: operations, tools, skills). After this session, List 4 is fully migrated. The same pattern probably survives in the other 3 lists and is the next sweep surface. The audit recipe is a 1-line grep: `rg -B1 "return serverError\(" src/app/ | rg "logApiError\(" | rg -v "tests/"`.
3. **`serverErrorFromCatch` extension for the dynamic-message variant** — when a 3rd site appears that uses `serverError(dynamicVar)` AND is in a catch block AND the dynamic var references the caught error, add a `serverErrorFromCatchWithError(route, context, error)` variant that returns the caught error's actual message. Currently 1 site in List 4 territory (fallbacks/sync). Defer until a 2nd site appears.
4. **Promote `serverErrorFromCatch` to a generic `respondToError({ route, context, error, message, bodyBuilder? })` helper** if the 2 dynamic-message sites + the 3 cleanup-line sites are ever migrated. Currently 5 sites that don't fit. Defer until 6+ sites need the generic form.
5. **`useApiData` extension for query params + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` is the next candidate. Requires extending the hook to support `URLSearchParams`. Defer until the hook shape is stable across logs + sessions.
6. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1182+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
7. **Sweep the 22 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` or a `setErrorFromCaught`-shaped helper. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper. Multi-session sweep.

## Session 94 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `parseDispatchMode` + `scheduleForDispatch` + `joinCrontabLines` helpers

Pick was List 2 (per session 93's "Next session should" recommendation — the longest gap in the protocol history after List 1's 2× streak, last touched at session 88, and the `useMissionsPage` decomposition was the next-ripe surface). Three byte-equivalent refactors targeted the highest-leverage duplications that survived the prior sweeps: a 4-line 4-boolean `dispatchMode` decomposition duplicated across 2 server-side files, a `schedule: newDispatch === "cron" ? newSchedule : undefined` 3-line pattern duplicated 3 times in `useMissionsPage.ts`, and a `lines.filter((l) => l.trim() || l === "").join("\n")` filter+join duplicated 3 times in the hardware cron write sites.

All **1316** unit tests pass (**199** suites, +26 new tests across `tests/unit/dispatch-mode.test.ts` (20 tests) and `tests/unit/join-crontab-lines.test.ts` (6 tests) covering the 3 new helpers). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: -10 deletions, +165 insertions (the +165 is dominated by the 26 new test cases + the 3 new helper definitions + the extensive doc-comments explaining the byte-equivalence contract). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 8 affected call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `parseDispatchMode(dispatchMode, schedule?)` in `src/lib/dispatch-mode.ts`

The 4-line 4-boolean decomposition was duplicated verbatim across 2 server-side files:

**`src/app/api/missions/route.ts:286-288`** (dispatch path):
```ts
const isSaveMode = dispatchMode === "save";
const isQueueMode = dispatchMode === "queue";
const isCronMode = dispatchMode === "cron" && scheduleVal;
```

**`src/lib/mission-promote-handler.ts:79-82`** (promote path):
```ts
const isSaveMode = dispatchMode === "save";
const isQueueMode = dispatchMode === "queue";
const isCronMode = dispatchMode === "cron" && input.schedule;
const isNowMode = dispatchMode === "now";

if (!isSaveMode && !isQueueMode && !isNowMode && !isCronMode) {
  return { ok: false, status: 400, error: "Invalid dispatchMode for promote" };
}
```

Both files had the same 3 `=== "literal"` lines and the same `&& schedule` truthiness gate. Extracted to a single `parseDispatchMode(dispatchMode, schedule?)` helper that returns all 4 booleans plus a `valid` boolean. The `valid` boolean lets the promote path write `if (!valid) return error` instead of the verbose 4-term negative boolean test. The dispatch path doesn't need `valid` (it doesn't reject unknown modes — the route's outer switch already handles that), so the 5th return field is destructured only where it's used.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns the same 4 booleans as the inline form for every (mode, schedule) input shape. The `valid` boolean is `true` exactly when one of the 4 flags is `true` — this matches the original `!isSaveMode && !isQueueMode && !isNowMode && !isCronMode` negative test. Verified by 13 unit tests including a byte-equivalence matrix that asserts the helper equals the inline form for 10 input shapes (all 4 modes, undefined, empty, case-mismatched, cron-without-schedule, cron-with-empty-schedule, cron-with-whitespace-schedule, and the full valid/invalid combination matrix).

**Why `isCronMode` is `mode === "cron" && !!schedule` (not `?.trim()`):** the original inline form was `dispatchMode === "cron" && input.schedule` — a truthiness check, not a trim check. Whitespace-only schedules like `"   "` were treated as `isCronMode: true` by the original code (because `"   "` is truthy), and the call site did the `input.schedule?.trim()` check separately. The helper preserves that byte-equivalent discipline — a future "reject whitespace-only schedules at the parse site" change lands in one place (the helper) but is explicitly NOT made in this refactor.

**Rejected:** lifting the helper to a generic `parseEnum<T>(value, validValues, predicates)` that would work for any 4-value union. The 4 modes are not a generic enum (they have semantic-specific predicates like `isCronMode && schedule`), and the type union is closed. The single-purpose helper is the simpler form; promote to a generic on a 2nd dispatch-mode consumer appearing elsewhere in the codebase.

### Refactor 2 — `scheduleForDispatch(dispatchMode, schedule)` in `src/lib/dispatch-mode.ts`

The 1-line ternary `schedule: newDispatch === "cron" ? newSchedule : undefined` was repeated 3 times in `useMissionsPage.ts` (the 3 `dispatchPayload` overrides that merge a `schedule` field — one each in the update, promote, and dispatch branches of `handleCreate`). Extracted to a `scheduleForDispatch(mode, schedule?)` helper that returns the schedule verbatim for cron and `undefined` for the other 3 modes.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns exactly `mode === "cron" ? schedule : undefined` for every input shape. Verified by 7 unit tests including a byte-equivalence matrix that asserts the helper equals the inline form for 7 (mode, schedule) combinations. The most subtle case: `scheduleForDispatch("cron", "")` returns `""` (not `undefined`) — this matches the inline form's verbatim return. The empty-string suppression (if any) is the caller's responsibility (e.g. the existing `input.schedule?.trim()` check in the API layer).

**Why this is a separate helper from `parseDispatchMode`:** `parseDispatchMode` is server-side (returns 4 booleans + valid), `scheduleForDispatch` is client-side (returns a single field for an API payload). Different consumers, different return shapes. Combining them would force every consumer to destructure both shapes.

### Refactor 3 — `joinCrontabLines(lines)` helper in `src/app/api/cron/hardware/route.ts`

The verbatim `lines.filter((l) => l.trim() || l === "").join("\n")` pattern was repeated 3 times in the hardware cron route (POST create line 334, PUT update line 423, PUT delete line 481). Extracted to an inline `joinCrontabLines(lines)` helper in the same route file (no new module — it's a 1-liner, not worth a separate file).

**Why inline, not `@/lib/`:** the helper is 1 line of pure function. Promoting to `@/lib/` would add a file boundary (and an import) for a function that's only used in this 1 route. The 3 call sites in the same file benefit from being a few lines apart from the helper definition. Promote to `@/lib/` on a 2nd route consumer (e.g. a future `software-cron/route.ts` that uses the same filter discipline).

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns exactly the same string as the inline form for every input. Verified by 6 unit tests in `tests/unit/join-crontab-lines.test.ts` including a byte-equivalence matrix that asserts the helper equals the inline form for 6 input shapes (empty, all-blank, mixed blanks, comment + entry blocks, whitespace-only middle lines, and the read-then-write round-trip case).

**The load-bearing bit:** the filter `(l) => l.trim() || l === ""` preserves intentionally empty lines (used as separators between job blocks in the on-disk crontab) while dropping lines that are only whitespace. A future "fix" that swapped to `.filter(Boolean)` or `.filter((l) => l)` would silently strip blank lines from the on-disk crontab, potentially concatenating job blocks. The helper + the byte-equivalence matrix lock this behaviour in.

### What was rejected

- **Migrating the chat page's `sessions.find((s) => s.id === activeSessionId)` (2 render-time sites at line 125 + line 463) to a `useMemo`-derived `activeSession`.** Initially attempted, but reverting because: the prior `const` form re-ran `find` on every render, which gave the downstream `messages` `useMemo` a fresh `activeSession` reference every render — meaning the `messagesEndRef.current?.scrollIntoView({...})` effect re-fired on every render. Memoising `activeSession` would change the scroll effect's dependency stability, which IS a behavior change (the auto-scroll would fire less often). The 2-site duplication is minor (one is in JSX, easy to read) and not worth a behavior change. Defer to a session that explicitly opts in to the "scroll on re-render no longer happens" behavior change.
- **Extracting a shared "insert new session" helper for `handleNewChat` + `handleSend` in the chat page.** The 2 sites diverge significantly: `handleNewChat` creates an empty session (no messages), `handleSend` creates + optimistically adds 2 messages + sets the title from the first user input. A shared helper would require a 5+ parameter options bag. Defer until a 3rd consumer appears (e.g. a "duplicate session" feature).
- **Promoting `submitToastForDispatch` (in `useMissionsPage.ts:30-35`) to `src/lib/`.** Already in `src/hooks/` and 1 consumer only (the dispatching toast in `useMissionsPage.ts:575, 634`). The 3 call sites already use the helper. Promote to `src/lib/` on a 2nd consumer.
- **Lifting `joinCrontabLines` to a `@/lib/` module.** 1-liner function, 1 route file consumer, no test surface beyond the `join-crontab-lines.test.ts` byte-equivalence matrix. Promote on a 2nd route consumer.
- **Adding a `useMissionsPage` decomposition step.** Still the biggest hook in the codebase (1182 LOC, mostly state). The `setFormField` + `dispatchPayload` + `setModelAndProvider` + `clearMissionFormFields` + `applyTemplateToForm` + `populateFormFromMission` helpers (extracted in sessions 84, 87, 91) cover the highest-leverage repetition. A full decomposition would require careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification — out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results". Defer to a dedicated decomposition session.
- **Consolidating the chat page's `stopEvent` helper into `@/lib/`.** 1 file consumer, 2 call sites in the same file. Promote on a 2nd file consumer.
- **Consolidating the chat page's 3 `setInput("")` calls into a `clearInput()` helper.** 1 file consumer, 2 functional call sites (the 3rd is a `setSessions` cascade). 1-line reset — not worth a helper.
- **Sweeping the 6 `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/cron/hermes-sync.ts`, `src/lib/sync/SyncScheduler.ts`, `src/lib/backends/hermes.ts`, `src/lib/sync-manager.ts`, `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` (7 sites alone) to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` helper.** 9 files total. Multi-session sweep, deferred to a List 2/3/4 session with the budget to handle the spread.
- **Extracting `parseDispatchMode` to a generic `parseEnum<T>(value, validValues, predicates)`.** The 4 modes are not a generic enum (they have semantic-specific predicates like `isCronMode && schedule`), and the type union is closed. The single-purpose helper is the simpler form; promote to a generic on a 2nd dispatch-mode consumer.
- **Migrating the chat page's `safeTitle = s.title.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/g, "_")` (1 site) to a `safeFilename(title)` helper in `@/lib/`.** 1 site, 1 file. Promote on a 2nd consumer.
- **Consolidating the 2 `try { localStorage.setItem(LAST_CATEGORY_KEY, ...) } catch { /* ignore */ }` sites in `useMissionsPage.ts:357-361, 459-463` into a `safeSetLocalStorage(key, value)` helper.** 2 sites, same file. The 3-line wrapper is already so trivial that the helper would obscure the intent more than it would save. Defer.

- `src/app/api/missions/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 3-line dispatchMode decomposition → 1-line helper call

Net diff: **8 files (2 new, 6 modified), +165 / -10 lines** (the +165 is dominated by the 26 new test cases + the 3 new helper definitions + the extensive doc-comments; the -10 is the 3-line decomposition collapse × 2 + the 1-line ternary collapse × 3 + the 1-line filter+join collapse × 3).

### Verification

- **All 1316 unit tests pass** (199 suites, no regressions, +26 new tests covering the 3 new helpers and their byte-equivalence contracts)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit:** `parseDispatchMode(mode, schedule)` returns the same 4 booleans as the inline form for every (mode, schedule) combination (verified by the 13-test matrix in `tests/unit/dispatch-mode.test.ts`). `scheduleForDispatch(mode, schedule)` returns the same field value as the inline form for every (mode, schedule) combination (verified by the 7-test matrix). `joinCrontabLines(lines)` returns the same string as the inline form for every line array (verified by the 6-test matrix in `tests/unit/join-crontab-lines.test.ts`). Wire format, response shape, dispatch branch selection, and on-disk crontab format are all unchanged.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"4-boolean decomposition from a discriminated union" pattern** — when a closed union of 4 values is decomposed into 4 booleans + 1 `valid` flag at multiple sites, extract to a `parseX(value, ...)` helper. The `valid` flag lets consumers write `if (!valid) return error` instead of the verbose 4-term negative test. The helper should preserve the union type for type-safe dispatch on the result.
2. **"Field selection from a discriminated union" pattern** — when a single field is conditionally included in a payload based on a union value (`mode === X ? field : undefined`), extract to a `fieldForX(mode, field)` helper. The helper should return the field verbatim (including empty string) to match the inline form's byte-equivalent behaviour. The "empty-string suppression" (if any) is the caller's responsibility.
3. **"Inline 1-liner helper in a 600-line route file" pattern** — when a 1-line filter+join or other transformation is repeated 3+ times in a single route file, lift it to an inline helper in the same file. Don't promote to `@/lib/` until a 2nd file consumer appears. The byte-equivalence matrix in a separate test file locks the behaviour in.
4. **"Reverted behavior change" pattern** — when a refactor would technically deduplicate code but introduces a subtle behavior change (e.g. `useMemo` changing effect dependency stability), revert and document the rationale in a comment. The "AT LEAST identical results" rule applies, even when the behavior change is "probably better". A future session that explicitly opts in to the behavior change can land the refactor then.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 2 has been hit 4 times in this rolling PR (sessions 75, 82, 88, 94). The next pick should be **List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs)** — last hit at session 93 (the second-most-recent). List 1 still has known follow-ups: `useApiData` extension to absorb `(main)/logs/page.tsx`'s manual `useInterval` + `setRefreshing` pattern, and the `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` `loadSessions` migration to `useApiData({queryParams, pagination})`. Both are behavior changes that need an "AT LEAST identical results" opt-in.
2. **`useApiData` extension for query params + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` is the next candidate. Requires extending the hook to support `URLSearchParams`. Defer until the hook shape is stable across logs + sessions.
3. **`setErrorFromCaught` promotion** — when the 8th `setError(messageFromError(err, ...))` site appears (currently 7), promote to a `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` helper. The 8th site is most likely to appear in List 1 or List 4 territory.
4. **Sweep the 9 `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/cron/`, `src/lib/sync/`, `src/lib/backends/`, `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` helper. Multi-session sweep, deferred to a session with the budget to handle 9 files.

A full decomposition would require careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification — out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results". Defer to a dedicated decomposition session.
6. **Promote `joinCrontabLines` to `src/lib/`** when a 2nd route consumer appears (e.g. a future `software-cron/route.ts` that uses the same filter discipline).
7. **Promote `submitToastForDispatch` to `src/lib/`** when a 2nd consumer appears. Currently 1 consumer in `useMissionsPage.ts`.
8. **Migrate the chat page's `sessions.find((s) => s.id === activeSessionId)` (2 render-time sites) to a `useMemo`** — but ONLY in a session that explicitly opts in to the behavior change (the auto-scroll effect's dependency stability changes). The chat page's `setInput("")` calls (3 sites) and the `safeTitle` regex (1 site) are too small to refactor.
9. **Sweep the 6 inline `} catch { showToast("Failed to X", "error") }` sites in `src/app/operations/`** to `toastError`. Most use **hardcoded strings** (not the caught error's message), so `toastError` would show the actual API error instead of the hardcoded string — a behavior change, NOT byte-equivalent. Leave inline until a future session explicitly opts in to the "AT LEAST identical results = strictly more informative" interpretation.

## Session 93 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `dbSessionFields` + `parseAssistantLines` helpers + `MessageBubble` `fnName` reuse


Pick was List 1 (per session 92's "Next session should" recommendation — the longest gap in recent protocol history, last touched at session 89, and 3+ known follow-ups queued). Three byte-equivalent refactors targeted small but well-scoped duplications that survived the prior shallow sweep: a 4-field envelope shared across 2 route branches, a 4-line line-parser shared between mission-output and (transitively) the JSONL branch, and a 1-expression `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` repeated in a single component.

All **1290** unit tests pass (**197** suites, +13 new tests in `session-detail-helpers.test.ts` covering the 2 new helpers). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: -19 deletions, +241 insertions (most of the insertions are the new test cases + the new helpers themselves, all of which will be exercised by future migrations). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 5 affected call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `dbSessionFields(dbSession, sanitizedId)` in `src/lib/session-detail.ts`

`GET /api/sessions/[id]` has 2 branches that build a `SessionData` payload from a `SessionRecord` row — the mission-output-file branch (transcript exists at the mission output file) and the no-output-yet branch (mission-spawned session whose agent hasn't produced a file yet). Both branches shared the same 4 envelope fields derived from the row:

```ts
title: dbSession.title || sanitizedId,
model: dbSession.modelId || "",
source: dbSession.source,
created: dbSession.startedAt,
```

Repeated verbatim across 2 call sites, 4 lines each = 8 lines of identical-looking-but-could-drift envelope construction. Extracted to `dbSessionFields()` which returns the 4 fields as a plain object spread into the `buildSessionData({...})` call. Each call site collapses from 4 envelope lines to a single `...dbSessionFields(dbSession, sanitizedId)` spread. The 2 branches diverge in `format/filename/messages/size/missionId/note` and those stay inline.

**Byte-equivalence audit:** the helper returns `{title, model, source, created}` with the same `||` fallback discipline as the inline form. `title: dbSession.title || sanitizedId` falls back on null AND empty string (because `||` is truthy-falsy, not null-coalescing) — the helper preserves that. Verified by 6 unit tests including a byte-equivalence matrix that asserts the helper equals the inline form for 5 row shapes (full, null title, null modelId, empty-string title + modelId, null startedAt).

### Refactor 2 — `parseAssistantLines(content)` in `src/lib/session-detail.ts`

The mission-output-file branch read the file body and parsed it via:

```ts
const lines = content.split("\n").filter((l: string) => l.trim());
const messages = lines.map((line: string, i: number) => ({
  index: i,
  role: "assistant",
  content: line,
}));
```

The same split+filter+map skeleton appears in the JSONL branch (line 216) but with a different `.map()` body (`{index, ...msg}` after `JSON.parse(line)`), so the JSONL branch stays inline. The mission-output branch's body is simpler and worth lifting to a unit-testable parser — extracted to `parseAssistantLines(content)` which returns the same `Array<{index, role, content}>` shape.

**Why this isn't premature abstraction:** the parser is pure (no filesystem, no DB), has 3+ non-trivial behaviors to test (blank-line drop, filtered-index numbering, content verbatim preservation), and is the kind of utility a future export-to-JSONL tool or a `/api/sessions/[id]/export` endpoint would want. Verified by 7 unit tests including a byte-equivalence matrix against the inline form for 6 input shapes.

**Rejected:** extracting a `parseSessionLines(content, parser)` generic that would unify the mission-output and JSONL branches. The per-line parsing logic is different (mock assistant vs JSON.parse with try/catch fallback) and a generic would require either a callback parameter or a discriminated return type. The "Rule of Three" doesn't apply — only 2 consumers with different downstream shapes. The `parseAssistantLines` lift is a single-purpose helper for the 1 branch that uses it; promote to a generic on a 3rd consumer.

### Refactor 3 — `MessageBubble.tsx` `fnName` reuse

`MessageBubble.tsx` had `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` computed twice for the same `fn`:

```ts
const fnName = String(fn?.name || "unknown");   // line 169 (for the React key)
const tcKey = `toolcall-${i}-${fnName.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g, "-")}`;
// ... 8 lines later:
<span className="text-neon-green">
  {String(fn?.name || "unknown")}                // line 177 (recomputed for the rendered label)
</span>
```

The second expression re-derives the same string for the rendered label. Reused the existing `fnName` local — the rendered text becomes `{fnName}` (the same `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` value, no recomputation, no behavior change).

**Why this is byte-equivalent:** `fnName` is a `const` in the same `.map((tc, i) => {...})` callback scope, holding `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` for the current `tc`. The JSX `{fnName}` interpolation calls `String()` on it implicitly, producing the same string the inline `{String(fn?.name || "unknown")}` produced. No `useMemo` or external state — purely a local constant reuse.

**Rejected:** promoting to a `<ToolCallName fn={fn} />` micro-component. The 2 sites are in the same `.map()` callback and the extraction would be 1 line of component definition for 1 line of JSX reuse — over-engineering for a same-scope variable reuse. The inline constant is the simplest form.

### What was rejected

- **Migrating `(main)/logs/page.tsx` to `useApiData({refreshIntervalMs})`** — session 91/89's follow-up. Would require extending the `useApiData` hook to absorb the manual `useInterval` + `setRefreshing` micro-state, which is a behavior change (removes the "Refresh" button's micro-spinner). The skill explicitly flags this as "AT LEAST identical results" → not a byte-equivalent move. Defer to a future session that opts in to the behavior change.
- **Migrating `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` to `useApiData({queryParams})`** — would require extending the hook to support `URLSearchParams` and pagination. Same behavior-change concern. Defer until `useApiData` shape stabilizes across the logs + sessions + dashboards consumers.
- **Extracting `dbSessionFields` to a 2-arg `buildSessionDataForDb(dbSession, sanitizedId, overrides)` helper that builds the full payload** — would absorb the rest of the branch-specific fields (format/filename/messages/size/missionId/note) and unify the 2 branches into a single call. The shape of the 2 branches diverges enough (one returns size from `st.size`, the other from `dbSession.size`; one returns `missionId: dbSession.missionId`, the other `missionId: dbSession.missionId ?? null`; one has a `note`, the other doesn't; one has a file-derived `messages`, the other has `messages: []`) that the "unified" call would need 5+ override parameters. The 4-field spread keeps each branch's divergence explicit and readable. The 2-consumer Rule of Three doesn't justify the more abstract helper yet.
- **Migrating the inline `} catch (err) { showToast("Failed to X", "error") }` sites in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx:309` and `(main)/logs/page.tsx:81, 224`** to `toastError`. Both use **hardcoded strings** (not the caught error's message), so `toastError` would show the actual API error instead of the hardcoded string — a behavior change, NOT byte-equivalent. Leave inline.
- **Extracting `String(s)` coercion helpers from `MessageBubble.tsx:84, 119, 169, 177`** — only line 169/177 had the exact `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` form, so the lift was local. Lines 84 (`content || ""` for clipboard) and 119 (`String(msg.name)` for a name badge) are different expressions and stay inline.
- **The `useApiData` migration in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`** for `loadSessions` — would need `URLSearchParams` + pagination support. Defer per the logs-page rationale.
- **The `({["all", "error", "warning"] as const}).map(...)` literal in `src/app/page.tsx:826`** for the errors panel severity filter. The `as const` + `.map()` is already the type-safe idiom; promoting to a `ERROR_SEVERITIES` constant in `@/lib/` would be 1 consumer (dashboard) and adds a file boundary for no testability gain. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same 3-value enum.

### Files

- `src/lib/session-detail.ts` (MODIFIED) — added `dbSessionFields` + `parseAssistantLines` helpers + `DbSessionEnvelope` type
- `src/app/api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 2 call sites migrated to `dbSessionFields`, 1 call site migrated to `parseAssistantLines`
- `src/components/session/MessageBubble.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 1 inline `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` replaced with the existing `fnName` const
- `tests/unit/session-detail-helpers.test.ts` (MODIFIED) — added 13 new tests (6 for `dbSessionFields`, 7 for `parseAssistantLines`)

Net diff: **4 files modified, +241 / -19 lines** (the +241 is dominated by the 13 new test cases + the 2 new helper definitions; the -19 is the 8-line envelope collapse × 2 + the 5-line line-parser collapse + the 2-line MessageBubble change).

### Verification

- **All 1290 unit tests pass** (197 suites, no regressions, +13 new tests covering the 2 new helpers and their byte-equivalence contract)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean (verified the `DbSessionEnvelope` structural type accepts a `SessionRecord` row via subtyping)
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit:** `dbSessionFields(row, id)` produces the same `{title: row.title || id, model: row.modelId || "", source: row.source, created: row.startedAt}` for every row shape (verified by the byte-equivalence matrix in `tests/unit/session-detail-helpers.test.ts`). `parseAssistantLines(content)` produces the same `Array<{index, role, content}>` as the inline form for every input shape (verified by the second byte-equivalence matrix). `MessageBubble.tsx`'s `{fnName}` reuses the same `String(fn?.name || "unknown")` result the inline form computed. Wire format, response shape, and rendered output are all unchanged.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Shared envelope helper for sibling route branches" pattern** — when 2 route branches share 3+ identical envelope fields and diverge on the rest, extract just the shared envelope to a `xxxFields(source, fallback)` helper. The 2 branches keep their divergence explicit. The alternative (a full `xxxResponse(source, override)` helper) absorbs all divergence into override parameters and obscures the branch-specific logic. The envelope-only helper is the lighter touch; promote to the full builder on a 3rd consumer.
2. **"Same-shape parser, different downstream consumer" anti-pattern** — when 2 sites do `content.split("\n").filter(...).map(...)` but the `.map()` body is different (mission-output uses a mock, JSONL uses `JSON.parse`), don't extract a generic `parseLines(content, callback)`. The callback parameter adds noise for 2 consumers with different downstream shapes. Extract only the sites that have IDENTICAL downstream bodies; leave the divergent sites inline.
3. **"Same-callback `{const}` reuse" pattern** — when a `.map((x, i) => {...})` callback computes the same value at the top and uses it in 2+ places inside the callback, just reuse the const. Don't promote to a sub-component, don't extract a helper. The variable reuse is the simplest form.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 1 has now been hit twice in a row (sessions 89, 93). The next pick should be List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — last hit at session 88, the longest current gap after List 1's 2x streak. List 2 still has known follow-ups: `useMissionsPage` decomposition (1175+ LOC, the biggest hook in the codebase) and the 22-site `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sweep in `src/lib/cron/`, `src/lib/sync/`, `src/lib/backends/`.
2. **Migrate `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` to `useApiData({queryParams, pagination})`** — requires extending the hook. Once `useApiData` supports query params, the same hook can absorb `(main)/logs/page.tsx`'s manual `useInterval` + `setRefreshing` pattern. Both migrations are behavior changes (the `setRefreshing` micro-state disappears), so they need an "AT LEAST identical results" opt-in. Recipe is in this session's "What was rejected" block.
3. **Promote `dbSessionFields` to a generic `extractEnvelopeFields<T, K>(source, fields, fallbacks)`** if a 2nd envelope-extraction appears (e.g. `extractAgentFields(agent)` in an agents route). The current 1-consumer Rule of Three doesn't justify a generic; defer until 2 distinct envelope shapes want the same treatment.
4. **Promote `parseAssistantLines` to a generic `parseLines<T>(content, parseLine)`** if a 2nd consumer wants the "split + filter blank + map per line" shape with a different per-line parser. The current 1-consumer Rule of Three doesn't justify the generic; defer until a 2nd branch or a future export endpoint needs it.
5. **The 4 `} catch { showToast("Failed to X", "error") }` sites in `src/app/operations/`** are the next refactor target when a future session explicitly opts in to the "AT LEAST identical results = strictly more informative" interpretation. Recipe is in session 92's "What was rejected" block.
6. **Sweep the 22 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/sync/`, `src/lib/cron/`, `src/lib/backends/`, `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` (7 sites alone) and the `src/components/cron/` operations pages** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` helper. Multi-session sweep, deferred to a List 2/3/4 session with the budget to handle 8+ files.
7. **Status badge label override follow-up** — `StatusBadge.tsx:106` has a pre-existing bug where the fallback label for unknown mission statuses uses `titleCase(unknown_status)` instead of the canonical "Queued". Documented in session 89's "rejected" block. A future session can fix it.
8. **HindsightBrowser 4 inline `error ?? "..."` sites** — would benefit from a `runMutation`-shaped variant. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same shape.
9. **The `({["all", "error", "warning"] as const}).map(...)` literal in `src/app/page.tsx:826`** — promote to a `ERROR_SEVERITIES` constant in `@/lib/` if a 2nd consumer (e.g. a future "errors in last 24h" widget) wants the same 3-value enum.

## Session 92 — List 4 (Models, HERMES.md, Environment, All Settings) — `pushDiff` closure refactor in 2 routes

Pick was List 4 (per random selection `python3 -c "import random; random.seed(20260603); print(random.choice([1,2,3,4]))"` = 4, the first List-4 pick since session 90). Session 90's analysis had concluded List 4 was "mined clean" — this session targeted two short, well-scoped closure extractions that escaped the previous sweep's shallow-cosmetic scope: 9 inline `diffs.push({id, label, detail})` sites in `/api/models/[id]/diff/route.ts` and 4 inline `diffs.push({field, before, after}) + updates.X = Y` site pairs in `/api/models/sync/pull/route.ts`'s `computeDiffs` helper. Both extractions are pure byte-equivalent closures that collapse repeated `diffs.push({...})` boilerplate into a single token per call.

All **1277** unit tests pass (**197** suites, no new tests added — both routes are already covered by `tests/unit/models-import-api.test.ts` + `tests/unit/models-pull-context-length.test.ts`). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Net: -18 lines (44 insertions, 62 deletions). Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 13 affected call sites.

### Refactor 1 — `pushDiff(id, label, detail)` closure in `src/app/api/models/[id]/diff/route.ts`

The route's POST handler had 9 inline `diffs.push({id, label, detail})` calls spread across the push + pull branches (5 in the push branch: modelId/provider/baseUrl/credential/no-change; 4 in the pull branch: no-hermes-data/modelId/provider/baseUrl/no-change). The shape is identical — a 3-field object appended to a `DiffEntry[]` array. Extracted a local closure:

```ts
const pushDiff = (id: string, label: string, detail: string) => {
  diffs.push({ id, label, detail });
};
```

Each of the 9 sites collapsed from a 4-line object literal to a 1-line `pushDiff(id, label, detail)` call. The closure captures the `diffs` array via lexical scope, so no extra parameter is needed. The wire shape (`{ data: { diffs, modelName } }`) is identical — the closure is a 1:1 transformation of the 9 object-literal sites.

### Refactor 2 — `pushDiff(field, before, after)` closure in `src/app/api/models/sync/pull/route.ts`

`computeDiffs()` had 4 inline `diffs.push({field, before, after}) + updates.X = Y` site pairs (modelId, provider, baseUrl, contextLength). Each site had to (1) push a diff record to the `diffs[]` accumulator and (2) record the new value on the `updates` object. Extracted a local generic closure with a type-safe `field` parameter:

```ts
const pushDiff = <K extends keyof typeof hermes & keyof typeof model>(
  field: K,
  before: unknown,
  after: unknown,
) => {
  diffs.push({ field: field as string, before, after });
  updates[field] = after;
};
```

Each of the 4 sites collapsed from 2-4 lines (the `diffs.push({...})` + `updates.X = Y` pair) to a 1-line `pushDiff(field, before, after)` call. The generic constraint `keyof typeof hermes & keyof typeof model` correctly limits `field` to the 4 shared keys (`modelId`, `provider`, `baseUrl`, `contextLength`) — TypeScript's structural typing rejects any other field name at the call site. The `field as string` cast is required because TypeScript can't narrow through a generic index. The wire shape (the `{diffs, updates}` return object) is identical.

### What was rejected

- **Extracting `pushDiff` to a shared `@/lib/diff-helpers.ts` module.** Only 2 consumers (the diff route and the pull route), and the closures have different signatures (3 strings vs 3 generics). Sharing would require a single over-parameterised signature or a thin wrapper module — neither is a clear win over 2 file-local closures. Promote to a shared lib on 3rd consumer.
- **Using a class field or a `diffs.reduce()` chain** instead of a closure. A closure is the most idiomatic JS pattern for "operate on the enclosing array" — a `reduce` would require re-binding the array, and a class adds OOP overhead for a 4-line function. The closure is the simplest form.
- **Migrating the 4 `diffs.push({id, label, detail})` sites in the `SyncModal` `fetchDiffs` fallback in `src/components/models/ModelSyncButtons.tsx`.** Component-side, not route-side. Different shape (`{id, label, detail}` with a `provider/modelIdString`-derived fallback, not a `diffs[].field` accumulator). The component's fallback array is built inline from a hardcoded set of diff labels, not from a diff-accumulation loop. Not the same pattern — the closure refactor doesn't apply. Out of scope.
- **Migrating the inline `for (const task of Object.keys(aux)) maskApiKeyField(...)` loop in `src/app/api/config/route.ts:97-100` to `Object.values(aux).forEach(...)`.** Session 90 already evaluated this and called it "marginal leverage (1 LOC saved, no testability gain)". Left alone per session 90's guidance.
- **The 4 `} catch { showToast("Failed to X", "error") }` sites in `src/app/operations/`** (carryover from session 90). Migrating to `toastError` is NOT byte-equivalent (it would show the actual API error message instead of the hardcoded string). Per session 91's "scope expansion" precedent, this is a future-session target when a session explicitly opts in to the "strictly more informative" interpretation of the constraint. The recipe is in `references/control-hub-list3-refactor-session-92.md`.

### Files

- `src/app/api/models/[id]/diff/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — `pushDiff` closure added, 9 sites collapsed
- `src/app/api/models/sync/pull/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — `pushDiff` closure added inside `computeDiffs`, 4 site pairs collapsed

Net diff: **2 files modified, +44 / -62 lines** (the +44 includes the 2 closures + their JSDoc-style inline comments; the -62 is the 13 collapsed site bodies).

### Verification

- **All 1277 unit tests pass** (197 suites, no regressions, no new tests — both routes are already exhaustively covered by `tests/unit/models-import-api.test.ts` and `tests/unit/models-pull-context-length.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean (verified the generic `keyof typeof hermes & keyof typeof model` constraint compiles)
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit:** both closures push the same object literal to the same array (or set the same key on the same object). The wire shape (`{ data: { diffs, ... } }` in the diff route and `{ data: { diffs, ... } }` + the `updates` arg to `updateModel()` in the pull route) is unchanged. The diff is a name-and-indirection refactor, not a behavior change.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Local closure over accumulator" pattern** — when 3+ inline `diffs.push({...})` calls in the same function share the same shape, extract a `pushX(...args)` closure that captures the accumulator. The closure pattern is more idiomatic than `array.reduce()` for "operate on the enclosing array" because it doesn't re-bind the array on every call. 2 file-local closures in this session; promote to `@/lib/` on 3rd consumer.
2. **"Generic `pushDiff<K extends keyof T>(field: K, before, after)" pattern** — when the closure needs a type-safe key parameter, the `keyof typeof obj` constraint is the right tool. It rejects invalid key names at the call site AND captures the type for the `updates[field] = after` write. The `as string` cast on the push site is required because TypeScript can't narrow a generic index through a `diffs.push` call; this is a known TS limitation, not a bug.
3. **"Closure vs shared lib" discriminator** — 2 file-local closures is fine. 3+ would justify a `@/lib/diff-helpers.ts` (or wherever the consumer module lives). Don't over-engineer at 2 consumers.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 4 has now been hit 5+ times (sessions 60, 70, 73, 85, 86, 90, 92). **List 1 is the next-ripe surface** — hasn't been touched since session 89 (the longest gap in recent protocol history). Carries 3 known follow-up items from session 89: `(main)/logs/page.tsx` `useApiData` migration, `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` URLSearchParams + pagination extension, and the inline `err instanceof Error` site in `JobFormModal.tsx` for the setError migration.
2. **Promote `pushDiff` closure to `@/lib/diff-helpers.ts`** if a 3rd consumer appears. The current 2 consumers (diff route + pull route) don't share enough shape to justify a shared module — the diff route uses `{id, label, detail}` (3 strings), the pull route uses `{field, before, after}` (generic key + 2 unknowns). A shared module would need both signatures. Defer until a 3rd route or lib function wants one of them.
3. **The 4 `} catch { showToast("Failed to X", "error") }` sites in `src/app/operations/`** are the next refactor target when a future session explicitly opts in to the "AT LEAST identical results = strictly more informative" interpretation. Recipe is in `references/control-hub-list3-refactor-session-92.md`.
4. **Migrate `(main)/logs/page.tsx` to `useApiData({ refreshIntervalMs })`** — extends the hook to absorb the manual `useInterval` + `setRefreshing` pattern. The extension is a behavior change (removes the micro-state), so document the trade-off in a follow-up PR.
5. **`useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` is the next candidate. Requires extending the hook to support query params. Defer until the hook shape is stable across logs + sessions.
6. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1175+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
7. **Sweep the 22 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` or a `setErrorFromCaught`-shaped helper. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper. Multi-session sweep.

## Session 91 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `setErrorFromCaught` helper + 9-site migration

Pick was List 3 (per random selection `echo $(( RANDOM % 4 + 1 ))` = 3, second List-3 pick in a row). This session picks up the explicit carryover from session 90's "Next session should:" block: promote `setErrorFromCaught` to `@/lib/api-fetch.ts` and migrate the 7 (now 9) `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` sites that the audit had been tracking. Single byte-equivalent refactor.

All **1277** unit tests pass (**197** suites — +1 new suite `set-error-from-caught.test.ts` with 8 new tests). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 9 migrated sites.

### Refactor 1 — `setErrorFromCaught` helper promotion

Added `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` to `src/lib/api-fetch.ts` as the `setError` sibling of the existing `toastError(showToast, err, fallback)` helper. Same composition rules (toError() + `|| fallback` discipline), different consumer. The new helper is the canonical replacement for the 9 sites that did `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` — one fewer intermediate variable and one less line in the common catch block.

Byte-equivalence to the inline form is documented in the JSDoc and proven by the byte-equivalence test in `tests/unit/set-error-from-caught.test.ts`:
```ts
directSetError(messageFromError(err, fallback));
setErrorFromCaught(helperSetError, err, fallback);
expect(helperCalls).toEqual(directCalls);
```
Both code paths produce the same `setError` call for 6 different caught-value shapes (Error, empty Error, string, null, undefined, TypeError).

### Refactor 2 — 9-site migration

|---|---|---|---|---|
