### What was rejected

- **Migrating the `setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error")` site in `src/components/cron/JobFormModal.tsx:166` and the 4 sites in `src/app/recroom/story-weaver/create/page.tsx` + `src/app/api/stories/route.ts`.** These are in Lists 1/2/3 and 4 (recroom/stories is its own category), not in this session's List 3 focus. They're all in scope for a future `setErrorFromCaught` extension, but pulling them in here would expand the session's scope beyond the List 3 pick. **9 sites across 7 files is plenty for a single session** — the 22 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message` sites across the codebase are the next-tier refactor surface and will need a dedicated multi-session sweep.
- **Migrating `setGenError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error")` in `src/app/recroom/story-weaver/create/page.tsx`.** Same as above — recroom/stories territory, out of scope for a List 3 pick.
- **Migrating the 22 `err instanceof Error ? err.message : <fallback>` sites in `src/lib/sync/`, `src/lib/cron/`, `src/lib/backends/`, etc. to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` helper.** The `messageFromError(e, fallback)` is already a server-safe helper (it only depends on `toError` which is pure JS). A dedicated sweep would touch `lib/sync/SyncScheduler.ts`, `lib/sync-manager.ts`, `lib/backends/hermes.ts`, `lib/cron/hermes-sync.ts`, `lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` (7 inline sites in just that one file). Out of scope — would be a multi-session lib sweep, not a List 3 pick.
- **Extending the `setErrorFromCaught` signature to accept the React Dispatch type directly via a `React.Dispatch<React.SetStateAction<string | null>>` import.** Adds React coupling to `api-fetch.ts` (which is currently a pure-TS module shared with server code). The current `SetErrorFn` local alias preserves the public type without coupling.
- **Promoting `setErrorFromCaught` to accept a 4th `prevState: T | null` parameter for `setError((prev) => ...)` updater pattern.** None of the 9 migrated sites use the updater form. Rule of Three says: don't add the parameter until you need it.

Net diff: **8 files modified + 1 new test file, +179 / -16 lines** (the +179 is the helper + tests + JSDoc; the -16 is the 9 inline call sites collapsing to 9 helper calls).

### Verification

- **All 1277 unit tests pass** (197 suites, +1 suite with +8 new tests in `tests/unit/set-error-from-caught.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session-51 lesson:** `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` is the canonical implementation of `setError(messageFromError(err, fallback))` (`src/lib/api-fetch.ts:151`). The migration is a name change, not a behavior change. The verbose `setError(toError(err).message || fallback)` form in `config/[section]/page.tsx` is byte-equivalent to `setError(messageFromError(err, fallback))` because `toError(err).message || fallback` is literally the body of `messageFromError`. So the 2 verbose sites get the same byte-equivalence treatment.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"setErrorFromCaught is the setError sibling of toastError" pattern** — two parallel helpers in `api-fetch.ts`, one per consumer. The audit recipe is a 1-line grep: `rg "setError\(messageFromError" src/` (after this session: 0 hits) or `rg "setError\(toError" src/` (after this session: 0 hits). New code review should reject both inline forms.
2. **"Verbose `toError(err).message || fallback` is the same as `messageFromError(err, fallback)`"** — they have identical semantics. The helper form is preferred because it has a name and JSDoc, but the verbose form is not a bug. Sites that use the verbose form can either keep it (with a JSDoc comment explaining the equivalence) or migrate to `setErrorFromCaught`. This session chose to migrate.
3. **"Import `messageFromError` if and only if you have a non-setError, non-showToast consumer"** — 5 of the 9 migrated files dropped `messageFromError` from their imports entirely. Only `useModelsPage.ts` kept it (for `details[].detail` and bulk-update return). `setErrorFromCaught` is a strict subset of `messageFromError`'s consumer surface.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session** to spread the refactor surface. ## Session 90 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — 4-site `toastError` migration in operations pages

Pick was List 3 (per random selection `echo $(( RANDOM % 4 + 1 ))` = 3, the first List-3 pick since session 80). Single byte-equivalent refactor: a 4-site migration of the inline `showToast(messageFromError(err, "..."), "error")` pattern in `src/app/operations/` to the `toastError(showToast, err, "...")` helper that was promoted in sessions 77 + 79. The helper was already adopted in `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts` (9 sites) but the operations pages had been left untouched. This session closes the last 4 sites in List 3 territory.

All 1269 unit tests pass (196 suites, no new tests added — the helper is exhaustively tested in `tests/unit/toast-error.test.ts`). tsc clean, eslint `--max-warnings 0` clean, build passes. Byte-equivalent at runtime for all 4 migrated sites.

### Refactor 1 — 4 `showToast(messageFromError(...))` → `toastError(...)` migrations

| File | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx` | 168 | `toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to update skill")` — toggleSkill catch block |
| `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx` | 216 | `toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to save skill")` — saveSkillEdit catch block |
| `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx`  | 72  | `toastError(showToast, err, "Failed to load toolsets")` — loadToolsets catch block |
| `src/app/operations/agents/page.tsx` | 207 | `toastError(showToast, e, "Failed to load file")` — openFile catch block (uses `e` not `err`) |

All 4 sites are clean catch-block patterns with no extra side effects on the error message (the skills toggle site has a `setData(prevData)` revert BEFORE the toast, which is preserved unchanged). The migration is mechanical and byte-equivalent: `toastError(showToast, err, fallback)` produces the same `showToast(messageFromError(err, fallback), "error")` call as the inline form (per `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:128`).

### Why this matters

The `toastError` helper is the canonical "catch-block error unwrap → toast" pattern. With this session, **every catch-block toast in `src/app/operations/` now uses the helper**, matching the convention in `useModelsPage.ts`. The `showToast(messageFromError(...))` form is now an outlier — it survives in `(main)/logs/page.tsx`, `config/seed/page.tsx`, and a few other List 1/4 sites, but is fully eliminated from List 3.

### Audit recipe (to find any future inline site)

```bash
grep -rnE "showToast\(messageFromError\(" src/ --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" \
  | grep -v "api-fetch\|tests/"
```

**Pre-session: 4 hits in List 3** (the 4 migrated sites). **Post-session: 0 hits in List 3** + 0 hits in `src/lib/api-fetch.ts` (those are JSDoc examples, not real call sites).

### What was NOT migrated (and why)

- `src/app/operations/skills/[...path]/page.tsx:66` — `setError(messageFromError(err, "Unknown error"))`. Uses `setError` (a useState setter), not `showToast`. The `toastError` helper is for `showToast`-based error reporting. A `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` helper would be the equivalent, but with only 7 sites total across the codebase and no 2nd-consumer pattern, the Rule of Three says to keep the inline form.
- `src/app/operations/personalities/page.tsx:166` — same as above (`setError` not `showToast`).
- `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts:97,175,253` — same: `setError` (line 97) and `details[].detail` (line 175, 253). Not toast candidates.

### Files

- `src/app/operations/skills/page.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 2 sites migrated, import changed `messageFromError` → `toastError`
- `src/app/operations/tools/page.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 1 site migrated, import changed
- `src/app/operations/agents/page.tsx` (MODIFIED) — 1 site migrated, import changed

Net diff: **3 files, +7 / -7 lines** (each migration is a 1-line replacement + a 1-character import change).

### Verification

- **All 1269 unit tests pass** (196 suites, no new tests — `toastError` is already exhaustively covered by `tests/unit/toast-error.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean on all 3 modified files
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session-51 lesson:** `toastError(showToast, err, fallback)` is the canonical implementation of `showToast(messageFromError(err, fallback), "error")` (`src/lib/api-fetch.ts:128`). The migration is a name change, not a behavior change.

### What was rejected

- **Migrating the 7 `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` sites to a `setErrorFromCaught` helper.** With only 7 sites across 6 files, the Rule of Three says to keep the inline form. The helper would be `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, fallback)` — a 3-parameter setter wrapper. Not a clear win.
- **Refactoring `apiFetch` to accept `body?: unknown` like `safeApiCall` does, eliminating the 12 `JSON.stringify({...})` wrappers in List 3 callsites.** This is a signature-level change with type-level ripples across the entire codebase. The current convention (`apiFetch` takes `RequestInit`, `safeApiCall` takes a body object) is documented in the JSDoc and is consistent across all 30+ callsites. Out of scope.
- **Consolidating the 4 `Modal` import + 4 `useToast` + 4 `toastElement` patterns in the 4 operations pages into a shared `useOperationPage` hook.** Out of scope — the 4 pages have different state shapes, different modals, and different load/save semantics. A shared hook would have 4+ conditional branches and wouldn't reduce code.
- **Extracting the `{ showToast, toastElement } = useToast()` + `{toastElement}` render pattern into a custom `useToastElement()` hook that returns the element directly.** Cosmetic only, doesn't reduce code at the call site. Defer.
- **Auditing the inline `console.warn("Model auto-import failed", msg)` in `useModelsPage.ts:67` for a `logApiError` migration.** Different surface (server-side logging, not toasts). Already uses the canonical `toError(err).message || String(err)` pattern from session 79. No change needed.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"List 3 operations pages are now `toastError`-complete" pattern** — the 4 sites that remained after the session-77 + session-79 migrations are all closed. The `showToast(messageFromError(...))` inline form is now an anti-pattern in operations pages, and code review should reject it. The audit recipe is a 1-line grep.
2. **"Different catch-block consumer" discriminator for `toastError` vs `setError`/`details[].detail`** — `toastError` is only for `showToast`-based reporting. When a catch block writes to `setError` (a useState setter) or to a structured `details[].detail` field, the `messageFromError(err, fallback)` form is correct (it returns the message; the caller decides where to put it). The 7 sites in `src/app/operations/skills/[...path]/page.tsx`, `personalities/page.tsx`, `useModelsPage.ts` are all of this shape.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session** to spread the refactor surface. List 3 has been hit 6+ times now (sessions 67, 70, 77, 80, 90). **List 4 is the next-ripe surface** — it hasn't been touched since session 86 (the longest gap in the protocol history), and has accumulated 2 carry-over items from session 86: `safeWriteFileAtomic` helper promotion and `safeReadJson` helper promotion.

## Session 132 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `ok()` factory migration of 3 missed sites + filter-scope-mismatch fix

Pick was **List 3** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3, the first List-3 pick since session 130. Random pick: **List 3**. Session 130 documented a known gap (session 130 P-130-1, "filter-scope-mismatch") — the per-list page set in the mission brief is page-named (Models/Agents/Skills/Tools/Personalities = 5 pages) and maps to 6 backend subtrees, but the `ok-factory-source-patterns.test.ts` List 3 filter only covered 3 of the 6 subtrees (`api/models`, `api/agent`, `api/credentials`). The 3 missing subtrees (`api/skills`, `api/tools`, `api/personalities`) silently let 3 un-migrated `data:` sites through. This session closes the gap.

### 1. Filter extension (3 subtrees added)

The List 3 production it() block's filter was extended from:

```ts
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "models")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "agent")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "credentials")}`),
```

to:

```ts
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "models")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "agent")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "credentials")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "skills")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "tools")}`) ||
s.file.includes(`${join("src", "app", "api", "personalities")}`),
```

Per session 130 P-130-3: the page-named List 3 → backend-subtree mapping is **6 subtrees**, not 3. The block comment was updated to document the mapping for future maintainers.

### 2. 3 un-migrated `data:` sites discovered + migrated

Running the test BEFORE the migration (with the extended filter) catches the 3 sites:

```
Found 3 un-migrated 'return NextResponse.json({ data: ... })' site(s) in the List 3 surface:
  src/app/api/personalities/route.ts:51  return NextResponse.json({
  src/app/api/personalities/route.ts:78  return NextResponse.json({
  src/app/api/tools/route.ts:19  return NextResponse.json({
```

All 3 sites are 200-locked success responses, byte-equivalent to `ok(...)`:

| File | Line | Site | Migration |
|------|------|------|-----------|
| `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 51 | `return NextResponse.json({ data: { success: true, name, prompt, source: "SOUL.md" } })` (PUT/POST success) | `ok({ success: true, name: result.profile, prompt, source: "SOUL.md" })` |
| `src/app/api/personalities/route.ts` | 78 | `return NextResponse.json({ data: { personalities, total } })` (GET success) | `ok({ personalities: profiles, total: profiles.length })` |
| `src/app/api/tools/route.ts` | 19 | `return NextResponse.json({ data: { platforms: HERMES_PLATFORMS, toolsets: HERMES_CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS } })` (GET toolset catalog) | `ok({ platforms: HERMES_PLATFORMS, toolsets: HERMES_CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS })` |

**Per-file commit granularity** (session 116 P-2): 2 separate commits, one per file. The `api/personalities` and `api/tools` routes are independent of each other, so a per-file commit makes the carryover-detection state clean if the session hits the iteration cap mid-file.

**Import cleanup** (session 130 P-130-4): `api/tools/route.ts` no longer uses `NextResponse` after the migration, so the import was dropped (the only reference was the migrated site). `api/personalities/route.ts` still uses `NextResponse` at line 17 for the `instanceof NextResponse` bodyResult check, so the import is retained.

**Per the pitfall from session 111 P-1** ("local-var shadowing when adding a new helper import"), I checked for any pre-existing `const ok = ...` in the modified files. Neither file had one, so no rename was needed. Per session 130 P-130-4, the post-migration `rg -c "NextResponse\." <file>` audit confirmed `api/tools/route.ts` had 0 references and `api/personalities/route.ts` had 1 (the `instanceof` check).

### 3. Filter-scope self-test (`scanner form coverage` block)

A new self-test was added to the `scanner form coverage` describe block to pin the filter-scope contract. The test reads this test file's source and asserts each of the 6 expected subtrees has a matching `join("src", "app", "api", "<name>")` call in the production filter.

**Why the design**: an earlier iteration of this self-test (path-based: synthesize fixture paths and run the production filter against them) was a false positive — it passed even with the `api/tools` clause removed, because both `expectedSubtrees.length` and `matched.length` shrunk by 1 in lockstep. The substring approach (read the test file's source, check for `join("src", "app", "api", "<name>")` literally) is robust because `expectedSubtrees` is a hard-coded constant — if a future session removes a clause from the production filter, `expectedSubtrees` stays at 6 and the substring check fails.

**Self-test verification**: temporarily removing the `api/tools` clause from the production filter makes the test fail with a clear diagnostic:

```
List 3 filter-scope self-test failed: the following expected subtrees are NOT
covered by the production filter: src/app/api/tools. Add the missing subtrees
to the List 3 filter in the 'has zero ... List 3 surface' it() block. Expected
subtrees (from mission brief + session 130 P-130-3): src/app/api/models,
src/app/api/agent, src/app/api/credentials, src/app/api/skills,
src/app/api/tools, src/app/api/personalities
```

The test was then restored and re-verified green.

### 4. What was NOT migrated (and why)

- **The 3 `ok()` sites in `api/skills/*` and the 18 in `api/models/*`** — already migrated in sessions 111-117. Re-verified via the `ok-factory-source-patterns.test.ts` source-pattern test (List 3 filter now covers all 6 subtrees, 0 un-migrated sites).
- **The `messageFromError` sites in `src/app/operations/`, `src/components/models/`, `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts`** — already migrated in sessions 77 + 91. Re-verified by the audit recipe `rg -nU 'setError\(messageFromError' src/ | rg -v 'api-fetch|tests/'` (0 hits).
- **The `toastError` sites in operations pages** — already migrated in session 90. Re-verified by the audit recipe `rg -nE "showToast\(messageFromError\(" src/` (0 hits in `src/app/operations/`).
- **The `serverErrorFromCatch` sites in `api/skills/*` + `api/tools/*`** — already migrated in session 127. Re-verified by the `server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts` (List 3 surface assertion, 0 un-migrated sites).
- **The `apiFetch + JSON.stringify` sites in `useModelsPage.ts`** (e.g. line 170: `apiFetch(\`/api/models/sync/${action}\`, { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({...}) })`) — candidate for the session 131 P-131-2 `apiFetch → safeApiCall` migration, but the migration changes the field accessor (`d.data?.field` → `result.data?.data?.field`) and is a 1-file, 1-site change. Defer to a future List 3 session that explicitly opts in to accessor-shift migrations.
- **The `safeApiCall<{ data: T }>` envelope-unwrap bug-class sites in `useModelsPage.ts:413`** — same as above: candidate for the `safeApiCallData` helper migration, but the call is in List 3 territory and was deliberately not part of this session's scope (this session is about the source-pattern test, not the safeApiCallData helper).

### 7. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Filter-scope-mismatch" pitfall (session 130 P-130-1)** — the per-list page set in the mission brief is page-named; the source-pattern test filter is backend-subtree-named. They do not auto-sync. Cross-check at session start with the audit recipe: list all page-named members for the picked list, then `rg -nP` the filter paths in the source-pattern test. If a page-named member has no matching filter path, the scanner is silently blind to that subtree. The session-132 self-test in `ok-factory-source-patterns.test.ts` codifies this check as an automated regression test.
2. **"Self-test design: substring vs path-based" lesson** — for filter-coverage self-tests, substring checks against the production filter's literal source are more robust than path-based fixture injection. The path-based approach I tried first was a false positive — both `expected.length` and `matched.length` shrunk in lockstep when a clause was removed. Substring checks against a hard-coded `expectedSubtrees` array catch the failure mode correctly.
3. **"Per-file commit granularity" pattern (session 116 P-2 recall)** — when 2 files both need `ok()` migrations in the same session, commit them separately. The 3 commits this session produced (personalities → tools → test) are each self-consistent: imports updated, no dead imports, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. The iteration cap can hit between commits without leaving any file in an inconsistent state.

## Session 134 — `fs/list` route factory migration (carryover from previous cron run)

Random pick for session 133: **List 3** (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities). The previous cron run hit the iteration cap after staging a 2-file factory migration of `src/app/api/fs/list/route.ts` (uncommitted in the working tree at the start of this session). Per the carryover protocol, this was the first thing committed — a 1-commit "finish what session 132 started" carryover before proceeding to the new List 3 work.

### 1. `fs/list` route factory migration (4 sites, 1 file)

The route was the last `src/app/api/fs/*` handler still using the inline `return NextResponse.json({ ... }, { status: <code> })` form:

| Site | Pre-migration form | Post-migration form |
|------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `resolveAllowedWorkspacePath` 400 | `return NextResponse.json({ error: resolved.error }, { status: 400 })` | `return badRequest(resolved.error)` |
| "Not a directory" 400 | `return NextResponse.json({ error: "Not a directory" }, { status: 400 })` | `return badRequest("Not a directory")` |
| Success 200 | `return NextResponse.json({ data: { path: abs, parent, entries } })` | `return ok({ path: abs, parent, entries })` |
| Catch 500 | `logApiError(...); return NextResponse.json({ error: "Failed to list directory" }, { status: 500 })` | `return serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, "Failed to list directory")` |

**Test coverage** — `tests/unit/fs-list-api.test.ts` went from 2 → 4 it() blocks. The pre-existing tests covered the 200 + "not allowed" 400 paths; the new tests pin the "Not a directory" 400 path and the new `serverErrorFromCatch` 500 path (with a `mockReaddirSync` that throws to exercise the outer catch). The `serverErrorFromCatch` jest mock replicates the canonical `logApiError` + 500 response body pair so the contract is preserved under the test harness.

**Imports** — `NextResponse` was removed from the route's imports (no longer used after migration); `badRequest` + `ok` added from `@/lib/api-response`; `serverErrorFromCatch` added from `@/lib/api-logger`. The `logApiError` import was dropped (its only consumer was the migrated catch block).

**Byte-equivalence** — `badRequest(msg)` is literally `NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status: 400 })` (`src/lib/api-response.ts:30`), `ok(data)` is `NextResponse.json({ data }, { status: 200 })` (line 197), `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, msg)` is `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `serverError(msg)` (the 500 response factory). All 4 migrated sites are status-code-locked (400, 400, 200, 500) and the factories preserve the exact body shape and status — wire-equivalent at the HTTP boundary.

### 2. Files

- `src/app/api/fs/list/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 3 import changes + 4 site migrations (2x `badRequest`, 1x `ok`, 1x `serverErrorFromCatch`)
- `tests/unit/fs-list-api.test.ts` (MODIFIED) — 1 `serverErrorFromCatch` jest mock addition + 2 new it() blocks (400 "Not a directory" + 500 catch path)

Net diff: **2 files, +79 / -9 lines** (the +79 is the new test coverage; the production route is net -4 lines from the 4 site collapses + 3 import changes).

- **All 1661 unit tests pass** (236 suites, +2 new it() blocks in `fs-list-api.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence** — see above

### 4. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Carryover is a separate commit" rule** — when the previous cron run hit the cap with uncommitted work, the next session commits the carryover as a single atomic commit BEFORE proceeding to the new work. The carryover commit is a self-contained "finish what session N started" — its message, scope, and tests stand on their own. This session's carryover commit (`21f1a92`) is a textbook example: it includes the production migration AND the test coverage gap, so the diff is reviewable in isolation.
2. **"Mock the factory, not the primitive" pattern for serverErrorFromCatch tests** — the new test mocks `serverErrorFromCatch` to replicate its canonical behavior (logApiError + 500 response body) so the contract is preserved under the test harness. This is the same pattern session 95 used for its 27-site List 4 migration. The test asserts the observable contract (status 500 + body shape) without coupling to the helper's internals.

## Session 133 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `safeApiCallData` migration in `useModelsPage.ts` + source-pattern test

Random pick: **List 3** via `shuf -n1 -e 1:... 2:... 3:... 4:...` = 3 (the 4th List-3 pick since session 130 — sessions 130, 131, 132, 133). Per the carryover protocol, this is the **new work** for the session (session 134 documents the in-flight carryover from the previous cron run; the 2 commits are sequenced carryover → new work).

The List 3 factory-family migrations (ok, serverErrorFromCatch, serverErrorFromError) were all closed by session 132. The next-ripe surface for List 3 is the **client-side `apiFetch` envelope-unwrap** family — specifically, the `safeApiCall<{ data: T }>` and `apiFetch<{ data?: T }>` patterns that require a manual `.data?.X` unwrap at the access site. The canonical replacement is `safeApiCallData<T>(...)`, which collapses the envelope in one call. This session migrates 4 sites in `useModelsPage.ts` (the only List 3 hook with non-trivial `Promise.all` reads + 1 envelope-typed write) and adds a sibling source-pattern test to pin the new shape.

### 1. 3 best-effort `Promise.all` reads in `useModelsPage.ts:loadAll` (lines 75-82)

The `loadAll` function had 3 best-effort `apiFetch(url).catch(() => ({ data: null as T | null }))` sites that were the inline workaround for "I want `T | null` from a `{ data: T }` envelope". The canonical replacement is `safeApiCallData<T>(url)`, which does the envelope unwrap + error swallow in one call.

| Site | Pre-migration | Post-migration |
|------|--------------|----------------|
| `/api/models/sync/drift` | `apiFetch(url).catch(() => ({ data: null as SyncDrift \| null }))` + `driftData.data` | `safeApiCallData<SyncDrift>(url)` + `drift` |
| `/api/models/fallbacks` | `apiFetch(url).catch(() => ({ data: null as { entries?: FallbackChainEntry[] } \| null }))` + `fbData.data?.entries` | `safeApiCallData<{ entries?: FallbackChainEntry[] }>(url)` + `fb?.entries` |
| `/api/models/fallbacks/config` | `apiFetch(url).catch(() => ({ data: null as { config?: FallbackConfig } \| null }))` + `fbCfgData.data?.config` | `safeApiCallData<{ config?: FallbackConfig }>(url)` + `fbCfg?.config` |

**Byte-equivalence at the access site** — pre-migration `driftData.data` is `SyncDrift | null`; post-migration `drift` is `SyncDrift | null`. Same for `fbData.data?.entries` vs `fb?.entries` and `fbCfgData.data?.config` vs `fbCfg?.config`. The `if (x) { setY(x) }` blocks now reference the unwrapped value directly.

**Outer try/catch preserved** — the first 3 `apiFetch(url)` reads in the same `Promise.all` (models, credentials, defaults) still throw on error, so the outer `try/catch` + `setErrorFromCaught(setError, err, "Failed to load registry")` continues to show the user-visible "Failed to load registry" toast if a primary endpoint is down. The 3 best-effort reads silently return `null` on per-endpoint error (matching the pre-migration `.catch(() => ({ data: null }))` behavior). The Promise.all block now has **mixed error semantics**: 3 strict-fail (throw → outer catch) + 3 best-effort (silently null). This is byte-equivalent to the pre-migration form.

**Variable rename for readability** — the 6 destructure names were shortened from `mData/cData/dData/driftData/fbData/fbCfgData` to `m/c/d/drift/fb/fbCfg` to match the new shorter expression shape. The reader no longer needs `.data?.X` indirection, so the `Data` suffix was redundant.

### 2. 1 double-envelope `safeApiCall<{ data: { config } }>` write in `persistFallbackConfigNow` (line 413)

The `safeApiCall<{ data: { config: FallbackConfig } }>(...)` form is a double-envelope anti-pattern: `safeApiCall<T>` already returns `{ ok, data?: T, error? }`, so the explicit inner `{ data: { ... } }` wrapper in the type parameter is redundant. The caller then unwraps with `res?.data?.config`, which is one indirection too many.

**Pre-migration**:
```ts
const { ok, data: res, error } = await safeApiCall<{ data: { config: FallbackConfig } }>(...)
const saved = res?.data?.config;
```

**Post-migration**:
```ts
const { ok, data: res, error } = await safeApiCall<{ config: FallbackConfig }>(...)
const saved = res?.config;
```

Same `{ ok, data?: T, error? }` envelope, single nesting. The `safeApiCall<T>` return type is documented in `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:107-118` (the `SafeApiCallResult<T>` type alias) — callers pass `T = the inner payload`, and the helper exposes it as `result.data?.T`.

### 3. 1 `apiFetch<{ data?: ... }>` read in `handleRefresh` (line 293)

The `/api/models/import` POST returns `{ data: { modelsImported, modelsSkipped, credentialsUpdated } }`. The pre-migration form was `apiFetch<{ data?: { modelsImported?: number; ... } }>(...)` with manual `result.data?.modelsImported` access. The canonical replacement is `safeApiCallData<{ modelsImported?: number; ... }>(...)` with direct `result?.modelsImported` access.

**Pre-migration**:
```ts
const result = await apiFetch<{
  data?: { modelsImported?: number; modelsSkipped?: number; credentialsUpdated?: number };
}>("/api/models/import", { method: "POST" });
const modelsImported = result.data?.modelsImported ?? 0;
const creds = result.data?.credentialsUpdated ?? 0;
```

**Post-migration**:
```ts
const result = await safeApiCallData<{
  modelsImported?: number;
  modelsSkipped?: number;
  credentialsUpdated?: number;
}>("/api/models/import", { method: "POST" });
const modelsImported = result?.modelsImported ?? 0;
const creds = result?.credentialsUpdated ?? 0;
```

Same observable result — `safeApiCallData<T>` returns `T | null`; the `?? 0` fallback handles both "no data" and "null" cases identically to the inline `?.X ?? 0` form. The catch path (toastError on failure) is preserved unchanged.

### 4. Source-pattern test — `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (NEW, 5 tests)

Sister test to the existing `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern.test.ts` (List 1 dashboard). The new test pins the post-refactor shape for `useModelsPage.ts`:

- ✓ the hook source file is readable
- ✓ the hook imports `safeApiCallData` from `@/lib/api-fetch`
- ✓ the hook has **zero** `apiFetch(url).catch(() => ({ data: null as ... }))` best-effort reads
- ✓ the hook has **zero** double-envelope `safeApiCall<{ data: { ... } }>` calls (line comments stripped first to avoid the JSDoc that documents the pre-migration form)
- ✓ the hook uses `safeApiCallData` ≥ 3 times in the `loadAll` Promise.all block

Per the session 132 filter-scope-mismatch lesson, the test targets the **canonical List 3 hook** (`useModelsPage.ts`) rather than the 6 backing subtrees. A future-extension anchor `_LIST_3_HOOKS` is retained (unused today, ESLint `void`'d) so a future session that adds more List 3 hook coverage can extend the surface without re-deriving the file list. The test file documents the per-list page → backend-subtree mapping (Models/Agents/Skills/Tools/Personalities = 6 subtrees) so future maintainers know which surfaces are in scope.

### 5. Files

- `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts` (MODIFIED) — 1 import extension (`safeApiCallData` added) + 4 site migrations (3 in `loadAll`, 1 in `handleRefresh`, 1 in `persistFallbackConfigNow`) + variable rename `mData/...` → `m/...`
- `tests/unit/safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts` (NEW) — 5 it() blocks + JSDoc history block

Net diff: **1 source file + 1 test file, +171 / -19 lines** (the +171 is the new test file; the production hook is net +42 / -19 from the 5 migrations + variable rename + comments).

### 6. Verification

- **All 1661 unit tests pass** (236 suites, +1 new suite with +5 new tests in `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern-list3.test.ts`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean (the new test file's `_LIST_3_HOOKS` unused-var is `void`'d to satisfy the `no-unused-vars` rule with a documented future-extension rationale)
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session 51 lesson** — `safeApiCallData<T>(url)` is literally `((await safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>(url)).data?.data ?? null)` per `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:154-160`. The 4 migrated sites are all byte-equivalent: same `T | null` return, same access pattern (`?.X ?? default`), same catch behavior (silent on best-effort reads, throw on strict-fail reads).
- **The 4 existing source-pattern tests still pass** — `ok-factory-source-patterns`, `safe-api-call-data-source-pattern` (List 1), `server-error-from-catch-source-patterns`, `server-error-from-error-source-patterns` (35 tests total). No scanner regression.

### 7. What was rejected

- **Migrating the 9 `apiFetch + JSON.stringify({...})` mutation sites in `useModelsPage.ts` and the 4 operations pages** (e.g. `useModelsPage.ts:168` syncModel, `:226` handleSetDefault, `:252` handleBulkAuxiliaryChange, `:353` handleFallbackEditSave, `:481` handleSyncFallbackToHermes, `:209` handleDelete, `:396` handleImportFallbackFromConfig; `operations/agents/page.tsx:285`, `operations/personalities/page.tsx:160`, `operations/skills/page.tsx:191, 243`). The migration to `safeApiCall(url, { method, body: {...} })` would auto-stringify the body (saving 1 `JSON.stringify` call per site), but it changes the failure mode from "throw on error" to "return `{ ok: false, error: string }`" — a behavior change at the catch-block level. The current `try/catch + toastError` pattern is the canonical 2-line catch idiom (session 90), and the migration would require a `if (!ok) { toastError(...); }` rewrite at every site. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — defer to a future List 3 session that explicitly opts in to `safeApiCall` mutations.
- **Migrating the 4 strict-fail `apiFetch(url)` reads at the top of `loadAll` (models, credentials, defaults) to `safeApiCallData<T>(url)`.** This would silently swallow errors that the outer `try/catch` currently surfaces as "Failed to load registry". Preserving the throw-on-error behavior is the correct trade-off — the user needs to know if a primary endpoint is down. The migration is byte-equivalent at the *value* site (`m.data?.models ?? []` → `m?.models ?? []`) but changes the *failure* site (throw → null). Out of scope — defer to a future session that explicitly opts in to the "more permissive failure mode" interpretation.
- **Migrating the `useMissionsPage.ts` `safeApiCall<{ data?: { mission?: { id: string } } }>` sites (3 sites at lines 661, 692, 720) to `safeApiCall<{ mission?: { id: string } }>`.** List 2 territory (Cron, Missions, Chat), not this session's List 3 focus. Same single-nesting pattern; defer to a List 2 pick.
- **Migrating the `useCronJobMutation.ts:136` `safeApiCall<{ data?: { pausedCount?: number } }>` site.** List 2 territory. Defer.
- **Migrating the `useMissionsApi.ts:46` `safeApiCall<{ data?: { category?: { id: string } } }>` site.** List 2 territory. Defer.
- **Adding a `safeApiCallData` source-pattern test for the List 2 surface** (useMissionsPage, useMissionsApi, useCronJobMutation). The 4 List-2 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites are all candidates for the same single-nesting migration. Defer to a List 2 pick.
- **Adding a `safeApiCallData` source-pattern test for the List 4 surface** (config/[section], config/seed). The 0 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` sites in List 4 are already zero (session 91 swept them). The test would be a no-op green. Defer.
- **Extending `_LIST_3_HOOKS` to cover the 5 operations pages.** The 5 operations pages (`agents`, `personalities`, `skills`, `skills/[...path]`, `tools`) don't use `safeApiCallData` today — they use `apiFetch` for reads and `safeApiCall` for mutations. Adding them to the test would require either (a) asserting "zero `apiFetch<T>` reads" (false positive on legitimate `apiFetch` calls) or (b) asserting "zero `apiFetch<{ data?: T }>` reads" (the actual rule, but the operations pages don't have any). Defer until a future session migrates the operations pages to `safeApiCallData`.

### 8. Patterns to take forward

1. **"Envelope-unwrap taxonomy"** — there are 3 ways to read a `{ data: T }` envelope in the codebase, and each has a different use case:
   - `apiFetch<{ data?: T }>(url)` + `result.data?.X` — strict-fail reads where the outer try/catch surfaces errors to the user (the 3 `loadAll` "primary" reads: models, credentials, defaults)
   - `safeApiCall<{ data?: T }>(url)` + `result.data?.X` — read+toast mutations where the caller needs `ok` + `error` for the success path (handleSyncFallbackToHermes, etc.)
   - `safeApiCallData<T>(url)` + `result?.X` — best-effort reads where per-endpoint error is silently swallowed (the 3 `loadAll` "best-effort" reads: drift, fallbacks, fallbacks/config) and write+access where the caller doesn't need `ok`/`error` (handleRefresh)
   
   The choice between (1) and (3) is a **failure-mode** choice: throw + user-visible error vs. silent null + per-section blank. The choice between (2) and (3) is a **success-path** choice: do you need `ok`/`error` for branching, or just the inner payload? This session is the first to codify the taxonomy in the JSDoc for `safeApiCallData` and the source-pattern test.
2. **"Single-nesting for safeApiCall<T>" pattern** — `safeApiCall<T>` already returns `{ ok, data?: T, error? }`, so the type parameter should be the **inner** payload, not the envelope. `safeApiCall<{ config: T }>` is correct; `safeApiCall<{ data: { config: T } }>` is a double-envelope anti-pattern. The source-pattern test's "zero double-envelope" check pins this rule.
3. **"Filter-scope per-list" pattern** — per the session 132 filter-scope-mismatch lesson, the per-list page set in the mission brief is page-named but the test filter is backend-subtree-named (or hook-named, in this case). The new test targets `useModelsPage.ts` (the only List 3 hook with envelope-typed reads) rather than the 6 backing subtrees. The `_LIST_3_HOOKS` future-extension anchor documents the page-to-subtree mapping so the next session can extend the surface if needed.

### 9. "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session** to spread the refactor surface. List 3 has been hit 11+ times now (sessions 67, 70, 77, 80, 90, 91, 112, 117, 119, 130, 131, 132, 133). **List 1 is the next-ripe surface** — last touched session 129 (4 sessions ago), and the session-129 follow-ups are: (a) the `JobFormModal.tsx:166` inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Unknown error"` site (was deferred from session 91 because the `setErrorFromCaught` helper had only 7 sites — the count is now 9 after session 91, so it's ripe for a 2-consumer → N-consumer promotion), (b) the `useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination in `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` (deferred from sessions 98, 109, 122, 123), and (c) the inline `console.error` redundancy rule from session 131 P-131-4 applied to `(main)/logs/page.tsx` and `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` (3-5 sites). **List 2** is the next-ripe surface after List 1 — the 4 `safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope sites in `useMissionsPage.ts` (3) + `useMissionsApi.ts` (1) + `useCronJobMutation.ts` (1) are the natural follow-up to this session's `useModelsPage.ts` migration. **List 4** is the quietest surface (last touched session 124) but most-ripe for new content discovery (the per-list source-pattern tests are CLOSED for all 4 lists now, so the next List 4 pick needs to find new refactor opportunities OUTSIDE the 4 factory families).
2. **`safeApiCall<{ data?: { ... } }>` double-envelope sweep (List 2)** — the 4 List-2 sites in `useMissionsPage.ts:661, 692, 720` and `useMissionsApi.ts:46` and `useCronJobMutation.ts:136` are the same single-nesting pattern this session closed in List 3 (`useModelsPage.ts:413`). The migration is byte-equivalent: `safeApiCall<{ data?: { mission?: { id: string } } }>` → `safeApiCall<{ mission?: { id: string } }>` + `res?.mission?.id` (drop the `res?.data?.` indirection). 5 sites in 3 files, 1-list-scope. Defer to a future List 2 pick.
3. **`apiFetch + JSON.stringify` migration to `safeApiCall` (cross-list)** — 12+ mutation sites in `useModelsPage.ts` and the 4 operations pages. The migration changes the failure mode (throw → return ok/error), so requires per-site `if (!ok) { toastError(...); }` rewrites. Defer to a future session that explicitly opts in to `safeApiCall` mutations.
4. **`useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` is the next candidate. Requires extending the hook to support query params. Defer until the hook shape is stable across logs + sessions.
5. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1298+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
6. **Promote `successMessageForDispatch` to `src/lib/`** when a 2nd consumer appears. Currently 1 consumer (useMissionsPage). List 2 territory.
7. **Status badge label override follow-up** — `StatusBadge.tsx:106` has a pre-existing bug where the fallback label for unknown mission statuses uses `titleCase(unknown_status)` instead of the canonical "Queued". Documented in session 89's "rejected" block. A future session can fix it.
